# tool-skiplist — backlog rows /create-next-tool should NOT build.
#
# Format:  <slug>  # reason
# Blank lines and lines starting with # are ignored. scripts/pick-next-tool.py
# reads this; the build agent appends a line when it spots a semantic near-dup
# mid-run (the exact-slug picker can't catch those — see SKILL.md "Known
# limitation"). Keep reasons short and concrete.
#
# Only list CONFIRMED duplicates of an existing blocks/<slug>/ tool, or rows that
# genuinely cannot be built in gizza's browser-local pure-Rust+ffmpeg model and
# aren't better handled by the picker's out-of-model defer. Do NOT list tools
# that merely share a word with an existing one (age-calculator is NOT a dup of
# calculator; http-request-builder constructs bytes and does NOT send, unlike
# http-request) — those are real, distinct tools.

pdf-to-text            # duplicate of blocks/pdf-extract-text (same: extract text from a PDF)
convert-image-format   # duplicate of blocks/image-convert (same: re-encode image to another format)
image-vectorize        # duplicate of blocks/vectorize (same: trace a raster image into SVG)
image-to-webp          # duplicate of blocks/image-convert (already does format=webp with quality)
image-pad-to-square    # duplicate of blocks/image-contain-fit (set width=height for square padding; arbitrary WxH/aspect padding already covered)
jpg-compressor         # duplicate of blocks/image-compress (re-encodes jpg at a quality, keeps dimensions)
audio-gain-adjust      # duplicate of blocks/audio-volume-adjust (precise dB gain via volume=NdB + optional alimiter soft-clip, on by default)
audio-bass-adjust      # duplicate of blocks/audio-eq (bass param is a low-shelf gain around 100 Hz with -20..20 dB boost/cut; same user function plus mid/treble controls)
true-peak-checker      # not viable in current gizza surfaces: needs audio decode + 4x oversampled true-peak analysis returning numeric dBTP/log output; ffmpeg page/chat path returns media files, pure path has no audio decoder
gps-track-stats        # duplicate of blocks/gpx-analyzer (already reports GPX distance, duration, speed/pace, max speed, elevation gain/loss; GeoJSON support is a gpx-analyzer/gpx-to-geojson improvement, not a distinct tool)

# Architecture-fit skips: need MORE THAN ONE media input. The page driver
# (site/tool.js) handles a single source="file" upload, and the descriptor
# Input enum + resolve_source/dispatch_ffmpeg model one media input — so a tool
# that combines two uploads doesn't fit without a multi-input architecture change.
add-audio-to-video     # needs two media inputs (video + audio); gizza ffmpeg model is single-input
video-mux-audio        # duplicate/not viable for same reason as add-audio-to-video: adding an audio track to a video requires two media inputs (video + audio), while gizza ffmpeg page/dispatch currently accepts one source
still-image-audio-video  # needs two media inputs (cover image + audio track); single-source ffmpeg dispatch + single-upload page + no ffmpeg in chat SW -> no viable surface (same skip as add-audio-to-video)

# Two-opposite-IO-shapes: a single descriptor has ONE Input and one result shape.
# A bidirectional file<->text converter is really two tools (encode: Input::File
# -> text; decode: text param -> file envelope) — Input::File forces a required
# url/ref oneOf, which the decode direction has no use for.
base64-file-converter  # bidirectional file<->base64 = two IO shapes; split into base64-encode-file / base64-decode-file
data-uri-codec  # duplicate of blocks/data-uri-encode + blocks/data-uri-decode (same bidirectional encode/decode of pasted text/base64 bytes to/from data: URIs)
credit-card-validate  # duplicate of blocks/luhn-validate (offline Luhn validation plus best-effort card brand/network by prefix and length: Visa, Mastercard, Amex, Discover, JCB, Diners)
csv-to-json            # duplicate of blocks/csv-json-convert (already parses CSV->JSON with header/delimiter options + type inference)
cartesian-product-generator  # duplicate of blocks/cartesian-product (already generates full Cartesian products from 2-4 lists as lines, CSV, JSON, or count)
image-watermark        # text mode duplicates blocks/add-text-to-image (which covers caption/watermark text overlay); opacity+position are add-text-to-image improvements; the logo-overlay mode needs 2 media inputs (out of model)
jpg-to-png             # duplicate of blocks/image-convert (already does format=png)
readable-article       # duplicate of blocks/readability-extractor (strip nav/ads/clutter from article HTML)
subtitle-translator    # core function is translation = needs an ML model (transformers-js); out of gizza pure+ffmpeg model. (A pure srt<->vtt convert/retime tool would be fine.)
video-convert          # duplicate of blocks/video-transcode (converts video format/container; mov/mkv targets would be a video-transcode improvement)
video-merge            # needs >1 media input (concat several clips); block-utils dispatch_ffmpeg is single-input + page driver takes one file
photo-slideshow-video  # needs multiple image inputs plus optional audio track; gizza ffmpeg/page model supports one media upload and single-source dispatch only
ken-burns-slideshow-video  # multi-image ffmpeg->video (zoompan pan/zoom + xfade crossfades over a SET of images): page file-input is single-upload only and ffmpeg cannot run in the chat SW -> CLI-only; not pure-Rust (needs ffmpeg) so the multi-IMAGE chat+CLI escape hatch doesn't apply. Same blocker as photo-slideshow-video / video-concat. Deferred.

# audio-only family — Input::Audio + AssetKind::Audio landed 2026-07-02, so plain-ffmpeg
# single-audio-input tools are buildable now (page accept="audio/*", format="audio").
# Entries below remain ONLY where an ML model / multi-input surface is required.
video-to-frames        # duplicate of blocks/video-frame-extract (extract still frames from a video)
burn-subtitles         # needs 2 media inputs (video + .srt); single-input ffmpeg dispatch + page take one file
csv-to-tsv  # dup of csv-change-delimiter (comma->tab is the default tab target)
decrypt-file  # dup of encrypt-file (mode=decrypt already implemented)
file-encryptor  # dup of encrypt-file (password AES-GCM encrypt+decrypt already implemented)
password-file-encrypt  # dup of encrypt-file (password-encrypt any file into a portable self-describing blob; XChaCha20-Poly1305/Argon2id vs AES-256-GCM/PBKDF2 is an internal-cipher choice, same user function)
password-file-decrypt  # duplicate of blocks/encrypt-file (mode=decrypt already decrypts files produced by the password encryptor with the passphrase)
javascript-runner  # needs a real JS engine (boa); boa compiles to wasm32-wasip1 but imports wasi poll_oneoff which the gizza wafer runtime does not provide -> fails to instantiate. No pure-Rust JS engine fits the minimal WASI host.
notebook-runner  # not viable in current pure-Rust/wafer model: executing uploaded .ipynb Python code cells requires a Python interpreter/pyodide runtime plus stdout/display capture and sandbox resource controls. Same arbitrary-code-execution class as javascript-runner/ruby-runner/lua-runner, and gizza blocks cannot safely execute Python notebooks offline without a full interpreter.
sqlite-query  # needs a full SQLite engine over uploaded .db files; available Rust bindings depend on libsqlite3 C/wasm toolchains (rusqlite bundled spike failed for wasm32-wasip1 here), and no proven pure-Rust SQLite query engine fits wafer + wasm-pack + CLI surfaces
json-csv-converter  # full dup of csv-json-convert (already bidirectional: direction=auto|csv-to-json|json-to-csv, headers/delimiter/pretty/flatten)
json-to-csv  # dup of csv-json-convert direction=json-to-csv (incl. flatten for nested keys)
jsonl-convert  # duplicate of blocks/data-format-converter (already converts JSON arrays/objects ↔ NDJSON/JSONL, with csv/tsv interop)
json-transform-pipeline  # duplicate of blocks/jq-query (jq/jaq already supports map, select/filter, pluck/projection, group_by, sort_by, unique and flatten over pasted JSON arrays)
mix-audio  # needs 2 DISTINCT media inputs (two audio tracks) + audio INPUT; single-input ffmpeg dispatch + no AssetKind::Audio/accept=audio page input
profanity-filter  # duplicate of blocks/censor-text (built-in common-profanity wordlist, mask + flag, case-insensitive whole-word)
text-encryptor  # duplicate of blocks/text-encrypt (password-encrypt text to a shareable blob + decrypt, AES-256-GCM)
totp-tool  # near-duplicate of blocks/totp-generator (TOTP generation; overlapping scope)
tsv-to-json  # duplicate of blocks/csv-json-convert (delimiter=tab, direction=csv-to-json yields array of objects keyed by header)
unzip-archive  # duplicate of blocks/unzip (lists + extracts individual files from a ZIP, with inline content)
url-analyzer  # duplicate of blocks/url-safety-inspect + blocks/parse-uri (already breaks down URL components, decodes percent/query pieces, and flags punycode/obfuscation/phishing-risk heuristics)
tiktok-video-spec-checker  # needs ffprobe-style video stream metadata (aspect ratio/resolution/duration/codec) and text diagnostics; current gizza ffmpeg surface transforms one media input to one media output and does not expose ffprobe/stdout metadata to CLI/page/chat
video-concat  # multi-input ffmpeg: page file-input is single-upload only and ffmpeg cannot run in the chat SW -> CLI-only; multi-source ffmpeg wiring unproven + arbitrary-clip concat needs resolution/audio normalization. Deferred.
youtube-upload-spec-checker  # needs ffprobe-style video/audio stream metadata (codec, resolution, frame rate, color, bitrate) and text diagnostics; current gizza ffmpeg surface transforms one media input to one media output and does not expose ffprobe/stdout metadata to CLI/page/chat
video-luma-histogram-report  # needs ffprobe/signalstats-style numeric aggregation/reporting across video frames; current gizza ffmpeg surface transforms one media input to one media output and does not expose stdout/metadata to CLI/page/chat
chat-language-detector  # needs statistical/ML natural-language identification for reliable chat routing; heuristic keyword detection would be misleading in current pure-Rust+ffmpeg tool model
video-speed  # duplicate of blocks/change-speed (speed up/slow down a video by a factor, audio kept in sync)
code-line-counter  # duplicate of blocks/code-metrics-analyzer (already counts code/comment/blank lines by language and reports complexity/COCOMO-style estimates)
video-frame-grab  # duplicate of blocks/video-frame-extract (grab a single still frame at a chosen timestamp; PNG-vs-JPEG format is a minor improvement to that tool, not a distinct one)

generate-totp  # near-duplicate of blocks/totp-generator (TOTP from base32 secret; digits/period/algorithm/timestamp all already covered)
html-strip  # near-duplicate of blocks/html-to-text (strips HTML tags → clean plain text with entities decoded; same scope)
image-to-base64  # near-duplicate of blocks/file-to-data-uri (encode file→data: URI, covers images) + extract-decode-base64 (decode); no distinct surface
image-metadata-dumper  # duplicate of blocks/image-metadata-viewer (already reads image metadata as structured fields + GPS; XMP/IPTC breadth is an improvement to that block, not a new tool)
exif-batch-export  # duplicate/not viable as a distinct gizza tool. Verified 2026-07-27: the single-image extraction engine is already blocks/image-metadata-viewer, which reads EXIF/TIFF metadata, camera/lens/timestamps, decoded GPS, and structured fields; related privacy/reporting is covered by metadata-privacy-linter and removal by strip-exif. The picked row's only new dimension is "many images at once into one CSV/JSON table", which requires a batch/multi-image input surface. Current gizza page/file input supports one uploaded media file, and multi-image/source_list tools have no page surface for the mandatory query-param + Playwright verification gate (same class as qr-backup-restore/image-sequence-to-video). A dedicated block would either duplicate image-metadata-viewer for one image or be an unverifiable multi-input wrapper.
photo-geotag-extractor  # duplicate of blocks/image-metadata-viewer (already decodes EXIF GPS latitude/longitude and lists GPS fields; altitude/bearing/DMS are improvements to that block)
javascript-beautifier  # near-duplicate of blocks/js-beautify (formats JavaScript with indentation)
json-formatter  # near-duplicate of blocks/json-beautify (validate + pretty-print + minify(indent=0) JSON with error reporting; same scope)
json-format  # duplicate of blocks/json-beautify + blocks/json-repair: json-beautify already validates, pretty-prints, and minifies JSON with configurable indent while preserving key order; json-repair already tolerantly fixes trailing commas and duplicate keys keep the last value. A separate "pretty/minify/sort keys with optional duplicate-key and trailing-comma repair" tool would only combine existing formatter/repair surfaces; key sorting or duplicate-key warnings belong as small options on those blocks, not as a new near-duplicate block.
json-minify  # near-duplicate of blocks/json-beautify (indent=0 minifies JSON to smallest single line; same scope)
json-prettify  # duplicate of blocks/json-beautify (pretty-prints/minifies JSON with configurable indentation, validation, and preserved key order/spacing; same scope)
json-stringify  # near-duplicate of blocks/json-escape (escape mode + quotes=true wraps text into a JSON string literal; same scope)
ofx-to-csv  # duplicate of blocks/qif-ofx-convert (format=ofx/auto parses OFX/QFX bank statements into normalized CSV transactions)
parse-qr-code  # duplicate of blocks/qr-decode
otpauth-qr-decoder  # duplicate/composition of existing QR + otpauth tools. Verified 2026-07-27: blocks/qr-decode already reads a QR-code image (Input::Image url/ref, image+quircs/rqrr pipeline) and returns the decoded text payload(s) in order; if the QR contains an `otpauth://` provisioning URI, that is exactly the requested extraction. If the QR contains `otpauth-migration://offline?data=...`, blocks/qr-decode extracts that URI and blocks/otpauth-migration-decoder already expands it into standard otpauth:// URI(s) or JSON. blocks/otpauth-uri covers constructing standard provisioning URIs, and totp-generator/totp-secret-setup cover downstream OTP setup/generation surfaces. A dedicated otpauth-qr-decoder would add no QR decoder, no otpauth parser beyond chaining existing blocks, and no new verifiable surface; it is a narrower wrapper around qr-decode plus the existing migration decoder.
statistics-calculator  # duplicate of blocks/descriptive-stats (mean/median/mode/std-dev/quartiles from a list of numbers; regression on a 1-D list is undefined)
toml-to-json  # duplicate of blocks/json-yaml-convert (already converts TOML->JSON, plus YAML, any direction)
word-frequency-counter  # duplicate of blocks/word-frequency (count + rank word occurrences, with stop-word filtering)
xml-validator  # XSD validation needs a wasm-INSTANTIABLE XSD validator; xsd-schema (only viable crate) compiles but its sync schema resolver links std::fs (path_open/fd_close) which the wafer runtime does not provide -> fails wafer instantiation (same class as rqrr). Without XSD it is just well-formedness, already covered by blocks/xml-formatter (well-formedness check + byte-position errors). No in-model surface.
yaml-to-json  # duplicate of blocks/json-yaml-convert (from=yaml to=json, preserves types + nested structures; also json-yaml-converter direction=yaml-to-json)
yaml-to-toml  # duplicate of blocks/json-yaml-convert (from=yaml to=toml, any direction; same as the toml-to-json/yaml-to-json skips — round-trips YAML->Value->TOML, requires a top-level table)
yara-scan  # no wasm-INSTANTIABLE pure-Rust YARA engine. boreal (only candidate) compiles for wasm32-wasip1 but its compiler unconditionally links std::fs (YARA `include` directive resolver: add_rules_str -> read_to_string/canonicalize, a live runtime branch DCE/LTO cannot remove, no feature flag to gate it) -> imports WASI path_open/fd_close/fd_read the wafer runtime does not provide -> fails wafer instantiation (same class as rqrr). yara-x needs wasmtime+cranelift (cannot JIT-in-wasm); yara/yara-sys need the libyara C lib (no wasm). No in-model engine -> no chat surface.
code-to-image  # duplicate of blocks/code-screenshot (syntax-highlighted code snippet -> shareable PNG; same code/language/theme params)
css-selector-extract  # duplicate of blocks/css-select-extract (fetch a page and extract text/inner-HTML/named-attribute from every element matching a CSS selector; same scope)
extract-exif           # duplicate of blocks/image-metadata-viewer (reads EXIF/TIFF metadata from a photo: camera/lens/exposure/timestamps + decoded GPS; same scope)
html-beautify  # duplicate of blocks/html-formatter (indents/pretty-prints HTML, normalizes nesting + attribute spacing; same scope)
math-expression-evaluator  # duplicate of blocks/calculator (meval-based: evaluates arithmetic expressions with functions e.g. sqrt, parentheses and constants pi/e; same scope)
md5-hash  # duplicate: text MD5 covered by blocks/hash-text (algorithm="md5", with input/output encodings) and file MD5 covered by blocks/file-hash (returns md5 digest of any file). No distinct surface.
percent-encode-decode  # duplicate of blocks/url-encode (percent-encode/decode text per RFC 3986 with selectable reserved-char sets: component/uri/form covers query/path/component; plus per_line + repeat). Same scope.
url-codec  # duplicate of blocks/url-encode (percent-encode/decode URLs and query-string components; target=component/uri/form covers form '+' handling, per-line batches, and repeated decode/encode). Same scope.
readability-scorer  # duplicate of blocks/readability-score (computes Flesch Reading Ease + Flesch-Kincaid Grade + Gunning-Fog + SMOG + Coleman-Liau + ARI with the underlying word/sentence/syllable/complex-word counts; same scope). "Flag long/complex sentences" is a minor improvement to readability-score, not a distinct tool.
regex-extractor  # duplicate of blocks/regex-extract (applies a regex (Rust syntax) to text and returns all matches, with ignore_case/multiline/dotall flags, capture_group selection, and dedup; same scope). Returning all capture groups per match is a minor improvement to regex-extract, not a distinct tool.
string-unescaper        # duplicate of blocks/string-escaper (already has mode=unescape for json/javascript/html/url; backslash escapes overlap js-unescape)
subtitle-timing-shifter  # duplicate of blocks/srt-shift (already shifts SRT timestamps by a fixed offset); the "stretch/rescale" half is a linear-scale improvement to srt-shift, not a distinct tool
unique-lines  # duplicate of blocks/remove-duplicate-lines (removes duplicate lines preserving order, keep first/last, adjacent_only/uniq, ignore_case, trim); the "count each" half is blocks/count-line-frequency. No distinct capability.
untar  # duplicate of blocks/extract-tar (lists + extracts files from a tar/tar.gz/tgz archive, gzip auto-detected, repacked as ZIP). Same scope.
url-parser  # duplicate of blocks/parse-uri (splits a URL/URI into scheme/host/port/path/query-params/fragment per RFC 3986). Same scope.
view-exif  # duplicate of blocks/image-metadata-viewer (reads + displays all EXIF/TIFF metadata fields from an image: camera settings, timestamps, decoded GPS; same scope as extract-exif skip)
xml-escape-unescape  # duplicate of blocks/string-escaper (target=html/xml escapes & < > " ' and unescapes named + numeric (&#NN;) entities, mode=escape|unescape; same scope)

video-trim-segment  # duplicate of blocks/video-trim (keeps a single [start, start+duration] start-to-end range via keyframe-accurate lossless input-seek stream-copy, cutting away everything else; end = start+duration and the stream-copy IS the keyframe-accurate lossless mode)
image-optimize  # duplicate of blocks/image-convert (format=webp + quality 1-100 already re-encodes/shrinks to WebP). The only delta is AVIF, which is INFEASIBLE on the required page surface: browser @ffmpeg/core@0.12.10 is built without libaom/librav1e/libsvtav1 (verified in the embedded --enable-* config), so it has an AV1 decoder + AVIF muxer but NO AV1 encoder. WebP-only optimize == image-convert.

video-speech-transcript-extractor  # out-of-model: speech-to-text needs an ASR model (backlog feasibility names transformers-js + Xenova/whisper-base, ~74MB weights). gizza is pure-Rust + ffmpeg; ffmpeg.wasm has NO speech-recognition (it only extracts/resamples the audio pre-step), and Whisper weights blow the wasm size budget + can't instantiate under `wafer build` / can't be headlessly page-verified. Picker mis-tags it ffmpeg because "ffmpeg.wasm" in the feasibility text makes IN_MODEL win over MODEL, defeating the out-of-model deferral. Same class as any whisper/transformers-js/onnx row.
image-resizer  # duplicate of blocks/image-resize (resizes with width/height + aspect-ratio-lock fit modes contain/cover/stretch via ffmpeg scale/force_original_aspect_ratio; plan_resize validates dims). The extra verbs in the row description are each already their own block: crop=blocks/image-crop, rotate=blocks/rotate-image, flip=blocks/flip-image. "batch support" is a chat/CLI invocation pattern, not a distinct tool surface. No new capability.

No distinct surface.)

pdf-extract-pages  # duplicate of blocks/pdf-split (its pure split(pdf, pages_spec) keeps the pages named by a 1-based comma+range spec e.g. "1,3-5,8" (plus all/odd/even), deletes the rest, prunes+renumbers, and re-serializes into a new PDF -- exactly "pulls a chosen subset of pages into a new PDF". pdf-split summary is literally "Extract or split specific pages out of a PDF"; skill desc "Extract specific pages from a PDF into a new PDF". Same scope.)
audio-trimmer  # duplicate of blocks/trim-audio (cuts an audio file to a chosen [start,end] range via mode=keep atrim, OR removes the selection via mode=remove aselect, with optional fade in/out — trim-audio has exactly the keep/remove modes + fade_in/fade_out params. Same scope.)
video-trim-clip  # duplicate of blocks/video-trim (extracts a chosen start-end time range from a video via keyframe-accurate lossless input-seek stream-copy -ss/-t -c copy, keeping the source container; end = start+duration and the stream-copy IS the "when codecs allow, lossless and fast" mode. Same scope as the already-skiplisted video-trim-segment.)
video-resize-resolution  # duplicate of blocks/video-resize (its plan(in, w, h) already scales to an exact width×height and preserves aspect on any omitted axis via ffmpeg scale=W:-2 / scale=-2:H, re-encoding H.264/CRF23; summary is literally "Scale a video to a target resolution". The only delta is named standard presets 1080p/720p, which are just height=1080/720 with aspect-preserve on width — a preset-chip UX shortcut over the identical core function, not a distinct capability.)
mp4-to-mp3  # duplicate of blocks/extract-audio-from-video (its plan_extract(format, bitrate, in) already pulls the audio track out of ANY video container — MP4 included — via ffmpeg -i in -vn -c:a libmp3lame -b:a <kbps>k out.mp3, with a 32-320 kbps bitrate param and a default of 192; MP4->MP3 is the exact default path. WAV is an extra lossless option. "MP4" is just a source container, not a distinct capability. Same scope.)
video-to-wav  # duplicate of blocks/extract-audio-from-video (its plan_extract(format="wav", bitrate, in) already extracts a video's audio track as an uncompressed lossless WAV via ffmpeg -i in -vn -c:a pcm_s16le out.wav — exactly "extracts a video's audio as an uncompressed WAV for transcription, mastering, or further editing". WAV is one of that block's two format enum values (mp3|wav); pinning format to wav is not a distinct capability. Same class as the mp4-to-mp3 skip of the same block.)
resize-image  # duplicate of blocks/image-resize (already resizes to exact target width/height with aspect-lock fit modes contain/cover/stretch via ffmpeg scale + force_original_aspect_ratio; plan_resize validates dims). The two deltas in the row are param variations on the identical resize op, not a distinct tool: "scale factor" = scale=iw*f:ih*f (target dims expressed as a percentage of source), and "selectable resampling quality" = the ffmpeg scale flags=lanczos/bicubic/bilinear/neighbor knob. Both belong as params to image-resize. Same class as the already-skiplisted image-resizer.
audio-format-converter  # duplicate of blocks/audio-convert (its plan_convert(in, format, bitrate) already transcodes audio between mp3/wav/ogg/flac/m4a via ffmpeg -c:a libmp3lame|pcm_s16le|libvorbis|flac|aac with -b:a <kbps>k on lossy targets, 32-320 kbps bitrate param, default 192; -vn drops attached-picture streams. MP3/FLAC/OGG/WAV match directly and AAC == the m4a target (aac codec in m4a container). The only deltas are Opus (one more output enum value) and a VBR-quality knob alongside bitrate — minor format-option additions to the identical "transcode audio between formats at chosen bitrate" surface, i.e. enhancements to audio-convert, not a distinct tool. Same class as the xlsx-sheet-extractor / mp4-to-mp3 skips.)
crop-image  # duplicate of blocks/image-crop (its plan_crop(in, x, y, w, h) already crops to a given rectangle via ffmpeg crop=w:h:x:y and validates w/h > 0, keeping the input extension). The only delta in the row is "or aspect ratio", which is not a distinct crop op: an aspect-ratio crop just computes a centered rectangle from a chosen ratio (e.g. 1:1, 16:9) and feeds the identical crop=w:h:x:y filter -- a preset/param shortcut over the same core function, belonging as params on image-crop. Same class as the video-resize-resolution 1080p/720p preset skip. No distinct capability.
video-downscale-resolution  # duplicate of blocks/video-resize (its plan(in, w, h) already re-encodes a video to a lower target resolution while preserving aspect ratio on the omitted axis via ffmpeg scale=W:-2 / scale=-2:H, H.264/CRF23; "1080p->720p" is just height=720 with aspect-preserve on width. Same as the already-skiplisted video-resize-resolution case — a preset shortcut over the identical core function, not a distinct capability.)
pdf-merge  # duplicate of blocks/merge-pdf (its pure merge(pdfs) concatenates the pages of every input PDF, in the given source order, into one document — descriptor takes a source_list("inputs", min 2) of PDF URLs/refs and its skill desc is literally "Combine multiple PDF files into a single PDF, in the given order". "chosen page order" = the input source order. Same scope.)
image-filter  # duplicate of blocks/photo-filter-presets (the general "apply a named filter/look to an image via a selector" tool: its 9-preset enum already includes grayscale + sepia among vintage/warm/cool/noir/vivid/invert/fade, each a fixed ffmpeg -vf chain). The row is "apply grayscale/sepia/blur/sharpen/brightness/contrast to an image" — a re-bundle of verbs EACH already its own dedicated block: grayscale=blocks/image-grayscale, sepia=blocks/photo-filter-presets(sepia preset), blur=blocks/blur-image (adjustable Gaussian radius), sharpen=blocks/sharpen-image (unsharp mask), brightness/contrast=blocks/image-brightness-contrast. No new capability — adding a strength knob + the blur/sharpen/brightness/contrast options belongs as improvements to photo-filter-presets, not a parallel tool. Same class as the image-resizer/resize-image "bundle-of-existing-verbs" skips.
encrypt-text-passphrase  # duplicate of blocks/text-encrypt (high-level passphrase text encryption: derives a key from the passphrase via PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 and outputs a self-contained authenticated ciphertext string — AES-256-GCM blob magic|salt|nonce|ciphertext+tag, base64-wrapped into one copy-pasteable token, with matching decrypt. Exactly "derive a key from a password and output a self-contained authenticated ciphertext string". Same scope as the already-skiplisted text-encryptor dup of the same block.)
run-sql-query  # duplicate of blocks/sql-playground (executes pasted CREATE TABLE / INSERT / SELECT statements against a fresh in-memory SQL database per call and returns the LAST statement's result rows as table/csv/json — descriptor param `sql` desc is literally "include your CREATE TABLE / INSERT before the SELECT". "in-browser SQLite engine" vs GlueSQL is an internal-engine choice, same user function; same class as the password-file-encrypt/math-expression-evaluator engine-choice skips.)
video-compressor  # duplicate of blocks/video-compress (its build_argv(crf, in) already shrinks a video's file size via a single-pass CRF re-encode to H.264/libx264 + AAC, keeping the container when it can hold those codecs; descriptor exposes a `crf` quality/size knob "Lower = higher quality/larger; higher = smaller. Clamped to 18-34." (default 28), and the block summary is literally "Shrink a video's file size with a single-pass CRF re-encode to H.264/AAC". The picked row's "adjusting CRF/bitrate while keeping watchable quality" is the exact same function — CRF is the primary knob; a true target-bitrate 2-pass encode is the block's own noted follow-up (one build_argv→ffmpegExec call is single-pass), i.e. an enhancement to video-compress, not a distinct tool. Same scope.)

# PDF page rasterization is un-buildable in gizza's wasmi model (not a dup: extract-pdf-images
# pulls out STORED image objects, pdf-extract-text gets the TEXT layer; nothing rasterizes a page).
pdf-to-images  # Rendering a PDF PAGE to a PNG/JPEG needs a full renderer (interpret content stream + fonts + vector fills), not just a parser. (1) pdfium-render / mupdf are C-library bindings -> won't wasm-instantiate under `wafer build`. (2) The one working PERMISSIVE pure-Rust page rasterizer, `pdf-render` 1.0.0-beta.17 (hayro/pdfluent, Apache-2.0 OR MIT, CPU via vello_cpu; VERIFIED rendering a lopdf-built black-rect test PDF to correct pixels on host, 9/9 core tests) pulls `fast_image_resize`, which compiles WASM SIMD (v128, opcode 0xfd) UNCONDITIONALLY for target_arch=wasm32 -- its wasm32 convolution modules are gated ONLY on `#[cfg(target_arch="wasm32")]` and forced on via `#[target_feature(enable="simd128")]`, with NO cargo feature to disable them -- and it's a hard dep of pdf-render (used to scale embedded images). gizza's wasmi runtime (0.44.1) has the `simd` cargo feature OFF platform-wide (Engine::default() disables WasmFeatures::SIMD; every one of the ~430 blocks is non-SIMD wasm), so `wafer build` validation rejects the module: "unexpected SIMD opcode: 0xfd (at offset ...)". Turning on wasmi SIMD is a cross-cutting RUNTIME change (validator + gizza CLI + browser chat Service Worker + Cloudflare executor all build wasmi with simd off), not a per-tool build. (3) The SIMD-free alternative `fop-pdf-renderer` 0.1.2 (tiny-skia, same rasterizer as the shipped svg-to-png) reads SYSTEM fonts via std::fs (`std::fs::read_dir`/`std::fs::read` in glyph.rs + rasterizer.rs:151) in the live text-render path to resolve Standard-14 substitutes -> links path_open/fd_read/fd_close WASI imports the wafer runtime does not provide (won't instantiate; same class as rqrr/yara/xsd) AND embeds no fonts, so text couldn't render headlessly regardless. Revisit if wasmi's `simd` feature is enabled platform-wide, or a permissive pure-Rust, font-EMBEDDING, tiny-skia-based PDF rasterizer matures.

# HEIC/HEIF decode is genuinely un-buildable in gizza's model (not a dup, not an ML-model row).
heic-to-jpg  # HEIC decode has no viable surface. (1) ffmpeg has NO HEIF still-image demuxer: verified on a real nokiatech sample.heic that even system ffmpeg 6.1 (which HAS the native `hevc` decoder) fails with "moov atom not found" — HEIF uses meta/iloc/iinf boxes, not moov; the page's @ffmpeg/core 0.12.10 is a smaller build and also lacks it. (2) Pure-Rust: the ONE crate that actually decodes real HEIC (`heic` 0.1.6, verified decoding the sample to correct 1440x960 RGB) is AGPL-3.0-only OR commercial — incompatible with MIT-licensed gizza-ai served as a hosted site. MIT/permissive alternatives are empty stubs (gamut-heic 0.2.0 = "implementation pending" placeholder; heif 0.1.0 = a `cargo new` `add(a,b)` template; oxideav-h265 0.0.0 = scaffold). (3) libheif-rs / heif-rs / libheif-sys are bindings to the C libheif+libde265 — need the native lib and won't wasm-instantiate under `wafer build` / wasm-pack. Revisit if a permissive-licensed pure-Rust HEVC-intra decoder matures.
image-qr-scanner  # duplicate of blocks/qr-decode (its pure run(image_bytes) already detects and decodes every QR code found anywhere in an image and returns each payload in detection order, using the exact wasm-safe stack -- the `image` crate to rasterize + `quircs` to identify/decode -- and handles multi-code images; description is literally "read and decode the data in a QR code image"). image-qr-scanner is the same "scan an image -> get QR payloads" tool; the only delta in the row is also returning code "positions" (quircs already exposes corner coords), a marginal add-on, not a distinct capability. Same class as the already-skiplisted parse-qr-code dup of the same block.
pdf-table-extractor  # duplicate of blocks/pdf-table-extract (detects tabular layouts in a text-based PDF via positional clustering — rows by baseline y, columns by left-edge x — and extracts them as CSV/TSV/JSON via to_csv; same scope and same method)
pdf-reorder-pages  # duplicate of blocks/pdf-organize (its organize() takes an `order` param parsed by parse_order() as an ordered 1-based page sequence, e.g. "3,1,2", which reorders the pages into that new sequence — exactly this row's "Reorders the pages of a PDF into a new sequence." pdf-organize goes further (a page may repeat=duplicate or be omitted=delete, plus an additive multiple-of-90 rotate on selected originals) and also supports "all"/"reverse" keywords and ascending/descending ranges. Pure reorder-into-new-sequence is a strict subset of pdf-organize's `order` param; no distinct capability. Same class as the pdf-delete-pages/pdf-rotate family already covered by pdf-organize.)
text-redactor  # duplicate of blocks/redact-pii (its pure redact(text, style) already detects and masks emails, phone numbers, credit-card numbers (Luhn-validated), IPv4/IPv6 addresses and US SSNs, then replaces each match with a configurable placeholder — style=label emits typed tokens ([EMAIL]/[PHONE]/[CREDIT_CARD]/[IP]/[SSN]) and style=mask overwrites each char with '*'; matches are de-overlapped earliest-start/longest-on-ties and replaced left-to-right, returning per-category counts. The picked row is "mask emails, phone numbers, credit-card-like numbers, and IPs in text with configurable redaction placeholders" — a strict SUBSET of redact-pii (which also covers SSN and IPv6). "Configurable redaction placeholders" == the existing style=label/mask knob. No distinct capability. Same class as the redact/mask/pii family already covered by redact-pii.)
sql-query  # duplicate of blocks/csv-query (its descriptor already takes `data` = a pasted CSV/table whose first row is a header, `query` = a SQL query "SELECT <cols|*> [WHERE <col op value>] [ORDER BY <col> [ASC|DESC]] [LIMIT n]", and `delimiter`, then runs that SQL directly over the pasted table — no manual CREATE TABLE/INSERT — and returns the result rows. That is exactly this row's "Run a SQL query over a CSV or pasted table and return results." csv-query already owns the search intent too: its page tags are "csv query","sql csv","query csv","select where csv". Full arbitrary SQL over pasted data (JOINs/subqueries/DDL) is separately served by blocks/sql-playground — the same GlueSQL engine the prior `run-sql-query` skip already established exists — and GROUP BY/aggregates by blocks/csv-group-by + blocks/csv-pivot. The only delta a "sql-query" could add over csv-query is a bigger SQL dialect over the same CSV-auto-load, i.e. an internal-engine/dialect-degree choice, not a distinct user function — same class as the run-sql-query engine-choice skip. No distinct capability.)

# Speech-to-text captioning is out-of-model: gizza's wasmi runtime has no ML model loader.
video-to-captions  # out-of-model: the core deliverable is caption TEXT, which needs Whisper-class ASR (speech-to-text). ffmpeg (the type_hint) can extract/convert the audio track and detect silence segments, but CANNOT transcribe speech into words; there is no ML model loader in gizza's wasmi runtime (same loader-blocked class as transcribe/OCR/summarize/translate). Shipping only an audio-extractor mislabeled as a captioner would not produce the timestamped caption file. Revisit only if an in-model ASR backend exists.
video-hardsub-extractor  # out-of-model: burned-in subtitle extraction requires OCR over video frames plus timing reconstruction; ffmpeg can crop/sample frames but cannot recognize text, and gizza has no OCR/ML model loader in the wasm/chat runtime.
video-background-blur  # out-of-model: requires person/foreground segmentation or matting (ML/CV model) to isolate the subject before blurring the background; ffmpeg alone can only apply global/static-mask blur and would not deliver the promised portrait/privacy background blur.
video-background-remove  # out-of-model: requires AI/person/object matting or segmentation to separate foreground from background; ffmpeg alone cannot infer a transparent WebM alpha matte or believable replacement background from arbitrary video.
heic-to-png  # out-of-model: same as heic-to-jpg (ea24995e) — no wasm/MIT HEIC/HEIF decoder (no ffmpeg HEIF demuxer; only pure-Rust decoder is AGPL); PNG output was never the blocker
invoice-data-extractor  # out-of-model: scanned/photographed invoice needs OCR (image→text); gizza wasmi has no OCR engine — field parsing is in-model but the image-OCR front half is not

voice-note-cleaner  # out-of-model: requires Whisper-class ASR plus summarization/cleanup model; ffmpeg/Rust can decode audio but cannot transcribe speech or summarize notes in current gizza pure+ffmpeg model
video-onscreen-text-ocr  # out-of-model: extracting on-screen text from sampled video frames requires OCR (image/frame -> text). ffmpeg can sample frames, but cannot recognize text, and gizza's current pure-Rust+ffmpeg/wasmi model has no OCR engine or ML model loader. Same class as invoice-data-extractor / video-to-captions ASR skips.
video-onscreen-text-detector  # out-of-model: reliably detecting burned-in titles/captions/watermarks in arbitrary video frames is OCR/text-region detection or computer-vision segmentation. ffmpeg can sample frames and measure generic edges/scene changes, but cannot distinguish text/graphics overlays from ordinary high-contrast scene detail or locate captions/watermarks with useful timestamps. Same current-model gap as video-onscreen-text-ocr.
svg-rasterizer  # duplicate of blocks/svg-to-png (resvg+tiny-skia rasterizes an SVG document to PNG/JPEG at any resolution, handling gradients/fonts/filters)
zip-extract  # duplicate of blocks/unzip (its core::extract lists every file in a .zip with path+size AND returns each file's content inline — text when UTF-8, else base64 — i.e. exactly "list and extract the contents of a .zip archive"; zip-inspect covers list-only). The sole delta, "optional password", is an enhancement to unzip, not a distinct tool, and encrypted-zip decryption is out of the current pure-Rust deflate-only wasm model: the zip crate is built default-features=false, features=["deflate"] specifically to stay wasm32-safe (no aes-crypto/legacy ZipCrypto decrypt wired, and AES pulls crypto deps). Same class as the already-skiplisted unzip-archive.
video-mux-external-audio  # multi-input ffmpeg: needs 2 DISTINCT media inputs (a video file + a SEPARATE audio file) muxed into one output. block-utils dispatch_ffmpeg is single-input and the page file driver takes one upload, and ffmpeg cannot run in the chat SW -> no viable surface (CLI-only is insufficient per loop rules). Same class as the already-skiplisted video-concat / video-merge / mix-audio / burn-subtitles multi-input entries. Deferred.
video-replace-audio  # multi-input ffmpeg: needs 2 DISTINCT media inputs (video + replacement audio) and the current gizza ffmpeg/page dispatch surface accepts only one uploaded media input; CLI-only would not satisfy loop verification. Same class as video-mux-external-audio.
extractive-summary  # duplicate of blocks/textrank-summarize (its core is exactly "extractive text summary via the TextRank algorithm": splits text into sentences, builds a word-overlap similarity graph, runs PageRank, and returns the top-ranked verbatim sentences in original order — i.e. "pick the most important verbatim sentences, no rewriting". The "highlight" delta is a page-rendering presentation of the same selected sentences, not a distinct compute tool.)

eml-msg-parser  # duplicate of blocks/eml-parse for its headline .eml scope: eml-parse already parses raw RFC 5322 / MIME into subject/date/message-id, from/to/cc (name+address), decoded plain-text AND HTML bodies, and a decoded attachment list (filename, content-type, size), with MIME multipart + encoded-header/body decoding automatic -- i.e. exactly this row's "extract headers, bodies, and decoded attachments" for .eml. The only differentiator, Outlook .msg, is a separate Microsoft Compound File Binary (CFBF/OLE2 structured-storage) container needing OLE2 + MAPI property-stream decoding -- binary/compound-file complexity outside eml-parse's text-message (eml: string) model -- and, if built, belongs in its own dedicated msg-parse block, not bundled into a tool whose .eml half is a strict dup. As specified (one tool doing both), it is a semantic duplicate of eml-parse, not a distinct single-model tool.
log-pii-redactor  # duplicate of blocks/redact-pii (email/IP/card/secret masking in text; bearer-token variant marginal, also covered by pii-tokenize)
png-to-avif  # out-of-model: AVIF encoding is unavailable in the browser runtime. The page/chat surface runs @ffmpeg/core@0.12.10 (stock single-thread build, js/ffmpeg.js) which ships libx264/x265/vpx/webp/mp3lame but NO libaom-av1 / no AVIF encoder, so `-c:v av1`/`.avif` output fails in-browser. This is why the two existing ffmpeg image-transcode tools deliberately exclude avif: image-convert supports only jpeg|png|webp and image-shrink-for-sharing explicitly rejects "avif". System-ffmpeg (CLI) has libaom-av1, but CLI-only is insufficient per loop rules (page+chat must work). Same class as other out-of-model ffmpeg skips.
text-secret-encrypt  # duplicate of blocks/text-encrypt (encrypt a short message/secret with a password into one compact, copy-pasteable ciphertext token, with matching decrypt — AES-256-GCM, key derived via PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256, self-contained blob magic|salt|nonce|ciphertext+tag, base64-wrapped. Exactly this row's "encrypt a short message or secret with a password into a copy-pasteable ciphertext block". The sole delta, "armored" output, is a cosmetic PEM-style BEGIN/END wrapper around the same base64 ciphertext — a rendering choice, not a distinct capability. Same class as the already-skiplisted text-encryptor and encrypt-text-passphrase dups of the same block.)
image-region-redactor  # duplicate of blocks/image-pixelate-censor (its core::censor already obscures a user-specified rectangular region (x, y, width, height in pixels, 0-based top-left, clamped to the image) of an image while leaving the rest untouched, with mode = pixelate (mosaic) OR blur and a strength knob — i.e. exactly this row's "blur or black out a rectangular region (license plate, address, name)". Blur is already a first-class mode; the same privacy use cases (face/plate/sensitive text) are called out in its skill description. The sole nominal delta, a solid "black out" fill, is a third fill style that belongs as an added mode on image-pixelate-censor (one-line region fill with the image crate), not a distinct tool — the region-selection + PNG-out compute is identical. Same rectangular-region censor model, so it is a semantic duplicate, not a distinct single-model tool.)
audio-split-on-silence  # multi-OUTPUT artifact, unsupported: the deliverable is N separate clips (one per non-silent region between detected silence boundaries), which ffmpeg produces via the segment muxer writing out%03d.wav. But the gizza ffmpeg dispatch model is single-output — ExecArgs.output is ONE filename String and ExecResult.output is ONE Vec<u8> (block-utils/src/ffmpeg.rs:107,113): the harness reads back exactly one named file, and the page/chat surface renders a single audio envelope (page format="audio" -> one <audio>). There is no zip/multi-file output surface for ffmpeg tools (only image|video|audio|text single media, per page-patterns.md). silencedetect + single-clip ops are already covered by blocks/audio-silence-remove (removes silent gaps into ONE output) and blocks/video-silence-cut; the distinct "emit each clip as its own file" intent is precisely the unsupported multi-output case. Same architecture-fit class as the multi-INPUT skips (video-concat/mix-audio), but on the output side. Revisit only if a multi-file/zip output surface lands.
audiobook-chapter-splitter  # multi-OUTPUT artifact, unsupported: the promised result is N per-chapter audio files from one audiobook (embedded chapters or timestamps), but gizza's ffmpeg dispatch/page/chat model returns exactly ONE media output file and has no multi-file/zip result surface. Building only one chapter or a shell script would not satisfy the tool. Same architecture-fit class as audio-split-on-silence.
ecc-keygen  # duplicate re-bundle: 2 of its 3 curves are already dedicated tools — Ed25519 = blocks/ed25519-key-pair-generator (generates an Ed25519 key pair, PEM/raw encodings), P-256 = blocks/generate-ecdsa-key-pair (generates ECDSA key pairs on P-256/P-384/P-521 with pkcs8 PEM + optional RFC 7517 JWK). The only novel curve is secp256k1 (Koblitz curve, k256 crate — no existing block covers it). As specified (one tool doing all three), it is a semantic duplicate of those two blocks, same class as the eml-msg-parser "one tool doing both, .eml half is a strict dup" and the image-filter "re-bundle of verbs each already its own block" skips. A distinct secp256k1 keygen belongs as an added curve on generate-ecdsa-key-pair (or its own k256 block), not a bundle whose two-thirds duplicates existing tools.

pdf-sign  # duplicate / out-of-model split: typed-signature stamping is already covered by blocks/pdf-page-numbers (it stamps arbitrary text templates, not only numbers, onto selected PDF pages with position, font, size, color, margin and opacity; using format="Jane Doe" and pages="3" is exactly a typed visual signature). The remaining differentiator, uploading a separate signature image and overlaying it on a PDF, requires two independent binary inputs (PDF + image) / an interactive placement workflow, which is not supported by the current gizza single-source chat/CLI/page model. A solid implementation would be an added mode on pdf-page-numbers or a future multi-input PDF-image stamper, not a distinct current backlog tool.
qr-encode  # duplicate of blocks/qr-code-generator
image-chroma-key  # duplicate of blocks/image-bg-replace: its "transparent" bg_type IS chroma-key-to-transparent-PNG — ffmpeg colorkey filter removes a solid green/blue-screen background (DEFAULT_KEY_COLOR=#00ff00) with similarity/blend thresholds and outputs a see-through PNG/WebP (core/src/lib.rs BgType::Transparent). Same operation, same defaults; no distinct capability to add.
color-to-alpha  # duplicate of blocks/image-bg-replace: bg_type=transparent with key_color + similarity/blend already makes a chosen solid color transparent within tolerance, producing a clean alpha cutout (PNG/WebP).
regex-match-tester  # duplicate of blocks/regex-tester
mp3-to-wav-decoder  # duplicate of blocks/audio-convert (format=wav): its Format::Wav arm emits an uncompressed PCM WAV via ffmpeg -c:a pcm_s16le (verified core/src/lib.rs), decoding ANY audio input including MP3 to WAV, and with no -ar the original sample rate is preserved by default — exactly "decode an MP3 into an uncompressed PCM WAV at the original sample rate". Same scope; same class as the already-skiplisted audio-format-converter dup of audio-convert.

m4a-to-wav  # duplicate of blocks/audio-convert (format=wav): ffmpeg `-i in.m4a` auto-detects the AAC/MP4 container and the Format::Wav arm emits an uncompressed PCM WAV via `-c:a pcm_s16le` (verified core/src/lib.rs:68), decoding ANY audio input including AAC .m4a to WAV, and with no `-ar` the original sample rate is preserved by default -- exactly "decode an AAC .m4a into uncompressed WAV/PCM at the original sample rate". Same scope; same class as the already-skiplisted mp3-to-wav-decoder / audio-format-converter dups of audio-convert.

mbox-parser  # duplicate of blocks/gmail-takeout-parser: that block is a GENERIC mbox parser, not Gmail-only. Its core split_messages() splits any mbox blob on the classic `From ` postmark lines (and falls back to a single message for a lone .eml), then parses each message with mail-parser and emits one row per message with date/from/to/cc/subject/message-id (plus an always-present X-Gmail-Labels column that is simply empty for non-Gmail archives, and an optional body snippet) as CSV or JSON. The picked row -- "splits an mbox archive into individual messages and lists each one's from/subject/date headers" -- is a strict SUBSET (from/subject/date are already three of gmail-takeout-parser's columns). Same operation (split on `From ` postmark -> per-message header table), same wasm-safe mail-parser engine; no distinct capability to add.

csv-select  # duplicate of blocks/csv-reorder-columns: the tool's own tagline -- "a cut for tables" (column projection: xsv/qsv select, mlr cut) -- IS csv-reorder-columns. Three of csv-select's four verbs are covered verbatim by that block's core::reorder(data, columns, header, delimiter): `columns` is a comma-separated list of names (when header=true) or 1-based indices; columns are output in the listed ORDER (= reorder), columns you OMIT are dropped (= select AND drop), and a repeated column is duplicated -- exactly this row's "Selects, reorders, ... or drops columns from a CSV/TSV". The sole differentiator is RENAME (the backlog example "keep name,email,country and rename country to region"); no existing block renames CSV columns (csv-to-vcard's match is field-mapping to vCard, not header rename). Per the established skiplist pattern (ecc-keygen, image-region-redactor, zip-extract, text-secret-encrypt), a lone novel delta on top of an otherwise-duplicate scope belongs as an ADDED capability on the existing block -- here an optional per-column rename on csv-reorder-columns (e.g. `newname:oldname` in the columns list, xsv/qsv-style) -- not as a distinct tool whose three-quarters strictly duplicates csv-reorder-columns. Scaffold not built.
video-gps-track-extractor  # out-of-model: extracting GPS coordinates requires PARSING the GoPro GPMF telemetry stream (proprietary KLV binary) or dashcam NMEA/Novatek formats into lat/lon track points, then emitting GPX/CSV. ffmpeg (the type_hint) only DEMUXES the embedded metadata stream as opaque `bin_data` (confirmed: -codecs lists bin_data with no GPMF decoder, and no GPX muxer/format exists) — it cannot decode GPMF into coordinates. This needs a dedicated GPMF parser (gopro/gpmf-parser, a C lib) plus per-vendor proprietary format handling; no wasm-safe Rust GPMF parser is in the proven-crates set. gizza's gpx-analyzer/gpx-privacy-scrubber consume already-parsed GPX, not video telemetry. Same loader-blocked/proprietary-parser class as the ffmpeg-can-demux-but-not-decode skips. Revisit only if a wasm-safe GPMF telemetry parser lands.

pii-scanner  # duplicate of blocks/redact-pii: its detection engine already IS this row's scanner — the same five categories (email, phone, IPv4+IPv6, Luhn-validated credit-card, US SSN via the same regex+Luhn+de-overlap pipeline), and redact-pii's redact() already computes exact match spans internally (accepted: Vec<(start, end, Kind)>, core/src/lib.rs) and returns the TYPES (per-category Counts) and COUNTS (+ total) that pii-scanner is asked to report. The only delta is (a) not modifying the text (a strict SUBSET of redact-pii, which is redaction-then-report) and (b) surfacing the LOCATIONS/offsets it already collects but currently discards after masking. Per the established pattern (log-pii-redactor dup of redact-pii; zip-extract/csv-select/ecc-keygen "lone novel delta belongs as an added capability on the existing block"), exposing the already-computed spans is an added detect-only/report mode on redact-pii, not a distinct tool. Same detection scope, same wasm-safe pure-regex engine; no distinct capability to build.

exif-privacy-audit  # duplicate of blocks/metadata-privacy-linter: that block already "scans an image's embedded metadata for privacy-sensitive fields and reports what would leak," classified by category + risk — its EXIF/TIFF (kamadak-exif) scan flags exactly this row's four fields (GPS = location, camera/lens serials = device, owner name = personal, software), plus timestamps and description/comment, and also XMP + IPTC. The picked row — "scans metadata and returns a privacy risk report flagging sensitive fields (GPS, device serial, owner name, software) present" — is a strict subset of metadata-privacy-linter's Report (per-Finding source/field/category/risk + min_risk threshold, reveal_values toggle). Pure Rust, same wasm-safe stack, runs on all backends incl. chat SW; no distinct capability to build.

csv-wrangler  # re-bundle of verbs each already its own block: filter rows = blocks/csv-filter (col op value conditions), trim cells + fill nulls/empty cells = blocks/csv-cleaner (trim + empty_cells=fill/fill_value + drop-empty + dedup), reorder/drop columns = blocks/csv-reorder-columns. The chain-of-steps "pipeline" is a bundling of these existing distinct tools; the only novel deltas are per-column RENAME and column SPLIT, which (per the csv-select skip) belong as added capabilities on csv-reorder-columns / a small dedicated block, not a parallel bundle whose bulk strictly duplicates csv-filter + csv-cleaner + csv-reorder-columns. Same class as the csv-select / image-filter / ecc-keygen re-bundle skips.

xlsx-to-markdown  # duplicate / composition of blocks/xlsx-to-csv + blocks/csv-to-table: xlsx-to-csv already reads an .xlsx/.xlsm/.xls/.ods workbook via calamine (pure Rust, wasm-safe) and emits a sheet (selectable by name or 0-based index) as text, and csv-to-table already renders that CSV as a GitHub Markdown table (Format::Markdown: header row + `---` separator + `|`-escaped cells, core/src/lib.rs). "renders sheets from an .xlsx workbook as Markdown tables" is exactly that two-step pipeline (workbook -> rows -> Markdown table) with no new engine. The sole delta -- rendering ALL sheets in one call as several stacked tables -- is a minor multi-sheet/output-format toggle that, per the established pattern (csv-select, csv-wrangler, ecc-keygen: a lone novel delta belongs as an ADDED capability on the existing block), is a markdown output-format / all-sheets option on xlsx-to-csv, not a distinct backlog tool. Same wasm-safe stack; no distinct capability to build.

bank-csv-summary  # re-bundle of blocks/csv-group-by (+ minor deltas): "break spending down by category, merchant, and month" is three group-by-and-sum views over one CSV, and csv-group-by already does exactly that — group_by a column (category or merchant) with aggregations=amount:sum plus a per-group count, one row per group. Given a bank export that carries a category and a description/merchant column, "by category" and "by merchant" are csv-group-by verbatim; "by month" is grouping on a date column bucketed to YYYY-MM, a minor derived-key delta that (per the csv-select / csv-wrangler / xlsx-to-markdown pattern) belongs as an added date-truncation capability on csv-group-by, not a distinct tool. The only genuinely distinct capability — INFERRING a spending category and NORMALIZING a merchant name out of free-text transaction descriptions (e.g. "SQ *BLUE BOTTLE 8005551234 CA" -> merchant "Blue Bottle", category "Coffee/Dining") — requires external, bank/region-specific merchant->category mapping tables or an ML classifier; there is no wasm-safe merchant-taxonomy dataset in the proven-crates set, and bundling one is out-of-model. Without that inference the tool reduces to group-by on already-present columns (strict dup of csv-group-by); with it, it needs an external mapping/ML loader that isn't available. Same re-bundle-plus-out-of-model-delta class as the csv-wrangler skip.

images-to-gif  # duplicate of blocks/gif-from-images: that block already "combines a set of images into a single animated GIF, one frame per image in order" with a delay_ms per-frame delay (10-60000, default 500) and loops forever — pure Rust via the `image` crate's GifEncoder (no ffmpeg, runs on all backends incl. chat SW), chat+CLI, no page. It uses exactly this row's shape: Input::None + a required `images` source_list (Param::source_list("images",1)) resolved per-frame via resolve_source, plus a background color for letterbox padding. The backlog row — "Assembles a set of uploaded images into an animated GIF with a chosen frame delay" / "Combine these 5 images into a looping GIF, 200ms per frame" — is covered verbatim (frame delay = delay_ms, looping = set_repeat(Repeat::Infinite)). No distinct capability to add; the wasm-crates.md reference itself names gif-from-images as this exact pattern's exemplar. Scaffold not built.

run-javascript  # not viable in the pure-Rust/wafer model; same class as the already-skiplisted javascript-runner. "Execute a snippet of JavaScript/TypeScript in a sandbox and return its console output and result" requires a full JS engine at runtime: the only pure-Rust option, boa, compiles to wasm32-wasip1 but imports wasi `poll_oneoff` (async/timers) which the gizza wafer runtime does not provide, so the block fails to INSTANTIATE under `wafer build` (not just compile) — the exact blocker already documented for javascript-runner. quickjs/deno/v8 are all C/C++/Rust-embedding-native-lib engines that need a Node/native host, not the minimal WASI sandbox. TypeScript adds a transpile step on top (swc/tsc), and neither the chat SW nor the CLI/wafer host can eval untrusted JS safely without a real sandbox — this is the "would require unsafe/server/Node/pyodide" case. gizza is pure-Rust + ffmpeg with no JS-engine runtime. Revisit only if a pure-Rust JS engine that instantiates under the minimal wafer WASI host lands. Scaffold not built.
csv-clean  # re-bundle of verbs each already its own block (same class as csv-wrangler/csv-select): filter rows = blocks/csv-filter (column condition), sort = blocks/csv-sort (by one or more columns, asc/desc), dedupe = blocks/csv-dedupe (remove duplicate rows) AND blocks/csv-cleaner (whose summary is literally "Clean a CSV: trim, dedupe, drop empty rows, fill cells, normalize delimiter" — csv-clean also near-dups this block by name), select/drop columns = blocks/csv-reorder-columns (reorder, swap, or drop columns; column projection). The row's four verbs map verbatim onto these four+ existing blocks with no new engine or distinct user function; a one-call "pipeline" bundling is exactly the csv-wrangler skip. No distinct capability to build.
csv-encoding-fix  # duplicate/re-bundle of existing CSV/text repair blocks: blocks/charset-transcode already fixes Latin-1/Windows-1252↔UTF-8 mojibake with auto charset selection and pass count; blocks/csv-cleaner already normalizes delimiters and line endings while trimming/deduping/filling CSV rows; blocks/csv-change-delimiter re-emits CSV/TSV/semicolon/pipe data with corrected quoting; blocks/csv-type-inferrer/csv-column-type-validator already sniff delimiters. Combining "detect delimiter + fix mojibake + normalize line endings" into one pipeline adds no new core capability or surface; any auto-delimiter option belongs on csv-cleaner/change-delimiter.

password-strength-checker  # duplicate of blocks/password-entropy: that block already scores password strength (entropy bits from the lower/upper/digit/symbol pool x length, a Very weak..Very strong rating, and an offline crack-time estimate) AND flags weaknesses (shorter than 8, single character type, common-password membership via a built-in list, single repeated char, sequential runs like 1234/abcd) -- i.e. exactly this row's "scores password strength". Its analyze() already computes internally every signal a policy would check: length, has_lower/upper/digit/symbol (and the class count), and common-password membership. The sole delta -- a CONFIGURABLE POLICY (min length / required character classes / forbid-common thresholds -> pass/fail verdict) -- adds only threshold parameters and a boolean verdict over signals password-entropy already derives; per the established pattern (csv-select, csv-wrangler, pii-scanner, ecc-keygen: a lone novel delta on top of an otherwise-duplicate scope belongs as an ADDED capability on the existing block), that is an optional policy mode on password-entropy, not a distinct tool. Same pure-Rust no-dep engine, same offline "password never leaves the device" model; no distinct capability to build.

product-mockup  # not viable in the pure-Rust/wafer model: "composite a design/screenshot onto a product mockup (t-shirt, mug, tote, poster)" needs two things gizza cannot supply. (1) EXTERNAL TEMPLATE ASSETS — a realistic mockup requires bundling high-res base product photos (a blank tee, a mug, a tote, a poster frame) plus their per-product displacement/lighting/mask maps; these are licensed stock assets, not something the block can generate, and none ship in the repo. A single required binary design input plus an internal product library is also outside the single-source chat/CLI/page model. (2) PERSPECTIVE/DISPLACEMENT WARP — pasting the artwork so it looks printed means a perspective transform + displacement mapping onto the product surface (mug curvature, fabric folds, poster foreshortening) and shading multiply; there is no wasm-safe perspective-warp/displacement crate in the proven set (wasm-crates.md has none; imageproc's geometric warp is not in-model and a flat un-warped overlay onto a bundled photo is a degenerate, low-value result). The `image` crate can only alpha-composite a flat overlay, which without warped product templates is just image-split-overlay/image-annotate-class compositing, not a mockup. This is the external-asset-library + non-wasm-warp + generative-rendering class the loop skiplists; revisit only if a wasm-safe perspective/displacement-warp crate AND a licensed product-template set land. Scaffold not built.

raw-to-jpg  # not viable in the pure-Rust/wafer model: decoding camera RAW (CR2/NEF/ARW/DNG) to a viewable JPEG/PNG needs per-camera TIFF-IFD parsing + demosaic + white-balance/color-matrix/gamma, none of which ffmpeg (the type_hint) can do — `ffmpeg -decoders` lists NO cr2/nef/arw decoder and no camera-RAW demuxer (only generic `tiff`, which won't debayer a raw-CFA DNG), and the `image` crate decodes no proprietary camera RAW. The only pure-Rust path, rawloader 0.37 (decode CFA/metadata) + imagepipe 0.5 (demosaic→sRGB), COMPILES for wasm32-wasip1 but is built on rayon multithreading in its CORE decode/render path — rawloader's `decode_threaded`/`decode_threaded_multiline` use `par_chunks_mut` (basics.rs) and imagepipe's buffer.rs/scaling.rs use rayon `par_iter` — so it fails the `wafer build` INSTANTIATE gate (rayon-core needs `std::thread::spawn`, unsupported under the minimal WASI host) and panics at rayon pool-init on the browser wasm32-unknown-unknown page build (no wasm-threads). Also imagepipe's `simple_decode_8bit` takes a filesystem `Path`, not the byte buffer the block model feeds. A single-threaded hand-roll of the full four-format RAW pipeline is equivalent to reimplementing rawloader and is out of scope / not in the proven-crates set. Same non-wasm-threading + loader-blocked class as the ffmpeg-can't-decode / rayon-blocked skips. Revisit only if a wasm-safe, single-threaded RAW→RGB crate that instantiates under wafer lands. Scaffold not built.
webm-to-mp4  # duplicate of blocks/video-transcode (format=mp4): its plan_transcode("mp4", quality, in) re-encodes ANY input container ffmpeg auto-detects — WebM included — to a broadly-compatible MP4 via `-i in -c:v libx264 -c:a aac -crf <q> -movflags +faststart out.mp4` (core/src/lib.rs build_argv Format::Mp4). Unlike the mov-to-mp4 block (justified by a lossless `-c copy` remux because MOV is ISO-BMFF with MP4-legal H.264/HEVC+AAC streams), WebM carries VP8/VP9 video + Vorbis/Opus audio — NOT broadly MP4-legal — so there is NO stream-copy path; webm->mp4 ALWAYS requires a full re-encode to H.264/AAC, which is precisely video-transcode's job. "webm" is just a source container, not a distinct capability. Same class as the mp4-to-mp3 / audio-format-converter dups.
avi-to-mp4  # duplicate of blocks/video-transcode (format=mp4): it already re-encodes any ffmpeg-detected input container, including legacy AVI/DivX/Xvid/MJPEG sources, to H.264/AAC MP4 with CRF quality and +faststart.
wmv-to-mp4  # duplicate of blocks/video-transcode (format=mp4): it already transcodes any ffmpeg-detected input container, including WMV/VC-1 sources, to cross-platform H.264/AAC MP4 with CRF quality and +faststart.
video-nsfw-content-flagger  # out of model: classifying frames for explicit/NSFW content requires a trained image classifier (CNN/vision model + weights) to score visual content; ffmpeg can only sample/extract frames, and no pure-Rust/wasm-safe NSFW-detection crate ships in the proven set. There is no rule-based/pixel-heuristic path that reliably flags "explicit content" — the whole tool IS the ML classifier. Same out-of-model class as other semantic/ML-classifier skips; revisit only if a wasm-safe NSFW-classification model lands. Scaffold not built.
screenshot-secret-ocr-checker  # out of model: the tool's core is OCR — recognizing rendered text from screenshot pixels — which requires a trained text-recognition engine (Tesseract/LSTM or a neural OCR model + weights). ffmpeg (the type_hint is "pure" but the input is an image) can only decode/resample pixels, not read glyphs, and there is NO wasm-safe pure-Rust OCR crate in the proven set (wasm-crates.md has none; no tesseract/leptonica binding builds+instantiates under wafer). The secret-flagging step (regex over recognized text for emails/API keys/tokens/IPs) is trivially in-model, but it is downstream of the OCR that produces the text — without a text layer there is nothing to scan, so the whole tool IS the OCR engine. gizza already ships regex-based secret/PII redactors that operate on TEXT input (blocks/redact-pii, blocks/json-redact, blocks/csv-pii-redactor), so the in-model half is covered; only the image->text OCR is missing and it is the out-of-model half. Same out-of-model/ML-classifier class as video-nsfw-content-flagger; revisit only if a wasm-safe OCR crate that instantiates under wafer lands. Scaffold not built.
loudness-normalizer  # duplicate of blocks/audio-normalize
video-chapter-splitter  # out of model (multi-OUTPUT ffmpeg; mirror of the video-concat/video-merge single-source skips): the tool's whole purpose is to split ONE recording at a list of chapter timestamps into N SEPARATE clips returned together (a zip of clips). The gizza dispatch + page model produces exactly ONE output file: block-utils ExecArgs.output is a single read-back filename and dispatch_ffmpeg returns a single Vec<u8> (block-utils/src/ffmpeg.rs, lib.rs), and the page driver renders only format=image|video|audio (single media + download link) or text -- there is NO zip/multi-file output format (references/page-patterns.md), so a zip-of-clips page renders as unusable base64 text. ffmpeg also cannot run in the chat Service Worker (chat-ffmpeg is page+CLI only), so an N-clip zip would be CLI-ONLY with no page and no chat surface -- the same "effectively un-buildable" situation as multi-input ffmpeg (video-concat/video-merge/photo-slideshow-video). The only single-output framing (return ONE chapter's clip = a [start,end] window) is a strict duplicate of blocks/video-trim (stream-copy trim to a [start,start+duration] window) / blocks/video-cut-segments (keep/join listed windows). Revisit only if a zip/multi-file page output format + multi-pass ffmpeg surface land. Scaffold not built.
convolution-reverb  # out of model: true convolution reverb needs a second media input (the measured impulse-response audio file) alongside the dry audio; the current gizza media descriptor/page model supports one uploaded media input plus scalar params. A preset/simulated reverb with no supplied IR would be a different tool, not this backlog row. Revisit only if multi-media-input ffmpeg tools land.
document-deskew  # duplicate of blocks/document-scan: document-scan's core (core/src/lib.rs) already "detect a document's four corners in a photo and perspective-correct (dewarp) it into a flat, cropped, tonally-cleaned scan" — it decodes the photo, finds the page quadrilateral (explicit `corners` or Otsu-contrast auto-detect), solves the 4-point projective homography, inverse-maps every output pixel with bilinear sampling, and applies an optional 90/180/270 rotation to fix orientation. That is exactly what document-deskew asks for ("Corrects perspective and rotation on a photo of a document so the page is flat and upright"): perspective correction + rotation → a flat, upright page. document-scan is a strict superset (it also offers tonal modes magic/grayscale/blackwhite/color, a4/letter/square output proportions, and a white margin), so a separate deskew tool would be a redundant subset of the same homography+rotate pipeline. Same semantic-near-dup class the exact-slug picker can't catch. Scaffold not built.
audiobook-m4b-builder  # multi-INPUT ffmpeg (mirror of the video-concat/audio-concat/video-mux-external-audio skips): its whole purpose is to combine MULTIPLE mp3 chapter files into one .m4b, i.e. >1 audio input concatenated in order, PLUS an optional SEPARATE cover-art image input and per-chapter marker metadata derived from each source. The gizza dispatch + page model accepts exactly ONE uploaded media input: block-utils dispatch_ffmpeg is single-input (ExecArgs is one input file, ExecResult is one Vec<u8>) and the page file driver takes a single upload; ffmpeg also cannot run in the chat Service Worker (chat-ffmpeg is page+CLI only). A multi-mp3 + cover-image builder therefore has no in-model chat or page surface and would be CLI-ONLY, which does not satisfy loop verification. Chapter-marker embedding + m4a/AAC muxing themselves are fine in ffmpeg; the blocker is purely the multi-input surface. Same architecture-fit class as video-concat / audio-concat / audio-crossfade / video-merge / video-mux-external-audio. Revisit only if a multi-input/multi-source ffmpeg dispatch surface lands. Scaffold not built.
pdf-nup  # not viable in the current pure-Rust/wasmi model: N-up/booklet imposition needs robust PDF page-content composition (copy each source page as a form XObject, scale/translate/rotate into a new sheet, merge resources safely, preserve fonts/transparency/annotations/boxes). Existing PDF tools can reorder/rotate/delete/stamp text, but none render or compose whole page graphics; the same full-page rendering/composition class is blocked by the pdf-to-images renderer limits. A toy lopdf page-tree rewrite would corrupt real PDFs. Revisit if a proven wasm-safe PDF page imposition/composition engine lands.
csv-append  # duplicate of blocks/csv-merge: csv-merge's core (core/src/lib.rs `merge(files, has_header, delimiter)`) already "Concatenates (stacks) multiple CSVs into one. With a header, the output uses the UNION of all input headers (first-seen order) and maps each file's rows into it by column name (missing columns are blank)." That is exactly what csv-append asks for ("Stack/append multiple CSVs vertically into one, aligning columns by header (union of columns)"): vertical row stacking of N CSV texts with header-union column alignment. csv-merge also exposes the same delimiter option (comma/tab/semicolon/pipe) and a header toggle (positional stacking when off), so a csv-append tool would be a strict rename/subset of the identical concatenation pipeline. Same semantic-near-dup class the exact-slug picker can't catch. Scaffold not built.
avif-convert  # not viable across the required surfaces (encode half is out-of-model; decode+webp half duplicates image-convert): the "to AVIF" direction needs an AV1 encoder, which the page/chat runtime lacks. The browser surface runs @ffmpeg/core@0.12.10 (stock single-thread build) which ships libx264/x265/vpx/webp/mp3lame but NO libaom-av1 / no AV1 encoder / no AVIF muxer, so `-c:v av1`/`.avif` OUTPUT fails in-browser (same verified limitation behind the png-to-avif and image-optimize skips). System-ffmpeg (CLI) has libaom-av1, but CLI-only is insufficient per loop rules (page+chat must work). The remaining "from AVIF" direction (decode AVIF -> jpeg/png/webp) is in-model, but that is exactly what blocks/image-convert already covers for jpeg|png|webp targets, and a decode-only "converter" is a strict subset of image-convert with no distinct capability. Same out-of-model AV1-encoder class as png-to-avif; revisit only if the browser ffmpeg build gains an AV1 encoder (libaom/librav1e/libsvtav1) or a wasm-safe pure-Rust AVIF encoder lands. Scaffold not built.
password-policy-checker  # duplicate of blocks/password-entropy: same tool as the already-skiplisted password-strength-checker row. This row's "configurable policy (length, character classes, repeats) and a built-in common-password blocklist, scoring its strength" is exactly what password-entropy::analyze() already computes internally -- length, has_lower/upper/digit/symbol plus a class count, common-password membership (exact AND substring via a built-in list), single-repeated-character, sequential-run (1234/abcd) detection, and an entropy-bits -> Very weak..Very strong rating with an offline crack-time estimate. The sole delta -- turning those signals into configurable thresholds (min length / required classes / forbid-common) that emit a pass/fail verdict -- adds only parameters and a boolean over signals already derived; per the established pattern (a lone novel delta on an otherwise-duplicate scope is an ADDED capability/mode on the existing block, not a new tool -- same reasoning that skiplisted password-strength-checker) that is an optional policy mode on password-entropy. Same pure-Rust no-dep engine, same offline "password never leaves the device" model; no distinct capability to build.
video-pixelate-region  # duplicate of blocks/video-blur-region: that block already "Pixelates (mosaics) a fixed rectangle to censor part of the frame" as a first-class mode. video-blur-region's core (core/src/lib.rs) exposes Mode::Pixelate whose filter_complex crops the fixed x/y/width/height rectangle, downscales it to (w/strength)x(h/strength) with flags=neighbor, scales it back up nearest-neighbour so each source block becomes one flat mosaic tile, then overlays it back onto every frame -- exactly the "pixelate a fixed rectangle to censor part of the video frame" this row asks for. Its descriptor already ships x/y/width/height (required) + mode enum ["blur","pixelate"] + strength (1-100 = mosaic block size in px for pixelate), re-encoding via libx264/aac. video-pixelate-region is therefore a strict subset (pixelate-only) of video-blur-region with no distinct capability -- the mosaic block size is just strength with mode=pixelate. Same semantic-near-dup class the exact-slug picker can't catch (cf. the image-side pair image-pixelate-censor which likewise unifies pixelate+blur region censoring in one block). Scaffold not built.
code-screenshot-beautifier  # duplicate of blocks/code-screenshot: that block already renders a code snippet into a polished screenshot PNG with exactly this feature set. code-screenshot's core (core/src/lib.rs `render_png`) uses syntect for syntax highlighting (language hint + friendly aliases, plaintext fallback), draws carbon-style window chrome (title bar with red/amber/green traffic-light dots), applies inner padding (PAD) between code and card edge plus an outer padded background margin (MARGIN) around the window card, and derives background/chrome colours from the selected theme. The picked row's "polished screenshot image with syntax highlighting, window chrome, padding, and a gradient backdrop" is that same tool; the only delta ("gradient backdrop" vs the current theme-derived solid backdrop) is a minor cosmetic variant of the outer-margin fill, i.e. an optional backdrop mode on the existing block, not a distinct capability. Same semantic-near-dup class the exact-slug picker can't catch. Scaffold not built.
chroma-key-composite-video  # not viable in the current gizza model (multi-input ffmpeg): chroma-key compositing needs TWO media inputs at once -- a foreground video to key out (green/blue screen) PLUS a separate background image/video to composite the keyed foreground over (ffmpeg `[1:v]chromakey[fg];[0:v][fg]overlay`). The gizza page surface has a SINGLE file upload, and chat ffmpeg cannot run in the Service Worker, so a two-input composite can only work CLI-side -- exactly the multi-input ffmpeg case documented as un-buildable in create-next-tool/references/page-patterns.md ("Multi-input ffmpeg ... the page file-input is a single upload and ffmpeg can't run in the chat SW, so it'd be CLI-only -- skiplist + defer"). Same class as the video-concat skip. The existing single-input video blocks (video-blur-region, image-split-overlay, etc.) all take exactly one media input; none can supply a second background stream through the page/chat surfaces. Revisit only if the page gains a second file input AND chat gains multi-input ffmpeg. Scaffold not built.
