exif-viewer  # duplicate of blocks/image-metadata-viewer (reads + lists every EXIF/TIFF tag from a JPEG/TIFF/HEIF photo: camera/lens/exposure/timestamps/software + decoded GPS via kamadak-exif; same scope as extract-exif/view-exif skips)
audio-info-probe  # duplicate of blocks/media-info (media-info uses pure-Rust symphonia to report container, per-track codec (AAC/MP3/FLAC/Opus/Vorbis/PCM/ADPCM/etc.), sample_rate, channels + channel_layout, bits_per_sample, per-track and overall duration, and overall_bitrate_kbps for any audio OR video file. audio-info-probe promises exactly codec/bitrate/sample-rate/channels/duration of an audio file — a strict subset of media-info audio output, same F3 no-page file->text report shape. Same scope.)
dominant-color-extractor  # duplicate of blocks/color-palette-extract: that block already extracts the top-N dominant colors from an image and returns per-color hex (#rrggbb) + rgb + coverage fraction (share-of-image), ordered most-dominant first, via a `colors` param (1-64, default 6). It quantizes the image into an optimal N-entry palette (NeuQuant) then counts each pixel's nearest palette entry to rank by dominance — semantically identical to this row ("clusters pixels ... top N representative colors with hex codes and coverage percentages"); only the clustering algorithm name (k-means vs NeuQuant) differs, which is an implementation detail, not a distinct surface. Scaffold not built.
image-palette-to-css  # duplicate of blocks/color-palette-extract: that block already extracts the dominant palette from an image, returns JSON with top-N hex/rgb/hsl swatches plus pixel-share percentages, and includes a ready-to-paste CSS custom-properties block (`css_variables`) for the extracted colors. It uses the same image->palette user workflow and exposes the same `colors` control (1-64, default 6). A separate "to CSS" variant would only rename/filter existing output fields (and a swatch strip is a presentation mode), not add a distinct gizza capability. Scaffold not built.
jwt-structure-validator  # duplicate of blocks/jwt-decode: jwt-decode already decodes a compact JWT fully OFFLINE with no verification key and does exactly the "structure validator" job — split_token() enforces the three-part structure (rejects 5-segment JWE and any count != 2/3), b64url_decode() errors when a header/payload/signature segment is not valid base64url, and the time-claim checks flag expired (exp) / not-yet-valid (nbf) / iat via a leeway param. It reports signature_present but never verifies the signature (that is blocks/jwt-verify's job). The proposed jwt-structure-validator ("decodes a JWT, validates its three-part structure and base64url segments, flags expired/not-yet-valid claims without verifying the signature") is a verbatim restatement of jwt-decode's scope — same F3 pure text->JSON surface, same header+payload+checks output. Scaffold not built.
lab-result-interpreter  # not viable in gizza's current safety scope (automated medical interpretation): the core deliverable is flagging pasted blood-test values as low/normal/high against BUNDLED reference ranges plus plain-language notes -- i.e. unqualified medical interpretation of clinical lab results. A single bundled generic range cannot account for the context real lab flagging requires: units (mg/dL vs mmol/L -- a unit mismatch alone inverts the flag), fasting state (glucose 110 is "HIGH" fasting but normal non-fasting), age/sex/pregnancy-specific ranges, and the assay-/lab-specific reference interval printed on the user's own report. With that context unavailable to a pasted-number tool, the low/normal/high flags and interpretive notes would be confidently WRONG for a large fraction of real inputs, in a domain where wrong output causes real harm (false alarm, or false reassurance that delays care) -- health-misinformation risk that disclaimers do not neutralize. The only safe reduction (user pastes BOTH the value and their own report's reference range, tool does a mechanical in/out-of-range comparison) discards the bundled-reference-range value that is the whole point of the row, leaving a trivial numeric compare. Not a duplicate of any existing block; skiplisted on safety scope, not model feasibility. Scaffold not built.
encoding-converter  # duplicate of blocks/multi-encoder: that block already converts text across Base64, hex, binary, and URL percent-encoding in either direction (encode|decode) -- its core exposes an Encoding enum (Base64/Hex/Binary/Url, plus Rot13/Morse) and a Direction enum (Encode/Decode) with a single transform(text, enc, dir) entry point. The picked row ("converts text/bytes between Base64, Base64URL, hex, URL-encoding, and binary in either direction") is that exact same encode/decode-across-formats tool with the same four core formats; the only delta is Base64URL, a one-line engine variant (base64::engine::general_purpose::URL_SAFE) that belongs as an added choice on multi-encoder, not as a separate near-identical tool. Also overlaps the dedicated hex-codec / binary-codec / url-encode / base-decoder blocks. Same semantic-near-dup class the exact-slug picker can't catch. Scaffold not built.
base64-image-inline  # duplicate of blocks/file-to-data-uri (encode direction) + blocks/data-uri-decode (extract direction): "converts an image to a base64 data URI for inlining" is exactly file-to-data-uri, which encodes ANY source file -- images included -- as a self-contained `data:<mime>;base64,<...>` URI via Input::File (url or ref), taking the MIME from the file with an optional `mime` override, for inline embedding in CSS url(...), <img src>, email, or JSON. "Extracts images back out of data URIs" is exactly data-uri-decode, which parses a `data:` URI (RFC 2397) into its MIME/params/encoding, decodes the payload, and for binary payloads magic-byte-sniffs the file type (e.g. detected_type "image/png") and returns bytes + hex preview -- so pulling the image back out of a data URI is already covered. An image-only variant is a strict subset of the existing general file<->data-URI pair (the general blocks already handle image/* MIME types); a narrower image-only tool adds no new capability to the gizza model. Also overlaps blocks/data-uri-encode (text->data URI) and blocks/extract-decode-base64 (scan text for embedded base64 blobs). Same semantic-near-dup class the exact-slug picker can't catch. Scaffold not built.

video-set-crf-quality  # duplicate of blocks/video-compress: that block already re-encodes a video at a chosen constant-quality CRF value via a single-pass libx264/AAC pass, trading file size against visual quality on a slider (descriptor exposes Param::number("crf") min 18 max 34, default 28, "Lower = higher quality/larger; higher = smaller"; summary "Shrink a video's file size with a single-pass CRF re-encode to H.264/AAC"). The picked row ("Re-encodes at a chosen constant-quality CRF value, trading file size against visual quality on a simple slider") is that exact same single-pass CRF quality knob. A wider CRF range (e.g. 0-51) is a param-bound tweak to video-compress, not a distinct capability. Same class as the already-skiplisted video-compressor dup of the same block. Scaffold not built.
video-scene-cut-detector  # not viable in the pure-Rust/wafer model: an ffmpeg ANALYSIS-to-TEXT tool with no page/chat surface (same class as the multi-input video-concat and multi-output video-chapter-splitter skips, but on the analysis side). Detecting hard cuts requires DECODING frames (ffmpeg select='gt(scene,t)' + metadata=print), so it is an ffmpeg tool -- but its deliverable is a TEXT report of cut timestamps + lavfi.scene_score confidence, not a media artifact. The generator's ffmpeg page runtime renders ONLY media: tool-ffmpeg.js#runFfmpeg always returns a `data:<mime>;base64` URL from the produced file and tool.js (runtime==="ffmpeg" branch) requires #tool-output-media/#tool-output-download, explicitly rejecting `runtime=ffmpeg + format=text` with "tool misconfigured: ffmpeg runtime needs an image/video/audio output" (tool.js:493-497). The pure/default page runtime CAN render text, but it runs the web export entirely in wasm with NO ffmpeg bridge, so it cannot decode a video in-browser. No block in the repo renders text/number output from an ffmpeg runtime (verified: zero meta.toml with runtime="ffmpeg" + format="text"/"number"). Chat ffmpeg is also non-functional (the Service Worker forbids import()/Worker), so the tool would be CLI-ONLY with no page and no chat surface -- the documented "effectively un-buildable" bar (references/page-patterns.md: single-surface ffmpeg tools that can only run in the CLI are skiplist+defer). A single-output-FILE reframing does not help: dispatch_ffmpeg reads back one file and returns its bytes (block-utils/src/ffmpeg.rs ExecResult.output + lib.rs dispatch_ffmpeg discards .log), and even if metadata=print writes the scores to a readback .txt the page still has nowhere to parse+render them. The only pageable reframings are DIFFERENT tools: burn cut markers into a VIDEO, or emit a scene-thumbnail contact-sheet IMAGE (tile filter) -- neither is "report each cut's timestamp and confidence score". Revisit only if a text/JSON output surface for the ffmpeg page runtime lands. Scaffold not built.
wavelet-denoise  # duplicate of blocks/audio-noise-reduce (+ not faithfully buildable in the ffmpeg model): audio-noise-reduce already "Reduces steady background hiss/hum/broadband noise from an audio file" and explicitly targets BROADBAND noise -- its core Method::Anlmdn (ffmpeg anlmdn, non-local means) is documented "slower, can preserve transients better on broadband noise" and Method::Afftdn (FFT-based) is the general adaptive-floor denoiser; descriptor already ships strength(1-100), method enum [afftdn|anlmdn], remove_hum, and mp3/wav/ogg/flac/m4a output over Input::Audio. The picked row ("Removes broadband noise from audio using wavelet-threshold denoising") is that exact user-facing surface -- decode audio, attenuate broadband noise, re-encode -- differing only in the named DSP algorithm (wavelet soft-threshold vs FFT/NLM). ffmpeg has NO wavelet AUDIO denoiser (no awavelet/awt filter), and this repo's ffmpeg tools only BUILD argv for ffmpeg to run -- the Rust core never touches decoded PCM, so a genuine DWT->soft-threshold->IDWT pipeline over audio samples is not expressible in the pure-Rust/ffmpeg architecture (it is neither the pure typed-in->typed-out shape nor an ffmpeg filtergraph). The only buildable "wavelet-denoise" collapses onto audio-noise-reduce's afftdn/anlmdn broadband denoiser, adding no distinct surface -- the wavelet algorithm name is an implementation detail, not a new capability. Same broadband-audio-denoise scope as the already-deferred audio-denoise row; that row's "audio-INPUT deferred" reason is now stale (audio-input tools became buildable 2026-07-02, which is how audio-noise-reduce shipped). Scaffold not built.
speech-segmenter  # multi-OUTPUT artifact, unsupported (same class as audio-split-on-silence / audiobook-chapter-splitter): the deliverable is N SEPARATE utterance clips, one per non-silent region between detected speech/silence boundaries (ffmpeg's segment muxer -> out%03d.wav). gizza's ffmpeg dispatch is single-output: ExecArgs.output is ONE filename String and ExecResult.output is ONE Vec<u8> (block-utils/src/ffmpeg.rs:107,113) -- the harness reads back exactly one named file, and the page/chat surface renders a single audio envelope (page format="audio" -> one <audio>). There is no zip/multi-file output surface for ffmpeg tools. The only single-output reframing -- collapse the silent gaps into ONE clip -- is already blocks/audio-silence-remove (silenceremove filter, keeps 0.25s of each gap so speech keeps natural micro-pauses) and blocks/video-silence-cut; the distinct "emit each utterance as its own file" intent is precisely the unsupported multi-output case. Revisit only if a multi-file/zip output surface lands.
file-lock  # duplicate of blocks/encrypt-file (password-encrypt any file into a self-contained portable blob you can share, and decrypt it back with the same passphrase — AES-256-GCM, key derived via PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256, self-describing header magic|salt|nonce|ciphertext+tag. Exactly this row's "password-encrypts a file into a self-contained blob you can share, and decrypts such blobs back to the original." key-format-convert  # duplicate of blocks/pem-der-convert + blocks/pem-to-jwk: the named function ("Converts cryptographic keys between PEM, DER, and JWK representations") is already the union of two existing blocks. blocks/pem-der-convert is a generic PEM<->DER re-encoder for any object (RSA/EC keys, X.509 certs, CSRs) in either direction (Direction::Auto|PemToDer|DerToPem, DerFormat::Hex|Base64, accepts DER in as hex or base64) -- that is exactly the "keys between PEM and DER" half. blocks/pem-to-jwk converts PEM keys -> JWK (RFC 7517/7518) for RSA (PKCS#1/PKCS#8/SPKI) and EC over P-256/P-384/P-521, emitting public or private JWK -- that is the "and JWK" half; blocks/jwk-thumbprint and blocks/pem-public-key-extract cover adjacent JWK/key needs. A single "key-format-convert" over PEM/DER/JWK adds no user-facing capability the toolkit lacks -- it merely fuses pem-der-convert and pem-to-jwk under one slug, fragmenting focused tools. The only pairwise directions not already shipped (JWK->PEM/DER) are a param-bound extension to pem-to-jwk, not a distinct tool. Same class as other multi-format-merge dups. Scaffold not built.
url-extractor  # duplicate of blocks/extract-urls: the named function ("Finds and normalizes all URLs in free text, including bare and parenthesized links") is exactly extract-urls's scope — its core "extract all http(s) URLs from text, deduplicate (first-seen order), and optionally split each into scheme/host/port/path/query/fragment" (regex `\bhttps?://[^\s<>"'`)\]}]+` + `url` crate validation + HashSet dedup). Of the two claimed deltas: PARENTHESIZED links are ALREADY handled — its `does_not_grab_brackets` test asserts `(https://example.com/a) [http://b.io/c]` yields both bare URLs (the char class excludes `)`,`]`,`}` and trim_trailing strips abutting punctuation); NORMALIZE = the existing dedupe + component split. The ONLY genuine delta is BARE/schemeless autolink detection (matching `www.example.com`/`example.com` with no scheme, then prefixing a scheme) — a broadened-regex variant that belongs as an added mode on extract-urls (widen URLRE to optionally catch bare hosts, then normalize), not as a separate near-identical tool. Same "find every URL in free text and return them deduplicated" user function, same engine class. This is the same semantic-near-dup class the exact-slug picker can't catch — cf. the already-skiplisted percent-encode-decode/url-codec (dups of url-encode), url-parser (dup of parse-uri), and encoding-converter (whose sole Base64URL delta was ruled "a one-line engine variant that belongs as an added choice on multi-encoder, not as a separate near-identical tool"). Scaffold not built.
pivot-table  # duplicate of blocks/csv-pivot: the named function ("Builds a pivot table from tabular data with chosen row/column dimensions and an aggregation (sum/avg/count/min/max)") is exactly what csv-pivot does. csv-pivot's descriptor takes `data` = pasted CSV/tabular text (header row), `rows` = the row-key column(s) (chosen row dimension — comma-separated names or 1-based indices, distinct combinations become output rows), `columns` = the column dimension whose distinct values become the pivot's output columns, `values` = the column aggregated into each cell, and `agg` = enumv(sum,count,avg,min,max) default sum — a byte-for-byte match of this row's "chosen row/column dimensions and an aggregation (sum/avg/count/min/max)". Its core::pivot() groups rows by the row-key column(s), spreads the column field's distinct values across output columns, and aggregates the value column per cell (sum/count/avg/min/max), emitting the pivot as CSV. "tabular data" vs "CSV" is not a distinct capability — csv-pivot already accepts pasted tabular text with a selectable delimiter (comma/tab/semicolon/pipe). Same user function, same engine, same aggregation set; no distinct capability to build. Scaffold not built.
markdown-preview  # duplicate of blocks/markdown-render: the named function ("Renders Markdown to clean, sanitized HTML for preview") is exactly markdown-render's scope. markdown-render (summary "Render Markdown into sanitized HTML") takes a single `markdown` string input (Input::None + Param::string("markdown")) and renders CommonMark + GitHub-flavored extensions (tables, task lists, strikethrough, footnotes, smart punctuation, heading attributes) to HTML via pulldown-cmark, then runs the result through ammonia to strip anything unsafe (no <script>, no event handlers, no javascript: URLs) while preserving legitimate Markdown elements (disabled task-list checkboxes, code-fence language classes, footnote/heading ids) -- i.e. it already emits CLEAN, SANITIZED HTML safe to inject into a page, which is precisely "sanitized HTML for preview". The sole word of delta ("for preview") is the intended USE of that sanitized HTML, not a distinct capability -- markdown-render's output is the preview HTML. Same input (markdown text), same output (safe HTML), same engine (pulldown-cmark + ammonia); no distinct surface to build. Same semantic-near-dup class the exact-slug picker can't catch. Scaffold not built.
voice-activity-detector  # not viable in gizza's model (out-of-model ML + unsupported ffmpeg-analysis-to-text output). The picked deliverable is a precise speech-presence timeline (start/end timestamps of spoken SEGMENTS) as a DATA result. (a) The named implementation is a VAD MODEL (Silero/WebRTC-class ML) that distinguishes speech from non-speech (music/noise); gizza has NO ML runtime — grep of blocks/ finds zero onnx/candle/silero/whisper/transformers, and every audio tool (audio-noise-reduce afftdn/anlmdn, audio-silence-remove, etc.) is pure ffmpeg. Same loader-blocked class as multilingual-speech-to-text / video-speech-transcript-extractor. (b) The only in-model substitute is ffmpeg `silencedetect` (energy threshold, not true VAD — cannot tell speech from loud music/noise), whose speech segments are just the inverse of detected silence; that exact log-parse already lives INSIDE blocks/video-silence-cut (pass-1 detect_argv+parse_silences) and blocks/audio-silence-remove, which CONSUME it to produce trimmed MEDIA. Exposing it as a standalone timeline REPORT is the same unsupported ffmpeg-analysis-to-TEXT shape skiplisted for video-scene-cut-detector: gizza's ffmpeg dispatch is single-media-output (block-utils/src/ffmpeg.rs ExecResult.output = one Vec<u8>, .log discarded), and the ffmpeg page runtime renders ONLY media — tool.js:496 hard-rejects `runtime=ffmpeg + format=text` ("ffmpeg runtime needs an image/video/audio output"), verified zero ffmpeg-runtime blocks emit text/number. Revisit only if an in-model VAD/ASR backend or a text-output surface for the ffmpeg page runtime lands. Scaffold not built.

image-sequence-to-video  # multi-input ffmpeg (mirror of the photo-slideshow-video / video-concat skips): its whole purpose is to encode a NUMBERED SEQUENCE of many PNG/JPG frames into ONE video at a chosen frame rate (ffmpeg `-framerate N -i frame%03d.png -c:v libx264 out.mp4`), i.e. >1 image input assembled in order. The gizza dispatch + page model accepts exactly ONE uploaded media input: block-utils dispatch_ffmpeg is single-input (ExecArgs is one input file, ExecResult is one Vec<u8>) and the page file driver takes a single upload; ffmpeg also cannot run in the chat Service Worker (chat-ffmpeg is page+CLI only), so a many-frames->video encoder would be CLI-ONLY with no page and no chat surface — the documented "effectively un-buildable" multi-input ffmpeg bar (references/page-patterns.md). No pure-Rust escape hatch: unlike gif-from-images (the `image` crate's GifEncoder assembles frames to an animated GIF with NO ffmpeg), a real VIDEO codec (H.264/mp4 at a chosen fps) requires ffmpeg, so this cannot be a chat+CLI pure-Rust multi-image tool. Same architecture-fit class as photo-slideshow-video / video-concat / audiobook-m4b-builder. Revisit only if a multi-input/multi-source ffmpeg dispatch surface lands. Scaffold not built.
csv-delimiter-converter  # duplicate of blocks/csv-change-delimiter: that block's core::change_delimiter(data, from, to) already reparses CSV/TSV/semicolon/pipe data with a chosen `from` field separator (literal char or the words tab/comma/semicolon/pipe) and re-emits it with a chosen `to` separator via the `csv` crate, which fixes up quoting for the new delimiter — fields containing the new delimiter get quoted, fields that no longer need quoting are unquoted (its own tests cover requotes_fields_containing_new_delimiter and unquotes_when_no_longer_needed). That is exactly this row's "Reparses CSV/TSV/semicolon data and re-emits it with a different delimiter, preserving quoting." find-replace-with-diff  # duplicate of blocks/find-replace (+ text-diff for the diff half), with an out-of-model interactive gate. The core transform — "batch regex find-and-replace across pasted text" — is exactly find-replace's scope: its core find_replace(text, find, replacement, is_regex, case_sensitive, global) already applies a regex OR literal find-and-replace over pasted text with case-sensitivity, global/replace-first, and $1/${name} capture-group references (regex mode) or verbatim replacement (literal mode), returning the new text plus a match count. The "shows a diff preview" half is exactly blocks/text-diff's scope: it already computes an LCS line diff between an old (left) and new (right) text and renders it as a unified `@@`-hunk diff or structured JSON — i.e. before/after find-replace output is a text-diff render, not a new capability. The only headline differentiator, "before you accept the result," is an INTERACTIVE accept/preview gating state (apply-then-confirm) that is not expressible in gizza's pure typed-in→typed-out chat/CLI/page model: every surface renders a single output and there is no accept button that gates whether the replacement is committed. The lone in-model delta is BATCH (applying multiple find/replace rules in one pass vs find-replace's single rule); per the established skiplist pattern (csv-select's rename delta on csv-reorder-columns, regex-extractor's all-capture-groups delta on regex-extract, url-extractor's bare-URL delta on extract-urls) a single widened-variant delta belongs as an added multi-rule mode on the existing find-replace block, not as a distinct tool whose transform + diff both strictly duplicate existing blocks. Scaffold not built.

That is exactly this row's "Compare two CSV/tables and report added, removed, and changed rows (optionally keyed by a column)." csv-cell-diff is a strict superset — it additionally reports per-cell changes and added/removed whole columns and emits a flat csv change-log — so a row-level csv-diff adds no distinct capability, engine, or surface (same wasm-safe csv-crate parsing, same key-or-positional pairing). Same user function; not a distinct tool. Scaffold not built.

(1) COMPRESSION/ENCODING half = strict dup: producing "a compressed URL-safe payload that travels in the link" is exactly lz-string-compress format=uri (lz_str::compress_to_encoded_uri_component = pieroxy's compressToEncodedURIComponent — the URL-query-safe alphabet with +// -> -/_ and no = padding that, in that block's own page words, "drops straight into a ?param= value with no further encodeURIComponent"; byte-compatible with the JS library, with matching decompress). raw-deflate + base64url (blocks/raw-deflate) covers the deflate route, and blocks/data-uri-encode / url-encode cover the plain encodings. Wrapping that same payload as "<base>#<payload>" adds no compression/encoding capability. (2) The ONLY delta over lz-string-compress — a clickable, SHAREABLE URL that self-decodes the fragment WHEN OPENED (the itty.bitty.site / paste-app pattern) — needs a hosted decode app pinned to a fixed domain to read location.hash, decompress, and render. This public toolkit ships no app/deploy (the branded hosted site is a separate private repo that consumes this one at a pin) and page copy must stay GENERIC/brand-free (check-tool-hygiene check 8 hard-fails any gizza.ai / domain string under page/), so there is no host the link can point at: the output would either hardcode a decode domain (banned) or emit a bare fragment with no decode host (useless, and == framing 1). password-hash  # duplicate of blocks/bcrypt-hash + blocks/argon2-hash: the named function ("Hash a password with bcrypt OR Argon2, and verify a password against a hash, for seeding or testing auth") is exactly the union of two existing focused blocks, both of which already hash AND verify with a fresh random salt. blocks/bcrypt-hash (summary "Hash or verify a password with bcrypt") exposes mode=hash|verify: hash() produces a standard $2b$ modular-crypt string at a configurable cost/work-factor (default 12), verify() checks a password against a $2a$/$2b$/$2x$/$2y$ hash (public hash(password,cost)->String and verify(password,hash)->bool). blocks/argon2-hash (summary "Hash or verify a password with Argon2id") exposes the same mode=hash|verify over Argon2id: hash() emits a PHC $argon2id$v=19$m=,t=,p=$salt$hash string with configurable memory_kib (default 19456)/iterations/parallelism, verify() reads the params back from the PHC string (public hash(password,m,t,p)->String and verify(password,phc)->bool). Between them the toolkit already ships both algorithms, both hashing and verification, and the "seeding/testing auth" use case. A single password-hash tool spanning "bcrypt or Argon2 + verify" adds no user-facing capability the toolkit lacks -- it merely fuses two focused blocks under one slug, fragmenting them; same class as the already-skiplisted key-format-convert (fused pem-der-convert + pem-to-jwk) and the argon2-verify/bcrypt-verify verify-mode dups. Scaffold not built.

The picked deliverable ("Render an HTML snippet or fetched page into a paginated PDF") is FAITHFUL CSS RENDERING: honor the document's fonts, colors, box model, floats/flex/grid, and paginate the laid-out result. That pipeline already exists as html-to-markdown | markdown-to-pdf and produces NO CSS-faithful rendering (no author fonts/colors/layout), so it neither satisfies the picked "render ... into a paginated PDF" intent nor adds a capability the toolkit lacks — it merely fuses two focused blocks under one slug. concat-files  # duplicate of blocks/files-to-prompt: the picked row ("Concatenates multiple uploaded text files into one document with clear per-file header separators") is exactly what files-to-prompt already does, and is a strict subset of its scope. files-to-prompt takes a single pasted `files` blob in which each file starts with a header line "<separator> path/to/file.ext" (default separator "==="), splits the blob at those header lines, and re-emits every file preceded by a formatted per-file header separator: in `format=plain` that is files-to-prompt's own layout ("path", a "---" rule, the contents, another "---"); in `format=markdown` (default) a "## path" heading + a language-fenced code block per file; `format=xml` wraps them in one Claude-style <documents>. That is precisely "one document with clear per-file header separators." Concatenating header-delimited text files with per-file separators adds no distinct capability, engine, or surface over files-to-prompt. That is precisely "a contact-sheet of thumbnails ... at ... intervals." pdf-redact  # NOT VIABLE as true redaction in gizza's pure-Rust/lopdf/wasm model, and any partial build is a false-secure SECURITY HAZARD (worse than no tool). The picked deliverable ("Permanently redact a PDF by removing the underlying text/images beneath black boxes -- true redaction") is a security GUARANTEE: every byte of sensitive content inside a marked region must be provably GONE, not merely covered. Drawing black boxes is trivial here (blocks/pdf-watermark already stamps isolated overlay content via lopdf q/BT/Tf/Tj/Q), but that is exactly the "not real redaction" the row rejects. Guaranteed removal is out of reach: (1) TEXT -- removing show-operator glyphs inside a rect needs the full text-positioning state machine PLUS per-glyph advance widths from font /Widths and, for CID/Type0 fonts, CMap/ToUnicode decoding; blocks/pdf-table-extract already runs that BT/Tf/Td/Tm/Tj machine and documents that runs whose encoding can't be decoded (e.g. unparseable ToUnicode CMap) are SILENTLY SKIPPED. For extraction, skipping = incomplete output (harmless); for redaction, skipping = the text stays under the box and is trivially copy-recoverable = a silent leak. Partial-string redaction (a run crossing the rect edge) further needs glyph splitting by width metrics gizza has no reliable path to. (2) IMAGES -- scanned documents (the #1 real redaction target) are a single page-sized image XObject; truly redacting them means DECODING the image, overwriting pixels in the region, and RE-ENCODING. That requires a full codec suite (DCTDecode/JPEG, CCITTFax, JBIG2, JPXDecode, Flate+predictors across colorspaces) instantiating under wasm32-wasip1 -- none of which this repo has; lopdf treats image streams as opaque bytes. A scanned PDF "redacted" here would draw a box with the full image intact beneath it -- delete the box, data returns. (3) Vector-path text, form XObjects, and nested/inherited content add more silently-missed surfaces. Because true redaction is a security promise and the pure-Rust/wasm surface can only ever cover SOME content while silently leaving images + CID-font text + XObject content recoverable, shipping it would actively mislead users into believing sensitive data is destroyed when it is not -- the classic false-secure failure. Same "the guarantee needs an engine gizza has no wasm-safe path to" non-viability class as the skiplisted html-to-pdf (needs a CSS layout engine) and the ML-detector rows. Revisit only if wasm-instantiable image codecs + a full font-metrics/CID text-layout redaction engine land. Scaffold not built.
data-chart  # duplicate of blocks/csv-chart-generator: the picked row ("Generate a chart (line/bar/scatter/pie) from supplied data") is exactly what csv-chart-generator already is -- a general-purpose chart generator that takes supplied tabular (CSV) data plus x/y column selectors and renders a standalone SVG in one of several chart types, selected by a chart_type param. csv-chart-generator's chart_type accepts bar | line | scatter | histogram (its render() explicitly validates the type and errors on unknown values such as "pie"), so 3 of data-chart's 4 requested types (line, bar, scatter) are already covered by the identical engine (hand-built SVG, pure-Rust, no plotting deps, runs on the chat Service Worker). The only non-overlapping type is "pie", which encodes a single categorical distribution (category -> value) -- functionally the same data shape a bar chart already renders in csv-chart-generator -- and is not grounds for a second general chart generator; adding a pie render mode would be an enhancement to csv-chart-generator, not a distinct tool with its own engine, capability, or surface. "From supplied data" is precisely csv-chart-generator's input model (a pasted CSV blob is the only tabular-data path in gizza's pure blocks, which are Input::None with text params). Same semantic-near-dup class the exact-slug picker cannot catch (like pdf-to-text vs pdf-extract-text). Scaffold not built.
format-js  # duplicate of blocks/js-beautify on the buildable half, and out-of-model on its distinguishing half. The picked row ("Formats JavaScript/TypeScript to a Prettier-compatible style") has two framings and both fail. (1) BUILDABLE half = strict dup: the only pure-Rust/wasm path to "format JS" is a token-aware pretty-printer that re-indents source without a real parser -- which is exactly blocks/js-beautify. That block already tokenizes JS (strings, template literals, regex literals, line/block comments, punctuators, arrows, word runs), tracks { ( [ nesting, and re-emits one statement/block-member per line with a configurable indent (spaces 1-8, default 2, or tabs via IndentChar::Tab), preserving strings/templates/regex/comments byte-for-byte verbatim so output is semantically identical -- i.e. producing clean, consistently-indented JavaScript from arbitrary/minified input. (2) The ONLY delta over js-beautify -- a TRUE Prettier-COMPATIBLE reprint -- is out of model: Prettier reprints from a full ESTree AST via its doc-based printer, deciding ASI semicolon insertion/removal, trailing-comma insertion, quote normalization, and print-width-driven line wrapping/breaking -- all of which require parsing JS/TS to an AST (ASI and trailing-comma safety are undecidable at the token level) and a Wadler-style doc layout engine. This repo has no wasm32-wasip1-instantiable JS/TS parser or doc printer (js-beautify itself documents it is "NOT a full parser"), so a token pass cannot honestly claim Prettier compatibility; shipping a reindenter under the "Prettier-compatible" label would be a false capability claim. Table-stakes Prettier params (printWidth wrapping, semi, singleQuote, trailingComma, tabWidth) are therefore out-of-model except tabWidth/tabs, which js-beautify already exposes. No distinct in-model surface. Same "the buildable form collapses into an existing block while the distinguishing guarantee needs an engine gizza has no wasm-safe path to" class as the skiplisted html-to-pdf. Scaffold not built.

prores-to-mp4  # duplicate of blocks/video-to-h264 (with mov-to-mp4 re-encode mode and video-compress covering the same operation). The picked row ("Compresses a large ProRes master into a shareable H.264 MP4") is exactly video-to-h264's job: it force-transcodes ANY input video — explicitly including the 10-bit / 4:2:2 sources that ProRes is — into a universally playable H.264 (libx264) MP4 with yuv420p 8-bit chroma, +faststart, and AAC audio, exposing a CRF-style quality knob and an H.264 profile choice. ProRes decode in ffmpeg is fully automatic (no ProRes-specific flag or filter exists), so the input codec being ProRes changes nothing about the argv; the operation is `-c:v libx264 -c:a aac -crf N` regardless. mov-to-mp4's re-encode mode is documented specifically for "ProRes and other non-MP4-legal MOV codecs", and video-compress is the CRF size/quality knob for the same H.264/MP4 output. There is no ProRes-specific in-model capability to differentiate a new tool. Scaffold not built.
av1-to-mp4  # duplicate of blocks/video-to-h264 (with video-compress covering the CRF size/quality knob). The picked row ("Transcodes an AV1 video into a broadly-compatible MP4 for devices that can't decode AV1") is exactly video-to-h264's job: it force-transcodes ANY input video — and its module doc EXPLICITLY names "10-bit / 4:2:2 / 4:4:4 / HEVC / VP9 / AV1 sources that 'won't play'" — into the single most universally playable form: H.264 (libx264) in an MP4 container, yuv420p 8-bit 4:2:0 chroma, +faststart, and AAC audio, exposing a CRF-style quality knob and an H.264 profile choice. AV1 decode in ffmpeg is fully automatic (libdav1d/libaom, no AV1-specific flag or filter exists), so the input codec being AV1 changes nothing about the argv; the operation is `-c:v libx264 -c:a aac -crf N -movflags +faststart` regardless of source codec. video-compress is the CRF size/quality knob for the same H.264/MP4 output. There is no AV1-specific in-model capability to differentiate a new tool. Same X-codec-to-mp4 semantic-near-dup class as the immediately-preceding prores-to-mp4 skiplist entry. Scaffold not built.
image-gaussian-blur  # duplicate of blocks/blur-image: that block already applies a TRUE Gaussian blur where its `radius` param IS the Gaussian standard deviation (sigma) in pixels — core::blur wraps image::imageops::blur (documented "the `radius` argument is the Gaussian standard deviation (sigma) in pixels"), validates a finite positive sigma, caps it at 200, and returns PNG. This row's "Applies a precise Gaussian blur at a chosen sigma for smoothing or background softening" is exactly blur-image's single capability — chosen sigma → smoothing/background softening. Renaming the knob from "radius" to "sigma" is not a distinct tool (they are the same quantity here); the only nominal delta would be surfacing a "sigma" label, which is a copy tweak on blur-image, not a parallel block. Same bundle-of-existing-verbs / semantic-near-dup class the exact-slug picker can't catch. Scaffold not built.

largest-files-finder  # not viable as described (no recursive dropped-folder filesystem access in gizza's model), and its only buildable form is a strict view over blocks/zip-inspect. (1) SURFACE: "scans a dropped folder" needs recursive folder-drop FS access, which no gizza surface provides — the page-generator runtime has zero folder-input support (no webkitdirectory/directory picker anywhere under tools/generator), pure blocks are Input::None + text params with no upload at all, and file-input blocks take exactly ONE upload slot (Input::File, a single SourceFields). The one existing "batch of files -> filename+size manifest" tool, blocks/dir-checksum-report, is chat/CLI-only and documents in its own module header that it has NO PAGE precisely because "a multi-file report needs more than one upload slot, like blocks/csv-merge and blocks/merge-pdf." So there is no headless-verifiable (page + CLI) surface on which "scans a dropped folder and lists the top-N largest files" can be honored. (2) The ONLY in-model way to get "top-N largest files with full paths + sizes" from a single droppable container is to read a ZIP's central directory -> each entry's full path + uncompressed size, which is exactly blocks/zip-inspect: Input::File, pure-Rust `zip`, Entry { name = full path inside the archive, size: u64 = uncompressed size, ... } plus a Listing.total_size, listing contents WITHOUT extracting. Sorting those entries by uncompressed size descending and slicing top-N is a trivial ordering/view over zip-inspect's already-emitted path+size fields — no distinct engine (same central-directory parse), no distinct capability, no distinct surface; the "reclaim space" framing adds no new data. (3) Modeled instead as a pasted TEXT listing of path,size lines it computes nothing real (just re-sorts numbers the user typed) and does not satisfy "scans a folder." Same "the folder/multi-file drop surface does not exist, and the buildable form collapses into an existing block" class as dir-checksum-report's no-page note and the concat-files/files-to-prompt dup. Revisit only if a recursive folder-drop input surface lands in the platform. Scaffold not built.
image-color-quantizer  # duplicate of blocks/image-color-quantize (same word + trailing "r"). The picked row ("Reduces an image to a target number of distinct colors using median-cut/k-means for a flat poster or smaller-file look") is exactly image-color-quantize's single capability: Input::Image + one integer param `colors` (min 2, max 256, default 16) that reduces the image to at most N distinct colors, deriving an OPTIMAL palette from the image itself (via NeuQuant), producing a posterized/retro flat look and often a smaller PNG — its descriptor even reads "a posterized / retro look, and often a smaller file". The specific palette-selection algorithm named in the row (median-cut / k-means) is an implementation detail, not a distinct in-model capability: NeuQuant is an image-derived optimal-palette color quantizer that solves the identical problem (map an image's colors to a target-N palette minimizing perceptual error), exposing the identical knob (target color count 2-256) and identical output (PNG). Swapping the internal quantizer would be an enhancement to image-color-quantize, not a parallel block; there is no differentiating parameter, input, or output. Same singular-verb / semantic-near-dup class the exact-slug picker can't catch (slug differs only by a trailing letter). Scaffold not built.

The browser @ffmpeg/core@0.12.10 ships libwebp, so it decodes both lossy AND lossless WebP on the page/chat surface. video-beat-detector  # not viable in current gizza model: beat/tempo detection is DSP ANALYSIS producing DATA (a BPM number + a beat-timestamp array), which neither of gizza's two surfaces can produce. (1) FFMPEG: there is NO beat/tempo/BPM/onset detection filter in ffmpeg -- the only tempo-named audio filter is `atempo`, which CHANGES playback tempo, it does not measure it (grep of `ffmpeg -filters` for beat|tempo|bpm|onset returns only atempo + "temporal" video filters). And the gizza ffmpeg model is argv-only: the wasm block merely constructs an ffmpeg argv and ffmpeg (in the CLI host) writes a single MEDIA output file that is wrapped in a media envelope (see blocks/waveform-image, which builds `-filter_complex showwavespic ...` and emits a PNG). There is NO stage where the wasm reads ffmpeg's decoded PCM back and runs an algorithm, so nothing in the ffmpeg path can emit a BPM/timestamps JSON. (2) PURE-RUST/WASM DSP: honest beat detection needs an onset-detection front end (spectral-flux / energy novelty over an STFT) followed by tempo estimation (autocorrelation or comb-filter of the onset envelope) plus beat-phase tracking -- a real DSP pipeline over decoded audio; but gizza's pure blocks are Input::None with text params and have no audio-bytes-into-wasm decode path (audio input is media/ffmpeg-only), and no wasm-instantiable decode+STFT+tempo-estimation stack ships here. Same DSP/ML-analysis-output-beyond-ffmpeg-filters non-viable class as the skiplisted transcribe/stem-split/autotune audio rows and spectral-subtraction-denoise's "explicit profile/training not exposed by the verified ffmpeg/browser model" note. Revisit only if a wasm-instantiable onset/tempo DSP engine (or an ffmpeg beat filter) lands. Scaffold not built.

ndjson-to-csv  # duplicate of blocks/data-format-converter (same class as the ndjson-to-json and json-to-csv skips): the picked row ("Flattens an NDJSON stream into a CSV with a column per discovered field") is exactly that block's from=ndjson to=csv surface. Its Format enum has a first-class Ndjson variant with the full alias set ("ndjson" | "jsonl" | "json-lines" | "jsonlines") plus from=auto detection, it parses one JSON record per non-blank line with per-line failure reporting, tabulates rows into CSV with the header built as the union of keys discovered across ALL records in first-seen order — the row's "column per discovered field" verbatim, pinned by unit test ndjson_to_csv_unions_keys ("{\"a\":1,\"b\":2}\n{\"a\":3,\"c\":4}" → "a,b,c\n1,2,\n3,,4\n") — and its flatten toggle expands nested objects/arrays into dot-notation columns (json_to_csv_flatten_dot_notation), with headers/delimiter handling shared across the CSV/TSV targets. Same input surface (paste NDJSON text), same engine (serde_json per-line parse → key-union tabulation via records_to_delimited), same CSV output. A dedicated block would be a strict subset — it could add no parameter, accepted form, or verification surface the converter doesn't already ship, and any NDJSON→CSV tweak (e.g. array index columns, custom flatten separator) belongs on data-format-converter itself. Scaffold not built.
json-array-to-jsonl  # duplicate of blocks/data-format-converter: its JSON -> NDJSON path converts a single JSON array into one compact newline-delimited JSON/JSONL record per line (see core::json_array_to_ndjson and Format::Ndjson aliases).

media-probe  # duplicate of blocks/media-info (same class as the extract-video-metadata and audio-info-probe skips — this row is their union): the picked row ("Probe a media file's container, streams, codecs, duration, resolution, bitrate, frame rate, and metadata", "ffprobe-equivalent inspection") is exactly media-info's surface. That block reads an audio or video file's bytes with pure-Rust symphonia demuxers (no decode, no ffmpeg) and reports container format (MP4/M4A/MOV, Matroska/WebM, OGG, WAV, AIFF, CAF, FLAC, MP3, AAC/ADTS), total + per-track duration, overall bitrate, and a per-track stream list — codec (AAC/MP3/FLAC/ALAC/Opus/Vorbis/PCM/ADPCM/…), stream kind (audio/video), sample rate, channel count + layout, bit depth — the ffprobe-style report the row asks for, on the same file→text surface (url/ref input). The row's only deltas — video resolution and frame rate — are the same "minor media-info improvement, not a distinct tool" already ruled on in the extract-video-metadata skip, and container/track metadata tags likewise belong on media-info's symphonia metadata reader. An ffmpeg-based rebuild wouldn't even be in-model for page/chat: a probe's output is TEXT, and runtime=ffmpeg + format=text is hard-rejected by the tool-page generator (tools/generator/assets/runtime/tool.js — see the video-black-frame-timeline skip), so media-info's pure-Rust path IS the in-model implementation, and it exists. Scaffold not built.

That deliverable is video/audio input -> TEXT/JSON diagnostics from ffmpeg, but the current ffmpeg page/runtime only returns one media artifact and hard-rejects runtime=ffmpeg + format=text; chat cannot run ffmpeg. Shipping only media-info-style metadata would duplicate media-info/media-probe, and shipping the real integrity checker would be CLI-only/unpage-verifiable, so no in-model standalone block. Scaffold not built.
find-replace-batch  # duplicate of blocks/find-replace: the picked row ("Applies an ordered list of literal or regex find/replace rules to the text in a single pass") differs from find-replace by exactly ONE delta — BATCH / multi-rule application — and the repo has already adjudicated that delta as a mode on find-replace, not a distinct tool. blocks/find-replace/core find_replace(text, find, replacement, is_regex, case_sensitive, global) already applies a literal OR regex find-and-replace over pasted text with case sensitivity, replace-all vs replace-first, $1/${name} capture-group references in regex mode and verbatim ($ literal) replacement in literal mode, returning the new text plus a match count. The lone capability find-replace-batch adds is running an ORDERED LIST of such rules in one pass — the identical "batch (applying multiple find/replace rules in one pass vs find-replace's single rule)" delta that the already-skiplisted find-replace-with-diff entry ruled "belongs as an added multi-rule mode on the existing find-replace block, not as a distinct tool," citing the established pattern (csv-select's rename delta on csv-reorder-columns, regex-extractor's all-capture-groups delta on regex-extract, url-extractor's bare-URL delta on extract-urls, encoding-converter's Base64URL delta on multi-encoder). Same wasm-safe engine (regex crate), same core transform, no distinct capability beyond looping the existing engine over a list of rules. Scaffold not built.
picture-in-picture-video  # not viable in the current gizza model (multi-input ffmpeg; mirror of the chroma-key-composite-video / video-concat / video-mux-external-audio skips): a webcam-in-corner PiP overlays ONE video as an inset on top of a SEPARATE main video, i.e. TWO distinct video inputs composited at once (ffmpeg `[1:v]scale=...[pip];[0:v][pip]overlay=x:y`). The gizza dispatch + page model accepts exactly ONE uploaded media input: block-utils dispatch_ffmpeg is single-input (ExecArgs is one input file, ExecResult is one Vec<u8>, block-utils/src/ffmpeg.rs) and the page file driver takes a single upload; ffmpeg also cannot run in the chat Service Worker (chat-ffmpeg is page+CLI only), so a two-video composite has no in-model page or chat surface and would be CLI-ONLY, which does not satisfy loop verification. The existing single-input video blocks (video-blur-region, image-split-overlay, etc.) all take exactly one media input; none can supply a second overlay stream through the page/chat surfaces. The scale/position/border of the inset are trivial ffmpeg once a second input exists; the blocker is purely the multi-input surface. Revisit only if the page gains a second file input AND chat gains multi-input ffmpeg. Scaffold not built.

The CSV parse/serialize/rebuild half IS pure Rust, but that half alone is just column projection (already covered by csv-select / csv-insert-column / find-replace); the translation is the entire value and it's the out-of-model half. The headline LOGO-IMAGE half composites a SEPARATE logo image onto the video — ffmpeg's overlay filter is VV->V (two inputs: [0:v] main + [1:v] logo), i.e. multi-input ffmpeg, which the gizza dispatch+page model can't do (block-utils dispatch is single-input, the page takes one upload, and chat SW can't run ffmpeg) — the exact blocker adjudicated in the picture-in-picture-video, video-mux-external-audio and chroma-key-composite-video skips. The remaining TEXT-WATERMARK half is a strict subset of shipped blocks/video-title-card, which draws arbitrary text (bundled bold font, literal — no escaping) at any of 7 positions INCLUDING all four corners (top/bottom x left/right at a fixed 24px MARGIN), with font size 8-400 (= the "scale" control), a CSS/hex text color, and an optional semi-transparent background box (color + opacity), gated to a start/end window that already spans the whole clip when end=duration — so "a text watermark at a chosen corner and scale" is precisely video-title-card with background=false, position=<corner>, font_size=<scale>. Its only genuine delta — OPACITY on the drawn text itself (drawtext alpha=) rather than only on the background box — is a one-param enhancement that belongs on video-title-card, the same one-param-enhancement skip class as html-table-from-csv, find-replace-batch, create-patch and note-action-extractor. Neither half yields a distinct buildable in-model tool. Scaffold not built; revisit the logo half only if the page/chat surface gains multi-input ffmpeg.

dotenv-normalizer  # duplicate of blocks/dotenv-manager (output="normalized"): the picked row ("Sorts, dedupes, groups by prefix, and aligns a .env file, flagging duplicate keys") is exactly dotenv-manager's normalized-output surface. That block parses a pasted .env (KEY=VALUE lines; # comments, blank lines, single/double quotes, inline comments and an 'export ' prefix all handled), dedupes duplicate keys last-wins, optionally sorts keys alphabetically via sort_keys, renders deduped KEY=value lines in its 'normalized' output mode, and its 'report' mode already flags duplicate keys (with the duplicates/missing/lint diagnostic summary). Same input surface (pasted .env text), same output (cleaned/normalized .env), same pure-Rust dotenv parse+dedupe+render engine shared with blocks/dotenv-validator and blocks/env-to-configmap. The row's only deltas — grouping keys by common prefix (a blank line between DB_*/AWS_* groups) and column-aligning the '=' — are cosmetic render options on the normalized output that belong on dotenv-manager itself (same one-param-enhancement class as the already-skiplisted env-template-generator, env-file-parser and env-json-converter dups of dotenv-manager, and the note-action-extractor/create-patch skips), not a separate block duplicating the dotenv parse/dedupe/render engine. Scaffold not built.
sprite-sheet-from-video  # duplicate of blocks/extract-frames (with an out-of-model text-side delta): the picked row ("Extracts evenly spaced frames from a clip and packs them into a single sprite-sheet/atlas image plus a CSS keyframe snippet") is, on its in-model image half, exactly what extract-frames already produces. extract-frames samples video frames at a fixed interval (one frame every N seconds) or a fixed fps — i.e. EVENLY SPACED frames — and tiles them into a single columns x rows grid image (ffmpeg `fps=1/N`/`fps=N`,scale,`tile=CxR:margin:padding:color`), delivered as one PNG/JPG output file. A "sprite-sheet/atlas image" IS that tiled grid of evenly-spaced frames; the sampling engine, the grid packing, the per-thumbnail width, the gap/background color, and the single-image output are all already there. The only two deltas are both non-distinct: (1) TIGHT/zero-gap uniform-cell packing (extract-frames bakes margin=4/padding=2) is at most a gap=0 option on extract-frames' existing tile stage, an added param on the existing block, not a new engine — same "lone delta belongs as a mode on the existing tool" class as the phone-number-extractor/vcard-contact-extractor/webp-to-jpg skips; and (2) the "CSS keyframe snippet" is a TEXT artifact an ffmpeg tool cannot emit in this model — dispatch_ffmpeg reads back exactly one media file (block-utils/src/lib.rs returns only ff.output), and the tool-page generator hard-rejects runtime=ffmpeg + format=text ("ffmpeg runtime needs an image/video/audio output"), so a video-decoding tool cannot deliver a side-channel CSS string on the page or in chat (same out-of-model text-output-from-ffmpeg bar as the video-scene-cut-detector and video-black-frame-timeline skips). Net: the buildable part strictly duplicates extract-frames (already the reason video-thumbnail-extractor and video-to-frames were skiplisted) and the residual CSS-snippet part is out-of-model. Scaffold not built.
jpeg-lossless-rotate  # out-of-model / not viable: the row promises TRUE JPEG lossless rotation by DCT-block transposition with NO re-compression (jpegtran-style), which is a coefficient-domain operation — Huffman-decode the entropy stream to quantized 8x8 DCT blocks, transpose the block grid AND transpose/flip within each block, then re-Huffman-encode — never touching pixels. That requires libjpeg's coefficient API (jpeg_read_coefficients / jtransform_execute_transformation / jpeg_write_coefficients), and NO proven wasm-safe pure-Rust crate exposes it. Evidence from a bounded feasibility scan (2026-07-22): (1) the actual jpegtran engine is `mozjpeg` 0.10 / `mozjpeg-sys` 2.2 — C FFI bindings that compile & statically link libjpeg-turbo via a C toolchain; like every C-library binding (pdfium-render, mupdf class) it will NOT instantiate under wafer's wasmi (needs libc/nasm/cc, pulls unavailable imports). (2) `lepton_jpeg` 0.5 (Microsoft's pure-Rust Lepton port) is the ONLY pure-Rust crate that truly parses baseline/progressive JPEG down to its quantized DCT coefficients, but its public API exposes ONLY whole-file `encode_lepton`/`decode_lepton` (+ `dump_jpeg`) — there is NO per-8x8-block coefficient read/transform/re-emit surface to hang a transpose on — AND it is fundamentally multithreaded (public `LeptonThreadPool`/`SimpleThreadPool` trait + `DEFAULT_THREAD_POOL` static, deps `thread-priority` + `cpu-time`), so even for the parts one could reach it falls in the same non-wasm-threading INSTANTIATE-gate class as the rayon/rawloader skips (no std::thread under the minimal WASI host / no wasm-threads on the browser build). (3) `ljpeg` 0.1 and `pure_jpegli` 2.0 implement the PREDICTIVE lossless-JPEG *format* (ITU-T T.81 Annex H, DICOM Process 14) — a different codec entirely, not a baseline-DCT coefficient transpose; irrelevant to rotating ordinary lossy JPEGs. (4) `mozjpeg-rs`/`oxideav-mjpeg` are pixel-domain encoders/codecs (decode→pixels / pixels→encode), not coefficient transformers. The only remaining path — decode→rotate→re-encode via the `image` crate — RE-COMPRESSES the JPEG (generational quality loss) and is therefore explicitly forbidden as it violates the tool's no-re-compression promise; it is also already exactly what blocks/rotate-image does (decodes to RGBA, rotates pixels, re-encodes). Building this via `image` would ship a tool that LIES about being lossless. Revisit only if a proven wasm-safe, single-threaded pure-Rust crate that reads JPEG DCT coefficients, applies a jpegtran-style block transpose, and re-emits a valid JPEG lands, or if wasmi gains platform-wide wasm-threads and lepton_jpeg exposes a coefficient-transform surface. Scaffold not built.
js-object-to-json  # duplicate of blocks/json-repair: the picked row ("Converts a JavaScript object literal — unquoted keys, single quotes, trailing commas — into valid JSON") is a strict subset of json-repair's tolerant recursive-descent parser, which already normalizes exactly this input class into strict JSON. json-repair's own descriptor lists (and its `json` param example `{'name': 'John', age: 30,}` demonstrates) unquoted keys and values, single/smart quotes, trailing commas, // and /* */ comments, and undefined/NaN/Infinity — i.e. every JS-object-literal affordance the row asks for. Verified live (2026-07-22): `gizza tool json-repair json="{foo: 'bar', baz: [1,2,3,], /* c */ qux: undefined, n: NaN, inf: Infinity}" indent=2` emits strict JSON {"foo":"bar","baz":[1,2,3],"qux":null,"n":null,"inf":null} — unquoted key→quoted, single→double quotes, trailing comma dropped, comment stripped, undefined/NaN/Infinity→null. A dedicated js-object-to-json block would add no engine, parameter, or verification surface json-repair lacks; it is the same pure-Rust syntax-only converter (indent control included: 2/4/tab/minify). The only theoretical delta — coercing JS undefined/NaN/Infinity to those literal JS tokens rather than JSON null — is not valid strict-JSON output anyway (JSON has no such tokens), so null is the correct in-model behavior and json-repair already does it. Same "lone delta belongs on the existing tool" class as the other json-* dup skips. Scaffold not built.
exif-gps-presence-checker  # duplicate of blocks/metadata-privacy-linter: the picked row ("Quickly reports whether an image embeds GPS location coordinates, without revealing the coordinates themselves unless asked") is a GPS-scoped subset of metadata-privacy-linter, which ALREADY does exactly this — including the identical privacy-preserving-redaction UX that is the row's only distinguishing feature. Verified against blocks/metadata-privacy-linter/core/src/lib.rs (2026-07-22): (1) it parses EXIF via kamadak-exif and detects GPS as a Finding with category="location" / risk=High, so "does this image embed GPS coordinates?" is already answered by whether a location finding appears; (2) its Report carries a top-level `clean: bool` ("nothing at/above threshold") and a plain-English `summary` string — i.e. the yes/no "is location present" verdict the presence-checker would emit; (3) crucially it ALREADY hides the coordinates unless asked: the `reveal_values` param defaults to false, Finding.value is `#[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]` and omitted when not revealing, and the decoded `gps: Option<Gps>` (signed decimal degrees) + `gps_map_url` OpenStreetMap link are populated "present only when found AND reveal_values is true", with `values_hidden` flagged — this is precisely "without revealing the coordinates themselves unless asked", the presence-checker's headline behavior, already implemented. A dedicated GPS-only block would add no engine (same kamadak-exif GPS decode), no parameter (same reveal toggle), and no output surface (same present/clean + optional decoded coords + map link) the linter lacks; it would just be metadata-privacy-linter filtered to the location category. Users wanting only the GPS answer read the linter's `clean`/location findings with reveal_values=false. Same "narrower re-slice of an existing tool's existing behavior, lone delta already covered" class as the other subset dup skips. Scaffold not built.

A dedicated wav-to-ogg-vorbis block would add no engine (same libvorbis encoder), no output surface, and no verification path beyond narrowing the source to WAV and fixing format=ogg; the VBR mode belongs as an added quality option on audio-convert itself. Scaffold not built.

video-silence-detector  # duplicate of blocks/video-speech-segment-detector (its segments=non-speech mode): the picked row ("Scans the audio track and outputs a timestamped list of silent segments (start/end/duration) above a chosen threshold") is exactly video-speech-segment-detector's non-speech deliverable. That block runs ffmpeg's `silencedetect` audio filter over the audio track (detect_argv: `-vn -sn -dn -af silencedetect=noise=<threshold_db>dB:d=<min_silence> -f null -`), parses the log's silence_start/silence_end markers via the shared video-silence-cut core (parse_silences/parse_duration/keep_segments), builds a labeled timeline, and with `segments=non-speech` lists ONLY the silent segments — each as start -> end + duration — in the requested `output` format: report (numbered `#n  start -> end  non-speech  D.DDDs` rows plus a "non-speech: N segments, S.SSSs (P%)" summary), csv (verbatim `start,end,duration,label` columns = the row's "start/end/duration" list, pinned by render_csv_exact_and_filtered), srt, or audacity label track. Its `threshold_db` param (default -30 dB, "loudness threshold below which audio counts as non-speech") IS the row's "chosen threshold", and `min_silence` (default 0.5s) is the minimum silent-run length; `voice_band` and `min_speech` only refine which runs qualify. A dedicated silence-detector block would add no engine (same silencedetect pass + same log parser), no parameter, and no output surface the speech-segment-detector's non-speech filter doesn't already ship — it would be a strict re-slice of an existing tool's existing behavior (same "narrower re-slice, lone delta already covered" class as the exif-gps-presence-checker and bip39-mnemonic-validate subset skips). The complementary shipped blocks video-silence-cut (removes silence -> video output) and audio-silence-remove/audio-edge-silence-trimmer cover the cutting side. Scaffold not built.

chord-recognizer  # not viable in gizza's pure-Rust+ffmpeg (no ML) model; audio-INPUT -> TEXT/DATA-OUTPUT music-analysis, same deferred class as the skiplisted chord-detect/key-detect/pitch-detect/bpm-detect and monophonic-audio-to-midi. The deliverable is a time-stamped chord-symbol sequence (major/minor/7th) derived from a decoded recording. Two hard blockers: (1) ANALYSIS engine — ffmpeg only decodes/resamples PCM and has NO filter that emits chroma/pitch-class/chord data to a parseable stderr log, so the proven log-parsing detector pattern (silencedetect/cropdetect/ebur128 -> parse LOG, e.g. video-speech-segment-detector) does NOT apply; recognizing chords needs a pure-Rust chromagram (STFT/FFT) + template-matching engine run over decoded PCM samples. (2) SURFACE — that engine needs decoded PCM that no headless-verifiable pure surface supplies: the only in-browser audio-decode path is the ffmpeg page runtime, which hard-rejects non-media (text) output (tools/generator/assets/runtime/tool.js:496 "ffmpeg runtime needs an image/video/audio output"), and the pure page runtime that CAN emit text has no in-browser audio-decode bridge; chat has no ffmpeg. So the text chord-sequence result has no buildable page/chat surface — same blocker documented for monophonic-audio-to-midi. Revisit only if a decoded-PCM->pure-DSP bridge (or a data-output ffmpeg page mode) lands. Scaffold not built.
big-csv-profiler  # duplicate of blocks/csv-stats: the picked row ("Streams a very large CSV in chunks to compute column stats (count, min/max, mean, nulls, cardinality) without loading it all at once") produces exactly csv-stats's output and cannot realize its only differentiator in gizza's model. csv-stats's core stats(data, header, delimiter) already computes, per column, count (non-empty value count), empty (= the row's "nulls"), distinct (= the row's "cardinality"), and for all-numeric columns min, max, mean and sum, plus the total row count (verified blocks/csv-stats/core/src/lib.rs, ColumnStats { column, kind, count, empty, distinct, min, max, mean, sum } + Stats { columns, rows }, 2026-07-22) — a strict superset of the requested {count, min/max, mean, nulls, cardinality}. The row's sole distinguishing claim is IMPLEMENTATION ("streams in chunks ... without loading it all at once"), which is architecturally impossible for a pure gizza block: the CSV arrives as a single fully-materialized `data: String` param in wasm linear memory (Input::None, no url/ref/file source, no disk/network handle), so there is nothing to stream and no chunked-read surface to expose — the streaming is an internal detail with zero observable API/output difference from csv-stats. A dedicated block would add no engine (same pure-Rust `csv` reader), no parameter (same data/header/delimiter), and no output the existing tool lacks. Same "narrower/identical re-slice, lone delta is a non-observable implementation detail" class as the csv-* subset skips. Scaffold not built.
text-secret-decrypt  # duplicate of blocks/text-encrypt (mode=decrypt): the picked row ("Decrypts an armored password-protected ciphertext block back to readable text") is exactly text-encrypt's decrypt half. That block's descriptor exposes mode=decrypt, whose core::decrypt_text base64-decodes the token, runs encrypt-file-core::decrypt (AES-256-GCM, key derived via PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 from the self-describing magic|salt|nonce|ciphertext+tag blob), and returns the original UTF-8 text; a wrong passphrase or tampered/invalid token fails cleanly. This is the decrypt counterpart of the already-skiplisted text-secret-encrypt dup of the same block, and the sole delta -- accepting an "armored" PEM-style BEGIN/END wrapper -- is a cosmetic rendering of the same base64 ciphertext (strip the header/footer lines), not a distinct capability; per the established one-param-enhancement pattern it belongs as optional armor tolerance on text-encrypt's decrypt path, not as a separate block duplicating the passphrase-AEAD-token decrypt engine. Scaffold not built.
obs-recording-remux  # duplicate of blocks/mkv-to-mp4 (its mode=copy lossless remux): the picked row ("Remuxes an OBS .mkv or .flv recording into a streamable .mp4 without re-encoding") is exactly mkv-to-mp4's copy path. Verified blocks/mkv-to-mp4/core/src/lib.rs (2026-07-22): mode=copy builds `-i <in> -map 0:v? -map 0:a? -c copy -movflags +faststart out.mp4` — a stream-copy container remux with NO re-encode (no libx264/aac), and `+faststart` moves the moov atom to the front for progressive/"streamable" web playback, which is precisely "remux ... into a streamable .mp4 without re-encoding". The module docs already frame this as the headline lossless `-c copy` remux for the MP4-legal-codec case, distinct from video-transcode/video-compress which always re-encode; the transcode fallback is retained for non-MP4-legal codecs. The only nominal deltas are cosmetic and non-distinguishing: (1) the "OBS recording" framing is a use-case label, not a capability — OBS's crash-safe .mkv output remuxed to .mp4 IS the canonical mkv-to-mp4 copy case; (2) the ".flv" source is just another input container — the remux argv is container-agnostic (ffmpeg auto-detects the container from the input regardless of extension, and build_argv never gates on it), and OBS FLV carries MP4-legal H.264 video + AAC audio, so FLV->MP4 is the identical `-c copy -movflags +faststart` stream-copy with no re-encode. A dedicated block would add no engine (same ffmpeg -c copy remux), no parameter (same mode/quality), and no output surface (same faststart out.mp4) mkv-to-mp4 lacks — a narrower source-container/use-case relabel of an existing tool's existing behavior, same class as the skiplisted mts-to-mp4 and webm-to-mp4 source-container dups. Scaffold not built.
glb-gltf-packer  # NOT VIABLE in gizza's current single-input/single-output dispatch model; NOT a duplicate (no existing glTF/GLB block; mesh-convert only does OBJ<->STL over pasted text, no glTF/GLB/textures). The row's function ("Packs separate glTF JSON + bin + textures into a single GLB, or unpacks a GLB back into glTF parts") is inherently multi-file on both directions and both sides are architecturally blocked here: (1) PACK reads SEPARATE binary resources (glTF JSON + .bin + N image files) and fuses them into one GLB — that is multi-FILE input, whose only surface is Param::source_list (the create-zip/merge-pdf/images-to-pdf pattern: Vec<SourceFields>, each a url⊕ref). That surface has NO page (create-zip's own header: "Surfaces: chat + CLI (no page — array input + binary output)") AND every url/ref entry resolves through wafer-run/network — which is BROKEN on this box: reproduced live 2026-07-22 with the committed detect-file-type via url -> "stream_init failed for wafer-run/network" (same runtime failure that skiplisted pdf-links-extract TODAY). block-utils Source is only Url|Ref (lib.rs:132-135) with no local-file path, and the CLI registers no attachment-injection surface, so a `gizza tool glb-gltf-packer files=[...]` exact-output case is unsatisfiable — the tool cannot pass the loop's CLI verification gate. (2) UNPACK takes ONE GLB and must emit MULTIPLE glTF parts (JSON + .bin + textures) — multi-OUTPUT, the unsupported class per the audio-split-on-silence / speech-segmenter skips (envelope/ExecResult is ONE artifact). It could be reframed to a single ZIP output (create-zip-style), but its INPUT is a binary GLB, again requiring Input::File url/ref -> the same broken wafer-run/network, with no page (binary upload). The two directions also need incompatible input surfaces (multi-file source_list for pack vs single-file for unpack), so a single dual-mode block is architecturally awkward on top of being unverifiable. The only network-FREE reduction — a pure paste-text glTF<->GLB converter over a SELF-CONTAINED .gltf whose buffers/images are already base64 data-URIs (parse JSON, decode data-URIs, write GLB; and back) — does NOT match this row: it cannot "pack SEPARATE bin + textures" (they must already be embedded) and unpack yields ONE embedded .gltf, not "glTF parts"; and the GLB side is binary so unpack input would need a base64 paste no competitor uses (the same "divorce from the intended URL/attachment model" the pdf-links-extract skiplist rejects). Building that narrower converter under this slug would mislabel it, failing the honesty gate. Revisit only when a multi-file-input surface with a page (or a working local-file/wafer-run runtime on the build box) lands so the separate-files pack/unpack can be faithfully built and verified. Scaffold not built.

duplicate-track-finder  # not viable in current gizza model: the tool's defining capability is detecting duplicate SONGS by "audio similarity" — i.e. same recording across different encodings/bitrates/sample-rates/loudness. That is acoustic fingerprinting (Chromaprint/AcoustID-class: resample→chroma-feature FFT filterbank→robust invariant hash→matcher) or an ML audio embedding — feature-extraction/model class, out-of-model (gizza is pure-Rust + ffmpeg, no ML loader; no proven wasm-safe chromaprint engine in references). The in-model pieces (tags + duration read via symphonia metadata; multi-file input via Param::source_list; raw/envelope PCM cross-correlation à la video-audio-sync-offset-finder) do NOT cover it: cross-correlation finds a time-offset between ALIGNED takes, it is not encode/loudness/rate-invariant same-song detection and is O(N^2) pairwise across a library. Shipping only tag+duration dedup would be a metadata deduper (csv-dedupe/vcard-deduplicate class), not the described audio-similarity duplicate finder — the core deliverable would be missing. Not a duplicate of an existing block (duplicate-image-finder is dHash over images). Revisit only if an in-model acoustic-fingerprint backend exists.
jwt-decoder  # duplicate of blocks/jwt-decode (+ blocks/jwt-verify for the HMAC path): the picked row ("Decodes a JWT into its header and payload, shows expiry/issued times, and optionally verifies an HMAC signature") is the exact union of two existing blocks and adds no capability, parameter, or surface either lacks. Verified 2026-07-23: blocks/jwt-decode/core/src/lib.rs already "decode[s] a compact JWT string and validate[s] time-based claims offline" — split_token parses the 2/3-segment compact JWS into header + payload (both base64url-decoded to serde_json Values on DecodeResult.header/.payload), and claim_i64 pulls exp/nbf/iat, emitting the time-based checks that surface "expiry/issued times" (DecodeResult.checks); it also already handles the signature_present flag and rejects 5-segment JWE. That covers the row's entire "decode into header and payload, shows expiry/issued times" scope verbatim. The sole remaining delta — "optionally verifies an HMAC signature" — is precisely blocks/jwt-verify's job: its core decodes the same compact JWT and verifies the signature against a shared HMAC secret (HS256/384/512, plus RS256/384/512 and ES256/384), with an expected_alg guard against alg-confusion downgrade and full exp/nbf/iat/iss/aud claim checks — a strict superset of "optionally verify HMAC". So every advertised feature of jwt-decoder is already shipped: decode+times by jwt-decode, HMAC verification by jwt-verify (with jwt-sign and jwt-claims-diff rounding out the family). A dedicated jwt-decoder block would re-implement jwt-decode's base64url header/payload split and time-claim reporting and bolt on jwt-verify's HMAC path under a new slug, duplicating two engines while adding nothing — the classic union-of-existing-tools dup. Merging an optional HMAC-verify toggle onto jwt-decode (or pointing users at jwt-verify) is the in-model path, not a separate block. Scaffold not built.
ts-to-mp4  # duplicate of blocks/mkv-to-mp4 (its mode=copy lossless remux): the picked row ("Converts an MPEG transport stream (.ts) capture into a seekable MP4 by stream-copying into the new container") is exactly mkv-to-mp4's copy path. Verified blocks/mkv-to-mp4/core/src/lib.rs (2026-07-23): build_argv(Mode::Copy) emits `-i <in> -map 0:v? -map 0:a? -c copy -movflags +faststart out.mp4` — a stream-copy container remux with NO re-encode, container-agnostic (it takes in_name/out_name and NEVER gates on the .mkv extension), and `+faststart` moves the moov atom to the front, i.e. the "seekable MP4" this row asks for. ffmpeg auto-detects the input container from content (MPEG-TS is auto-detected regardless of extension), and when muxing ADTS AAC from a TS into MP4 modern ffmpeg auto-inserts the `aac_adtstoasc` bitstream filter, so `-c copy` alone remuxes a .ts (H.264/HEVC video + AAC audio) into a faststart MP4 identically to an .mkv source. A dedicated ts-to-mp4 block would add no engine (same ffmpeg -c copy remux), no parameter (same copy/transcode + quality surface — mkv-to-mp4 already keeps a libx264/aac transcode fallback for non-MP4-legal codecs), and no output surface (same +faststart out.mp4) beyond narrowing the source container/use-case label from Matroska to transport-stream. Same strict-subset source-container-relabel class as the already-skiplisted obs-recording-remux (mkv-to-mp4 copy dup, incl. .flv/OBS source) and mts-to-mp4 (transport-stream source) rows. Scaffold not built.

Verified 2026-07-23: image-convert takes Input::Image via resolve_source(AssetKind::Image) and transcodes to a chosen target — `format=png` emits the lossless PNG this row asks for (build_argv omits -q:v for PNG). ffmpeg auto-detects the input container from content, and the local ffmpeg (7.1.4, V..... libdav1d) decodes AVIF (AV1-in-ISOBMFF) fine — verified a red 8x8 .avif round-trips to PNG via `ffmpeg -i red.avif out.png` — so image-convert's CLI already does avif→png with no new engine, parameter, or output surface, only a narrowed source-format label (same source-relabel dup class as the ogg/wma/wav→mp3 audio-convert skips). A distinct value would be a PURE all-surface (incl. chat) implementation, but AVIF decode has NO wasm-safe pure-Rust decoder: image `avif-native`, avif-decode, libavif, and dav1d all pull C AV1 libs (dav1d-sys / libaom-sys / libdav1d-sys) that need clang + a cross sysroot and fail `cargo build --target wasm32-wasip1` (dav1d-sys build.rs panics: "pkg-config has not been configured to support cross-compilation"; libaom-sys: "failed to find tool clang"). And the ffmpeg page path can't cover it either — the page's stock @ffmpeg/core (jsDelivr) ships WITHOUT libdav1d, so it can't decode AVIF in-browser. Net: the only working avif→png in gizza's model is image-convert's CLI/local path; a dedicated block adds nothing and its page couldn't decode AVIF. Scaffold not built.

video-silence-segment-finder  # duplicate of blocks/video-speech-segment-detector (its segments=non-speech mode; same class as the already-skiplisted video-silence-detector).
