video-loudnorm-ebur128  # not viable in the current gizza model: its defining feature — TWO-PASS EBU R128 measurement — cannot run on the gizza ffmpeg bridge. Verified 2026-07-23. True loudnorm two-pass requires an analysis pass (`-af loudnorm=...:print_format=json -f null -`), parsing the emitted measured_I/measured_TP/measured_LRA/measured_thresh/target_offset JSON, then a SECOND apply pass that feeds those measured values back into loudnorm for accurate linear normalization. But the gizza bridge is exactly one `build_argv -> ffmpegExec` per call with NO state persisted between calls (documented verbatim in blocks/video-target-filesize-encoder/core: "the gizza ffmpeg bridge is one build_argv -> ffmpegExec per call with no passlog persisted across calls, so true two-pass VBR is out of model"), and the web `build_argv` returns a single `ArgvPlan { argv, out_name }` = one ffmpeg invocation on both the page and CLI surfaces — there is no way to run the measurement pass, parse its JSON on the host, and thread the result into a second pass. This is precisely why the existing blocks/audio-normalize implements EBU R128 loudnorm as SINGLE-pass (`loudnorm=I={lufs}:TP=-1.5:LRA=11`) and documents it as "single pass". A single-pass fallback would (a) drop the tool's whole reason to exist (two-pass accuracy is in its name and description) and (b) be a video-wrapped near-duplicate of audio-normalize's single-pass loudnorm applied to a video's audio track (the video-in/keep-picture wrapper is already the pattern of video-audio-gain and video-audio-compress-dynamics). Out of model on the distinguishing feature; degenerate-to-duplicate without it. Scaffold not built.
video-audio-peak-normalize  # semantic duplicate of blocks/normalize-peak AND out-of-model on the video path (two-pass). Verified 2026-07-23. (1) DUPLICATE: normalize-peak's entire function is exactly this row — "Sample-peak normalization: find the loudest single sample in a file (its sample peak, in dBFS) and apply ONE constant gain so that peak lands exactly on a chosen target dBFS. Dynamics are untouched" (summary "Normalize an audio file to a target sample peak (dBFS)"), with a target dBFS param (-60..0, default -1.0), linked/per-channel gain, DC-offset removal, and int/float output. It MEASURES the peak (symphonia decode → loudest sample → exact gain via hound) entirely in pure Rust because that path never touches ffmpeg. The picked row's only delta is a VIDEO container instead of an audio file (keep the picture, normalize the audio track) — the same video-wrapper-of-an-audio-tool pattern that already skiplisted video-loudnorm-ebur128. (2) OUT OF MODEL on that video delta: peak-normalizing a video's audio to an arbitrary ceiling requires TWO passes — measure the global max sample (ffmpeg `volumedetect`/`astats` → max_volume dB), parse it on the host, then a SECOND `volume={ceiling-max}dB` apply pass. The gizza ffmpeg bridge is one `build_argv → ffmpegExec` per call with NO state persisted between calls (blocks/video-target-filesize-encoder/core: "one build_argv → ffmpegExec per call with no passlog persisted across calls"), and web `build_argv` returns a single `ArgvPlan { argv, out_name }` = one ffmpeg invocation on both page and CLI — there is no way to run the measurement pass, read its output, and thread it into a second apply pass. The only single-pass ffmpeg alternatives are dishonest: a fixed-dB gain (blocks/video-audio-gain) does NOT hit a measured ceiling, and dynamic normalizers (dynaudnorm/speechnorm) CHANGE dynamics, which the description explicitly forbids ("without changing dynamics"). Duplicate on the audio path, out-of-model on the video path. Scaffold not built.

audio-frequency-band-isolator  # duplicate of blocks/audio-filter: the picked row ("Isolates a single frequency band with a band-pass filter to extract or solo a sound") is exactly audio-filter's `filter_type=bandpass` surface. Verified against blocks/audio-filter/core: it supports lowpass/highpass/bandpass/notch, and band-pass mode emits ffmpeg `bandpass=f=<frequency>:width_type=h:w=<width>` with `frequency` as band center and `width` in Hz, returning an audio file with only that band passed. The "bass" / "sibilance" examples are just different center+width settings, not a distinct engine or output surface. A dedicated isolator would add no parameter beyond relabelling audio-filter's existing filter_type/frequency/width controls. Scaffold not built.

Verified against blocks/extract-dates manifest/page: it scans arbitrary text for ISO dates/datetimes, numeric dates, month-name dates, and clock times; returns each original match with normalized ISO 8601 value and kind (`date`/`datetime`/`time`) in order of appearance. A dedicated timestamp-extractor would add no parser, parameter, or output surface beyond a generic relabel of the existing date/time extractor. Scaffold not built.
har-secret-scanner  # duplicate of blocks/har-redact (its output=summary dry-run mode). Verified 2026-07-24. The picked row ("Scans a .har capture for leaked auth tokens, cookies, and API keys so they can be redacted before the file is shared") is exactly har-redact's scan/report path. har-redact/core is HAR-structure-aware: it walks log.entries and detects the same three secret classes the row names — cookie material (request/response cookies[].value + Cookie/Set-Cookie header values), auth/API-key/token header values (built-in AUTH_HEADERS list: authorization, x-api-key, x-auth-token, x-access-token, x-session-token, x-amz-security-token, csrf/xsrf, ... + user extra_headers), and sensitive query/form token params (built-in SENSITIVE_PARAMS: token/access_token/refresh_token/api_key/client_secret/secret/jwt/bearer/session/sid/... + user sensitive_params) plus request/response bodies. Its `output = "summary"` mode is documented in-core as "a dry-run report of per-category counts" and emits "HAR redaction summary / entries scanned: N / cookies redacted: N / auth/api-key headers redacted: N / query-string values redacted: N / ..." WITHOUT modifying the file — i.e. it already scans a HAR for leaked auth tokens/cookies/API keys and reports what was found so they can be redacted before sharing, and the same tool then does the redaction itself (output=har). A dedicated scanner adds no engine (same HAR walker + same auth/cookie/param detection lists), no new secret class, and no output surface har-redact's summary doesn't already ship; generic provider-prefix/entropy secret detection over arbitrary text is separately covered by blocks/secret-scanner (AWS AKIA/GitHub ghp_/Stripe sk_live_/JWT/PEM/keyword+entropy). Same scan-and-report surface, not a distinct capability. Scaffold not built.

ocr-to-hocr  # not viable in current gizza model: its whole function is running OCR (optical character recognition) — recognizing glyphs in an uploaded image's pixels and emitting the recognized words with per-word bounding boxes as hOCR/ALTO. Verified 2026-07-24: this requires a trained OCR/text-recognition ML model. Pure-Rust WASM options are all out-of-model for a gizza block: `tesseract`/`leptess` wrap the native Tesseract C library (not wasm32-wasip1-safe) and need ~tens-of-MB per-language traineddata; `ocrs` is pure Rust but runs a text-detection + recognition neural net via the `rten` runtime and requires shipping tens of MB of .rten model weights bundled into the wasm — inappropriate for a small self-contained block and its web page. The hOCR/ALTO output format itself is trivial XML serialization, but it is meaningless without the ML recognition step that produces the words and their coordinates; there is no non-ML heuristic that reads arbitrary rasterized text. Same ML-model class the skill/page-patterns reference lists as staying skipped (transcribe/whisper/transformers-js). Scaffold not built.

No non-ML heuristic reads arbitrary rasterized text, and no OCR-capable block exists to feed the extractor. Same ML-model class the skill lists as staying skipped. Scaffold not built.

With header=true (default for JSON object mode) it uses the first row/header cells as object keys and serializes later rows as a JSON array of row objects; with header=false it emits arrays. The picked row's exact function — "Parses an HTML <table> into a JSON array of row objects keyed by header cells" — is already html-table-extractor's `extract(html, index, Format::Json, header=true)` path, with CSV as an extra output. The picked row's function — parse the first/all table elements in pasted HTML and output CSV — is covered by this existing table extractor's CSV mode and table-index selection.

aes-gcm-decrypt  # duplicate of blocks/aes-cipher (its Mode::Gcm decrypt path). Verified 2026-07-24 against blocks/aes-cipher/core: decrypt(ciphertext, key, nonce, Mode::Gcm, fmt) already decrypts AES-256-GCM (32-byte key -> Aes256, one of its 128/192/256 sizes) given the raw key and 12-byte nonce, using RustCrypto aes_gcm::AesGcm's AEAD decrypt which verifies the 16-byte authentication tag and rejects tampered input ("decryption failed (wrong key/nonce or tampered data)", asserted in gcm_detects_tampering). The picked row's function — "Decrypts AES-256-GCM ciphertext given the key, nonce and tag, verifying integrity before output" — is exactly that path. Its only delta is supplying the 16-byte GCM tag as a SEPARATE field instead of appended to the ciphertext (aes-cipher documents "the 16-byte authentication tag is appended to the ciphertext"); that is a trivial ct||tag concatenation of the input, not a distinct engine, key size, parameter meaning, integrity check, or output surface. A dedicated block adds no capability. Scaffold not built.
av1-video-encoder  # not viable in current gizza model: no AV1 ENCODER in the browser @ffmpeg/core wasm build the page/tests use. Verified 2026-07-24 by probing @ffmpeg/core@0.12.10 (pinned in js/ffmpeg.js) ffmpeg-core.wasm: its embedded configure line is `--enable-gpl --enable-libx264 --enable-libx265 --enable-libvpx --enable-libmp3lame --enable-libtheora --enable-libvorbis --enable-libopus --enable-zlib --enable-libwebp --enable-libfreetype --enable-libfribidi --enable-libass --enable-libzimg` — no --enable-libaom, --enable-libsvtav1 or --enable-librav1e, and FFmpeg ships no native/built-in AV1 encoder (AV1 output requires one of those external libs, or unavailable-in-wasm hardware encoders av1_nvenc/qsv/amf). The binary's AV1 strings are all DECODE-side only (AV1 decoder, cbs_av1, av1_frame_merge/split bsf, av1_metadata, AV1 Annex B demuxer); grep for any AV1 encoder symbol (libaom-av1/libsvtav1/librav1e/av1_nvenc/aom_codec/SVT-AV1) returns nothing. The picked tool's function is to RE-ENCODE video TO AV1 (WebM/MP4), so any `-c:v libaom-av1`/`libsvtav1` invocation fails "Unknown encoder", meaning the mandatory ffmpeg page-spec "assert real media output (probe codec=av1)" gate and CLI exact-output case cannot be honestly satisfied. This is the page-unverifiable libx264/libx265-only codec case ops.md calls out for skiplisting. Existing efficient-re-encode blocks (video-transcode webm=libvpx-vp9, video-compress/video-to-h264=libx264) already cover the shrink-via-re-encode need with the codecs that ARE compiled in. Scaffold not built.

xpath-extractor  # duplicate of blocks/xpath-query. Verified 2026-07-25 against blocks/xpath-query/{src,core/src}/lib.rs: xpath-query already evaluates an XPath 1.0 expression (sxd-xpath + sxd-document, pure-Rust, runs in chat SW/CLI/page) against a pasted XML/XHTML document and returns the matched nodes IN DOCUMENT ORDER as a list — its response is {outputs: Vec<String>, count} where `outputs` is exactly a JSON list of matched-node results, with output=value (each node's string value) or output=xml (serialized outer XML), and scalar XPath results (count()/name()/boolean) collapse to a single-element list. That is precisely the picked row's function — "Runs an XPath query against an XML/HTML document and returns the matched nodes." The remaining framing delta, non-well-formed HTML input, is not a distinct in-model tool: sxd-xpath requires well-formed XML/XHTML (which xpath-query already advertises and parses), there is no pure-Rust wasm-instantiable XPath engine over an html5ever/rcdom HTML5 DOM, and arbitrary-HTML scraping is separately covered by blocks/css-select-extract, blocks/html-extract, and blocks/html-table-extractor. The low-level ffmpeg exec bridge's ExecArgs.inputs IS a Vec<(String,Vec<u8>)> (block-utils/src/ffmpeg.rs:104-107), but nothing above it can SUPPLY the second (image) input, and ffmpeg cannot run in the chat Service Worker (chat-ffmpeg is page+CLI only), so a two-input embed could only run CLI-side -- which does not satisfy the loop's page/query-param + Playwright verification gate. Exactly the multi-input ffmpeg case documented as un-buildable in create-next-tool/references/page-patterns.md ("Multi-input ffmpeg ... the page file-input is a single upload and ffmpeg can't run in the chat SW, so it'd be CLI-only -- skiplist + defer"). Same architecture-fit class as video-concat / video-ab-side-by-side / video-mux-external-audio / chroma-key-composite-video / video-crop-difference-detector. NOT a duplicate: no existing block attaches an arbitrary chosen image as a video's cover art (blocks/youtube-thumbnail-generator RENDERS a thumbnail image, blocks/video-remove-embedded-subs strips streams, mp4/mkv container-convert blocks copy streams) -- but the capability is blocked purely by the single-media-input architecture. Revisit only if the page gains a second file input AND a multi-source ffmpeg dispatch surface lands. Scaffold not built.
uniform-border-detector  # duplicate of blocks/content-trim-bounds-detector. Verified 2026-07-27 against blocks/content-trim-bounds-detector/{src,core/src}/lib.rs: that pure-Rust image block already auto-detects the solid uniform border color by voting the 4 corner pixels (detect_background, core:117-131, ImageMagick -trim convention), scans each edge row/column inward while >= background_percent% of its pixels match that color within `tolerance`, and reports the per-side thickness as trim_left/trim_top/trim_right/trim_bottom (core:290-293) plus the border `background` color, a `needs_trim`/has-border presence flag, and the enclosed content box. That IS precisely the picked row's function -- "detects a solid uniform frame/border around an image and measures its thickness on each side": same single image input, same corner-color uniform-border model, same per-side thickness + color output. A uniform-border-detector would add no distinct engine (identical edge-scan-vs-corner-color), no new input, and no new verifiable output surface -- only reframing "trim margin" as "border/frame" and a symmetry boolean trivially derivable from the four already-emitted trim_* values. Same near-dup class as pdf-to-text ~= pdf-extract-text. The sibling blocks/image-trim actually CROPS the border and blocks/image-border-frame ADDS one; content-trim-bounds-detector is the MEASURE-only detector. Scaffold not built.
registry-artifact-ripper  # not viable in the current gizza model: no LICENSE-COMPATIBLE wasm-safe Windows registry (regf) hive parser exists. The two pure-Rust hive crates that DO instantiate under wasm32-wasip1 are both copyleft -- `nt-hive` 0.3.0 is GPL-2.0-or-later and `nt_hive2` 4.2.4 is GPL-3.0 (verified: `nt-hive` builds clean for the target, but its license would force this MIT-licensed public toolkit block -- compiled into a cdylib -- under the GPL, which every existing forensic block deliberately avoids: `evtx` (evtx-parser) is MIT/Apache, browser-artifact-parser reuses the in-repo MIT sqlite reader). Hand-rolling a faithful regf parser from scratch (base block + hbin + nk/vk/sk + lf/lh/li/ri leaf/index cells + inline-vs-db_ big-data value reading) PLUS a shellbag ITEMIDLIST/ShellItem decoder is a large, binary-format-exact undertaking, and with NO sample hive fixtures available in this environment it cannot be verified here -- a silently-miswritten forensic extractor is worse than none. Same "the only ready engine is unusable and the faithful hand-built form is too large/unverifiable in-model" class as the registry-free forensic skips. Revisit if a permissively-licensed wasm-safe hive crate lands. Scaffold not built.

audio-speed-pitch-shifter  # duplicate of blocks/audio-time-stretch + blocks/audio-pitch-shift (union of two existing single-axis tools). Verified 2026-07-29 against both cores. The picked row's function -- "Changes playback speed and/or pitch INDEPENDENTLY, e.g. slow-down for practice or transpose a key" -- decomposes exactly into the two dedicated blocks that already give independent control of each axis: (1) "slow-down for practice" = change SPEED while keeping pitch = blocks/audio-time-stretch, whose atempo_chain(factor) is a WSOLA time-stretch (`atempo=` filters, product == factor, decomposed into 0.5..=2.0 instances) scaling tempo 0.25x..4x with pitch preserved; (2) "transpose a key" = change PITCH while keeping duration = blocks/audio-pitch-shift, whose build_filter(semitones) does the classic `asetrate=WORK_RATE*2^(semitones/12)`,`aresample`,`atempo=` resample-trick to shift +/-24 semitones with the duration held exact. Both single-axis operations already exist as focused tools; a combined "speed and/or pitch" block adds NO new engine (it would just chain the same asetrate/atempo filters both blocks already build), no new input surface, and no new verifiable output -- its only delta is applying both filters in one pass, a convenience wrapper whose two headline use cases are each fully served by a dedicated block. Same union-of-existing-tools dup class as the markdown-formatter (markdown-lint fix + markdown-table-format) skip. Scaffold not built.
epub-reader  # duplicate of blocks/epub-extract. Verified 2026-07-29 against epub-extract/core/src/lib.rs + descriptor. The picked row's function -- "Opens an EPUB and renders its chapters as readable text directly in chat" -- is exactly what epub-extract already delivers: it reads the EPUB ZIP's META-INF/container.xml -> OPF spine for reading order, resolves each chapter's title from the NCX/EPUB3 nav TOC (with heading-detection fallback), strips each spine XHTML document to plain readable text via nanohtml2text, and returns an ordered per-chapter structure (index, title, word count, and readable plain text) plus book metadata -- pure-Rust (zip, quick-xml, nanohtml2text) so it runs in the chat Service Worker. That IS "render its chapters as readable text in chat." epub-extract even exposes include_text (default true) for the full readable body and include_text=false for a structure-only table of contents, plus max_chapters paging. A "reader" block adds no new engine, input surface, or verifiable output over epub-extract; the only nearby sibling, blocks/epub-to-markdown, covers the whole-book single-blob Markdown/text case. Note epub-extract is intentionally CLI+chat only (no standalone page) because a binary EPUB file input with structured JSON output fits neither the pure-text page nor the ffmpeg file->media page shape -- the same no-page file-input pattern as epub-to-markdown / pdf-extract-text -- so a page-bearing epub-reader is not buildable in the current gizza page model either. Same reader-of-an-already-extracted-format dup class. Scaffold not built.

A dedicated amr-to-mp3 block would add only an SEO landing surface, not new capability. Scaffold not built.

Monkey's Audio decode is native in the bundled ffmpeg: `ffmpeg -decoders` shows `A....D ape  Monkey's Audio` and `ffmpeg -formats` shows the `D ape` demuxer, so a `.ape` upload flows straight through audio-convert format=flac with no new engine, input surface, or verifiable output. audio-convert's own description states any input ffmpeg can decode works, and FLAC is one of its Format enum targets. A dedicated ape-to-flac block would add only an SEO landing surface, not new capability. Same lossless-source -> FLAC converter dup class as the already-skiplisted flac-to-ogg-vorbis, and the same single-input->single-output-format converter class as amr-to-mp3 (# duplicate of blocks/audio-convert) and image-to-webp (# duplicate of blocks/image-convert). AMR-NB/AMR-WB decode is native in the bundled ffmpeg (decoders amrnb/amrwb + the `amr` demuxer -- already confirmed via `ffmpeg -decoders`/`-formats` in the amr-to-mp3 skip 2026-07-29), so a `.amr` upload flows straight through audio-convert format=wav with no `-ar` (original sample rate preserved) and no new engine, input surface, or verifiable output. Same AMR-source dup class as the already-skiplisted amr-to-mp3, and the same "decode ANY input to WAV via pcm_s16le" subset class as mp3-to-wav-decoder / m4a-to-wav / flac-to-wav-decoder. checksum-compare  # duplicate: no distinct in-model surface over blocks/verify-checksum + blocks/hash-text + blocks/text-diff. Verified 2026-07-29 against verify-checksum/core/src/lib.rs, hash-text/core/src/lib.rs, and text-diff/core/src/lib.rs. The picked row's function -- "Hashes two inputs with a chosen algorithm and reports whether they match" -- is the composition hash-text(input_b) -> verify-checksum(input_a, that digest): verify-checksum already hashes ONE data input (text, or hex/base64-decoded) and compares it against an expected checksum digest across md5/sha1/sha224/sha256/sha384/sha512/sha3-256/sha3-512/blake2b-512/blake2s-256/blake3 (auto-detects the algorithm by digest width, tolerates 0x/case/whitespace/hex-or-base64, and reports MATCH/MISMATCH with both the expected and actual digests). checksum-compare merely swaps the expected-digest operand for a second raw data input that gets hashed first -- a fusion of two focused blocks (same class as the skiplisted key-format-convert / password-hash / html-to-pdf), not a new capability. Critically, in gizza's buildable model the two inputs are pasted TEXT strings (there is no two-file upload slot -- cf. the dir-checksum-report / merge-pdf / csv-merge "a multi-file report needs more than one upload slot" no-page notes), and for two fully-visible text inputs "do their hashes match" is exactly "are the two inputs equal (byte-for-byte, or after an optional trim/lowercase normalize)" -- hashing data you can already see adds nothing over directly comparing it, which blocks/text-diff already does (LCS line diff of left/right that reports whether and where they differ). The whole value of a checksum is fingerprinting data you CANNOT directly compare (large/remote/binary files); that file-vs-file compare is the only surface where hashing-to-compare beats direct comparison, and it needs two upload slots the model does not provide. So every framing collapses onto an existing block: data-vs-known-digest = verify-checksum; data-vs-data text = text-diff/direct equality; data-vs-data binary = not buildable. Scaffold not built.

Its entire value is the live drawing loop: click/drag a pen across grid cells, bucket-fill regions, pick palette colors, and see the sprite update per stroke. gizza's page surface is a single static file upload + text/number form fields with NO interactive pixel-grid or drawing canvas (the generator's only canvas use in runtime/tool.js is copying a rendered OUTPUT <img> to the clipboard, not an input drawing surface), and chat/CLI are non-interactive text surfaces, so per-cell pen/fill mouse input cannot be captured on ANY gizza surface. The only in-model residue would be a headless "render a text-described grid of palette indices to a scaled PNG sprite" function, but (a) that is a DIFFERENT tool from the picked interactive editor (the drawing -- its whole point -- is dropped), and (b) image-bytes pure-Rust output is chat+CLI only with NO page (the color-palette-extract/image-info F3 no-page pattern; cf. the built blocks/image-to-pixel-art, which is likewise image-in/PNG-out with no page), so the loop's mandatory page/query-param + Playwright gate could not be satisfied. blocks/image-to-pixel-art already owns the non-interactive "pixels -> PNG sprite" intent from the image-input side. format-code  # duplicate of blocks/code-formatter (the existing multi-language auto-detect beautifier). Verified 2026-07-30 against code-formatter/core/src/lib.rs + src/lib.rs. The picked row -- "Auto-format source code (JS/TS, JSON, CSS, Markdown, etc.) to a consistent style" -- is precisely code-formatter's function: one pure-Rust beautifier that takes a code snippet, auto-detects the language (or accepts language=html|css|javascript|json), and re-indents it to a consistent style with a configurable indent (1-8 spaces or tab). Its JavaScript path reuses the token-aware js-beautify pretty-printer (so TS/JS re-whitespacing is already covered), JSON is validated + re-serialized with key order preserved, and CSS/HTML use forgiving tokenizers -- "nothing is renamed, reordered or dropped; only whitespace/line breaks change," i.e. exactly "to a consistent style." The specific languages the row names beyond code-formatter's set are each already shipped as dedicated blocks: CSS/SCSS/LESS = blocks/format-css (pretty-print with optional sorting), JSON = blocks/json-beautify, JS = blocks/js-beautify, and Markdown tables = blocks/markdown-table-format. A generic format-code block would add no new engine, input surface, option, or verifiable output over code-formatter and the language-specific formatters -- only an SEO landing surface. Same "general tool duplicates the existing multi-format tool" dup class. Scaffold not built.

The picked row's whole value is an INTERACTIVE in-page browser -- "navigable tree" + click-to-preview "inline preview of text and images" -- i.e. live UI state (select a tree node, preview pane updates). gizza's page surface is a single static file-upload + form-field form rendering ONE static output; there is no interactive tree/preview control on ANY surface (page/CLI/chat are non-interactive), the same not-viable-in-surfaces class as the skiplisted pixel-art-editor and find-replace-with-diff interactive gates. The non-interactive residue is already fully shipped: blocks/zip-inspect lists every entry with full path, per-file uncompressed/compressed size, method, CRC-32, ratio, is_dir, encrypted flag and modified time (the tree + per-file size), and blocks/unzip extracts each file returning inline `text` when it decodes as UTF-8 and base64 `data` for binary/images (the inline text+image preview content), both within bounded budgets. A zip-explorer block would add no new engine, input, option, or verifiable output over zip-inspect + unzip -- only the interactive browsing UI, which no gizza surface can produce. Scaffold not built.

The picked deliverable -- "Fetch a YouTube video's captions/transcript by URL or ID" -- has as its whole value a live network round-trip to youtube.com: fetch the watch page, parse `ytInitialPlayerResponse` for the timedtext caption-track URL, then GET the consent/signature/`pot`-token-gated timedtext endpoint (a private API that changes often and blocks non-browser clients). That is not deterministic local compute. It also has NO buildable page/chat surface: the browser page runtime's only network path is `?url=` loading a remote MEDIA file into the file input (tools/generator/assets/runtime/tool.js loadUrlIntoFile), which explicitly warns the host "may block cross-origin access" -- a browser fetch to youtube.com/timedtext is CORS-blocked, and the chat surface has no network at all, so the mandatory page/query-param + Playwright verification gate cannot be satisfied. The type_hint=ffmpeg is a picker false-positive: ffmpeg.wasm has no YouTube downloader (no yt-dlp) and captions are not a transcode. The SSRF-guarded CLI fetch does single public GETs with no redirect/cookie/consent handling, insufficient for YouTube's caption flow, and CLI-only would not satisfy the page gate regardless. Same live-external-service / not-viable-in-surfaces class as the other out-of-model rows. NOT a duplicate of blocks/youtube-thumbnail-generator (that is a pure-ffmpeg frame grabber on an uploaded video, it does not fetch from YouTube) nor of blocks/transcript-clean / srt-* (those operate on user-provided subtitle text). image-color-replace  # duplicate of blocks/image-bg-replace (solid mode) OR not viable as a page tool. Verified 2026-07-30 against image-bg-replace/core/src/lib.rs and the page runtime. The picked row -- "Replace one color (within a tolerance) with another throughout an image" -- has two realizations, and neither yields a distinct, page-verifiable tool. (1) The only realization that can render raster output on a PAGE is the ffmpeg runtime (confirmed: every image-transform block with a page/meta.toml uses runtime="ffmpeg"; tools/generator/assets/runtime/tool.js renders media output ONLY via the ffmpeg file-input path, and every pure-Rust `image`-crate image-in/image-out block -- blur-image, image-false-color, image-hsl-adjust, image-opacity, sharpen-image, image-pixelate-censor, image-to-pixel-art, etc. -- is chat+CLI only with NO page). The ffmpeg realization is a strict subset of image-bg-replace's bg_type=solid: its filter `split[a][b];[b]colorkey=0xRRGGBB:similarity:blend[fg];[a]format=rgb24,lutrgb=r=R:g=G:b=B[bg];[bg][fg]overlay` keys every pixel within `similarity` (the tolerance) of key_color and floods them with bg_color -- i.e. replace key_color, within tolerance, with bg_color, throughout the image. key_color=target, similarity=tolerance, bg_color=replacement, plus a `blend` soft-edge control, a transparent-replacement mode (bg_type=transparent), a gradient mode, png/jpg/webp output, and parse_color already accepting short #RGB/bare RGB, long #RRGGBB/RRGGBB, 0x-prefixed, and named colors -- every table-stake (tolerance, short+long hex, alpha/transparent replacement, formats) is already shipped. An eyedropper/interactive color-pick is out-of-model (no interactive image canvas on any gizza surface -- same class as the pixel-art-editor/zip-explorer skips). A dedicated ffmpeg image-color-replace block would add no new engine, input surface, option, or verifiable output over image-bg-replace -- only an SEO landing surface, the same core-capability-already-shipped dup class as grayscale-convert (=image-grayscale), tiff-to-png (=image-convert), and aac-to-mp3 (=audio-convert). (2) The genuinely-distinct alternative -- a pure-Rust per-pixel euclidean-tolerance recolor via the `image` crate -- is NOT a duplicate (different engine + alpha-preserving hard-tolerance semantics), but image-bytes pure-Rust output is chat+CLI only with NO page in the current gizza model (the F3/pixel-art-editor no-page pattern), so it cannot satisfy the loop's mandatory page/query-param + Playwright pixel-decode gate. Either branch fails: the page-capable one duplicates image-bg-replace, the non-duplicate one has no page surface. Scaffold not built. Revisit only if a pure-Rust image-bytes -> page render mode lands.

regex-playground  # duplicate of blocks/regex-tester. Verified 2026-07-30 against blocks/regex-tester/core/src/lib.rs. The picked row -- "Tests a regex against sample text and lists matches and capture groups" -- is exactly regex-tester's shipped scope: regex-tester::test(text, pattern, ignore_case, multiline, dotall) runs a regex against input text and returns a structured Report{match_count, group_count, group_names, matches} where each Match carries index, whole-match value, 0-based unicode-aware char start/end, and every capture Group (numbered AND named) with its value+span and a `matched` flag for optional groups that did not participate -- i.e. "lists matches and capture groups" verbatim, regex101-style. Its render() emits the same as human text for the page/CLI, with i/m/s flag toggles, empty-pattern and invalid-pattern errors. A regex-playground block would add no new engine, input, option, or verifiable output over regex-tester -- only a differently-named SEO landing surface. "Playground" implies an interactive live-typing sandbox, which no gizza surface provides (page/CLI/chat are all single-shot non-interactive), so there is nothing distinct to build even on that reading -- same interactive-UI-not-viable class as the skiplisted pixel-art-editor/zip-explorer. NOT merely adjacent to regex-extract (flat single-group extraction), regex-search, regex-column-validate, or regex-to-json -- it is the SAME test-and-report capability as regex-tester. Scaffold not built.
email-syntax-validate  # duplicate of blocks/email-validator: that block's core validate(input) is EXACTLY an RFC-syntax email checker with no network lookup — its own header states it "validate[s] an email address against RFC 5321/5322 syntax rules" and is "a *syntax* validator (it never touches the network — no DNS/MX or SMTP probing)". It splits local@domain, enforces the practical RFC 5321/5322 subset (MAX_TOTAL 254, MAX_LOCAL 64, MAX_LABEL 63, MAX_DOMAIN 253, single-unquoted-'@' rule, quoted-local handling, per-label/TLD checks), and returns valid/errors plus soft warnings + typo suggestions. The picked row "Checks email addresses for RFC 5322 syntax validity without any network lookup" is a strict subset of that scope, engine, and output — only the slug differs. Same semantic-near-dup class the exact-slug picker cannot catch. Scaffold not built.

raw-photo-metadata-extractor  # duplicate of blocks/image-metadata-viewer: camera RAW files (CR2, NEF, ARW, DNG) are all TIFF-based containers that store camera Make/Model, lens (LensModel/LensMake/LensSpecification), and capture settings (ExposureTime, FNumber, ISOSpeedRatings, FocalLength) as standard EXIF/TIFF tags in IFD0 and the Exif sub-IFD. image-metadata-viewer already feeds arbitrary bytes to kamadak-exif's Reader::read_from_container (which parses TIFF/DNG directly — a DNG *is* a TIFF) and returns the full field list plus decoded GPS, i.e. exactly camera+lens+capture-settings extraction. Verified: the same kamadak-exif 0.6 read_from_container path parses a bare little-endian TIFF and reads Make/Model, so RAW extraction adds no distinct in-model capability (proprietary maker-note lens-ID decoding is out-of-model and not what the row asks for).
scrypt  # duplicate of blocks/scrypt-derive (the picked function "derive keys and hash/verify passwords with scrypt at configurable N/r/p" is exactly scrypt-derive's surface). That block derives a key from a password using the scrypt memory-hard KDF (RFC 7914) with configurable N (CPU/memory cost), r (block size), p (parallelization), a salt, and output length, in hex or base64 (public derive()/derive_bytes()), and exposes mode=derive|verify where verify() re-derives from password+N/r/p+salt and constant-time-compares against an expected key (public verify()) — i.e. both key derivation AND password verification at configurable N/r/p. The row's only nuance, emitting/checking a self-contained PHC `$scrypt$` modular-crypt string, is an output-encoding choice around the same derive+compare engine (same class as the skiplisted argon2-verify/bcrypt-verify verify-mode dups and the password-hash bcrypt-hash+argon2-hash fusion), not a new user-facing capability; it would belong as an optional param on scrypt-derive, not a second block duplicating the scrypt KDF core. Scaffold not built.

log-timeline  # duplicate of blocks/log-parser (+ blocks/log-merger). Verified 2026-07-31 against blocks/log-parser/core/src/lib.rs and blocks/log-merger/core/src/lib.rs. The picked row "Normalize Apache/Nginx/syslog/auth.log lines into a unified, sortable event timeline with field extraction" is exactly log-parser's shipped scope PLUS a timestamp sort log-merger already ships. log-parser auto-detects the format (JSON/NDJSON, logfmt, syslog, Apache/nginx common+combined access logs) and "parses each line into a normalized set of fields" — its per-line Entry{ fields: Vec<(String,String)> } carries a `timestamp` field plus a unified severity (Trace<Debug<Info<Warn<Error) derived consistently across formats — then renders the filterable structured view as a Markdown table, JSON array, or CSV. That IS "normalize Apache/Nginx/syslog lines into a unified event view with field extraction"; auth.log is plain syslog RFC 3164, a format log-parser (and log-to-table's `syslog` preset) already handles, so it adds no new format. The only residual nuance, a "sortable timeline," is itself already shipped by log-merger, which "interleaves multiple pasted log sources into one unified timeline, sorted by each line's parsed timestamp" (parsing ISO 8601/RFC 3339, `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS`, Apache/nginx `10/Oct/2000:13:55:36 -0700`, syslog `Mon DD HH:MM:SS`, and unix epoch). A log-timeline block would add no new engine, input, option, or verifiable output over log-parser + log-merger — only a differently-named SEO landing surface. Same semantic-near-dup class the exact-slug picker cannot catch (there are already 8 log blocks: log-parser, log-analyzer, log-to-table, log-merger, log-format-converter, json-from-logs, ansi-log-renderer, ip-log-anonymizer). markdown-translate  # out-of-model: the picked deliverable's core is natural-language machine translation of a Markdown document's prose ("Translates a Markdown document while leaving code blocks, links, and formatting markers untouched"). Real MT needs a neural seq2seq model + multi-hundred-MB weights; gizza is pure-Rust + ffmpeg with a wasmi runtime that has NO ML model loader, and there is no wasm-safe machine-translation engine in the proven-crates set (references/wasm-crates.md) that can instantiate under wafer or be headlessly page-verified. The markdown-tokenize half -- segmenting translatable prose from fenced/inline code, link targets, and formatting markers (*, _, #, backticks) so they stay untouched -- IS pure Rust, but that half alone emits no translation; it is just a Markdown lexer with no user-facing output, and the "leaving code/links/markers untouched" clause is precisely what tripped the picker's in-model regex into mis-tagging the row `pure`, the SAME false-positive documented for the skiplisted csv-column-translate. The translation is the entire value and it is the out-of-model half. Same class as the picker's own out-of-model skips (translate, document-translate, image-translate, audio-translate) and the curated csv-column-translate / subtitle-translator skips ("core function is translation = needs an ML model"). Revisit only if an in-model translation backend lands. Scaffold not built.


video-encode-efficiency-report  # not viable in current gizza model: the tool's core deliverable is a COMPARISON of TWO user videos -- an encode against its source -- reporting size reduction, average bitrate, and (the load-bearing metric) SSIM. SSIM and size-reduction are inherently two-input: ffmpeg needs BOTH files present (`-i source -i encode -lavfi ssim`) to compute per-frame similarity, and size-reduction needs both file sizes. gizza's page surface (the primary supported surface for ffmpeg tools -- chat runs in a Service Worker where ffmpeg cannot run) accepts exactly ONE user upload: tool-ffmpeg.js writes the single uploaded `file` to ffmpeg's virtual FS, and its only extra-input mechanism (`plan.inputs`) is for BUNDLED fixed assets (fonts, drawtext text files), not a second user-provided video. Same un-buildable multi-input-ffmpeg class documented in create-next-tool references/page-patterns.md ("Multi-input ffmpeg (e.g. video-concat) is effectively un-buildable here: the page file-input is a single upload ... skiplist + defer") and the skiplisted video-concat-family tools. Dropping SSIM + size-reduction to fit a single input would gut it into a plain single-video bitrate probe, which does not satisfy the picked row. Scaffold not built. Revisit only if the page surface gains a second file-input widget.
video-frame-fingerprint  # not viable in current gizza model: the deliverable's output is inherently TEXT (a per-frame/per-second PERCEPTUAL HASH string -- a portable fingerprint stored for LATER MATCHING), not a media file, and the ffmpeg page surface (the primary supported surface for ffmpeg tools; chat runs in a Service Worker where ffmpeg cannot run) can only render an image/video/audio result. Verified 2026-07-31 against tools/generator/assets/runtime/tool.js: for runtime="ffmpeg" it requires the #tool-output-media / #tool-output-download elements, which the generator emits ONLY for format="image|video|audio"; a runtime=ffmpeg + format=text page hits the explicit guard showError("tool misconfigured: ffmpeg runtime needs an image/video/audio output") and returns -- so a fingerprint (text) tool has NO working page surface and cannot satisfy the loop's mandatory page/query-param + Playwright real-output gate. Separately, a genuinely PERCEPTUAL hash (robust + Hamming-comparable across re-encodes, which is the whole point of "fingerprint for later matching") requires post-processing decoded frames -- threshold-against-mean bit quantization / DCT -- that the argv-only ffmpeg model cannot do: ffmpeg produces the final output file and the Rust core / web build_argv only assemble argv (tool-ffmpeg.js runs the argv and reads exactly ONE out_name; the CLI/page never post-process ffmpeg's pixels in Rust). Spiked live 2026-07-31: ffmpeg's own per-frame hash muxer (`-f framehash`, cryptographic SHA-256) is NOT perceptual -- original vs a rescaled(640x480)+CRF40 re-encode gave completely different per-frame hashes even after scale=16:16,format=gray and luma quantization (lutyuv y=bitand(val,240)), so it fails robust matching. The genuinely-perceptual `signature` filter (MPEG-7 VideoSignature) IS present in native ffmpeg but (a) emits XML/binary TEXT (same page-runtime block above) and (b) is N->V: it writes the signature to a side `filename=` file while its stream output is a passthrough video, i.e. TWO outputs, which does not fit the single-output ArgvPlan (out_name) model. Same output-shape-not-viable class as the video-encode-efficiency-report / responsive-images skips (text/multi-file output the media-only ffmpeg page cannot render). Not a duplicate of an existing block, but moot -- unbuildable on every gizza surface. Revisit only if an ffmpeg-runtime text-output page mode or a Rust frame-post-processing hook lands. Scaffold not built.

video-match-cut-finder  # not viable in current gizza model: the deliverable is a TWO-VIDEO comparison that emits TEXT (a list of visually-matching frame pairs surfaced by comparing per-frame PERCEPTUAL fingerprints across both clips), and it fails on BOTH of the gizza-surface blockers already verified today for its two sibling skips. (1) Multi-input: the ffmpeg page surface (the only supported surface for ffmpeg tools; chat runs in a Service Worker where ffmpeg cannot run) accepts exactly ONE user upload -- tool-ffmpeg.js writes the single uploaded file to ffmpeg's virtual FS and plan.inputs is for BUNDLED fixed assets only, not a second user video (create-next-tool references/page-patterns.md:87-88: "Multi-input ffmpeg ... is effectively un-buildable here: the page file-input is a single upload ... skiplist + defer"), so a source-vs-candidate frame comparison has no working page input. (2) Text output: the surfaced result is a list of matching frame indices/timestamps (TEXT), but for runtime=ffmpeg the generator emits only #tool-output-media and tool.js:496 hits the explicit guard showError("tool misconfigured: ffmpeg runtime needs an image/video/audio output") for any format=text -- so the tool has no page surface and cannot satisfy the mandatory page/query-param + Playwright real-output gate. Additionally the load-bearing metric is a genuinely PERCEPTUAL (re-encode-robust, Hamming-comparable) frame hash, which the argv-only ffmpeg model cannot compute -- exactly the post-processing gap verified 2026-07-31 for video-frame-fingerprint (ffmpeg -f framehash is cryptographic not perceptual; the MPEG-7 `signature` filter emits XML text and is N->V two-output, neither fitting the single out_name ArgvPlan). This pick is the strict UNION of the un-buildable properties of the already-skiplisted video-encode-efficiency-report (two-input comparison) and video-frame-fingerprint (perceptual-hash TEXT output). Not a duplicate of an existing block, but moot -- unbuildable on every gizza surface. Revisit only if the page surface gains a second file-input widget AND an ffmpeg-runtime text-output page mode (or a Rust frame-post-processing hook) lands. Scaffold not built.

video-two-pass-encode  # not viable in current gizza model + honest single-pass equivalent already ships as blocks/video-target-filesize-encoder. The tool's whole differentiator is a REAL two-pass VBR encode (pass 1 gathers per-frame complexity stats into a passlog, pass 2 re-encodes reading that log for accurate, evenly-distributed quality at a target AVERAGE bitrate). Verified 2026-07-31 against the ffmpeg bridge contract (blocks/video-autocrop-bars/page/custom.js): each ffmpegExec(argv, inputsJson, out_name) is a FRESH ffmpeg invocation that is handed only inputsJson and returns exactly ONE output_b64 (the designated out_name) + the stderr `log` string — no arbitrary intermediate file persists across calls. Two-pass chaining that works (autocrop, cropdetect→crop) only works because pass-1's cross-call payload is TEXT captured from stderr (`log`) and parsed by the shared core; a real 2-pass encode instead needs pass-1's BINARY passlog FILE(s) to survive into pass-2, and libx264 writes TWO of them (ffmpeg2pass-0.log + ffmpeg2pass-0.log.mbtree) while pass 1 (`-pass 1 -f null -`) produces no media file for out_name to capture — so neither file can be shuttled back as a pass-2 input, and a fresh pass-2 instance never sees them. This is the exact limitation blocks/video-target-filesize-encoder/core/src/lib.rs already documents ("the gizza ffmpeg bridge is one build_argv → ffmpegExec per call with no passlog persisted across calls, so true two-pass VBR is out of model"), and blocks/video-compress echoes it. The in-model way to "hit a target average bitrate accurately" is exactly what video-target-filesize-encoder already ships: honest single-pass -b:v + -maxrate/-bufsize CBR-style targeting; dropping true 2-pass to fit this bridge would just re-create that block. Not a new in-model capability. Scaffold not built. Revisit only if the bridge gains cross-call FS persistence (a way to carry named intermediate files between ffmpegExec calls).

video-faststart  # semantic duplicate of blocks/video-duration-fix-remux. The pick's entire function -- move the MP4 moov atom to the front so the file streams progressively (`ffmpeg -i in.mp4 -c copy -movflags +faststart out.mp4`) -- is already fully delivered by blocks/video-duration-fix-remux. Verified 2026-07-31 against blocks/video-duration-fix-remux/{core/src/lib.rs,src/lib.rs,page/meta.toml}: that block exposes a `faststart` boolean param that DEFAULTS TO true and emits exactly `-movflags +faststart` for MP4/MOV output (core plan(): `if faststart && is_mp4_family(&out_ext) { argv.extend(["-movflags","+faststart"]) }`), combined with the same lossless `-map 0 -c copy` remux. Running it with its own defaults on an MP4 (container=keep, faststart=true, regen_timestamps=false) produces byte-identical argv to a dedicated faststart tool: `-i in.mp4 -map 0 -c copy -movflags +faststart out.mp4`. The existing block also already OWNS this discovery surface -- its meta.toml tags include "moov atom" and "faststart", its faststart field is labelled "Web fast-start (MP4/MOV: move index to front)", and it ships a dedicated preset example `"Rebuild as streaming MP4 (fast-start)"` (container=mp4, faststart=true). A standalone video-faststart would render a byte-identical result with strictly fewer options (no container remux, no PTS regen), i.e. a strict subset. Not built. Revisit only if faststart needs a materially different operation than the existing -c copy remux (it does not).

lua-runner  # not viable in current pure-Rust/wafer model: executing arbitrary Lua snippets requires embedding a Lua VM/interpreter in the block runtime, not just parsing/formatting source. Existing blocks only cover lua-formatter (static formatting via full-moon-style parsing), not execution. The create-next-tool proven wasm-safe crates list has no Lua interpreter recipe verified to instantiate under both wafer wasm32-wasip1 and wasm-pack wasm32-unknown-unknown; common Rust Lua bindings (mlua/rlua) depend on a bundled/native C Lua or LuaJIT backend and are not a proven fit for this minimal WASI/browser toolchain. This is the same runtime-engine class as the skiplisted javascript-runner/run-javascript: compiling is not enough; the engine must instantiate and safely sandbox execution with output/errors on all required surfaces. Revisit only if a proven wasm-safe Lua VM lands and sandbox/resource limits can be enforced. Scaffold not built.

ruby-runner  # not viable in current pure-Rust/wafer model: executing arbitrary Ruby snippets requires embedding a full Ruby VM/interpreter in the block runtime, including stdout capture, resource limits, and sandboxing. The backlog suggests Artichoke, but no Ruby interpreter recipe is proven in create-next-tool references for BOTH canonical wafer wasm32-wasip1 instantiation and wasm-pack/browser page tests, and this is the same arbitrary-code-execution class as the already-skiplisted javascript-runner/run-javascript and lua-runner. Existing blocks do not provide a Ruby runtime; a partial expression parser would be misleading rather than a Ruby runner.

pickle-to-json  # not viable in current pure-Rust+wasm gizza model (no Python interpreter/pyodide). The row's differentiating scope -- load a pickle "inside a sandboxed interpreter" and serialize DataFrames -- needs a live Python/pandas/numpy runtime. Pure-Rust pickle parsing (serde-pickle) can safely reconstruct only PLAIN data (dict/list/int/float/str/bytes/tuple/set/bool/None) by interpreting opcodes without executing them; it CANNOT materialize pandas DataFrames or numpy arrays, which pickle as GLOBAL/REDUCE/BUILD graphs referencing pandas.core.frame.DataFrame / numpy.core.multiarray._reconstruct reconstructors that only pandas/numpy can rebuild into meaningful JSON. Safely handling ARBITRARY pickles also requires REFUSING exactly those GLOBAL/REDUCE opcodes -- the arbitrary-code-execution (__reduce__) vector -- so the "sandboxed interpreter" promise reduces to plain-data-only. A plain-data subset would be a different, narrower tool that fails on the DataFrames the row leads with, so the described tool can't be delivered honestly. Same runtime-engine class as skiplisted lua-runner / javascript-runner. Scaffold not built. Revisit only if gizza gains a proven wasm-safe Python/pandas runtime.
geocode  # not viable in current gizza (pure/local) model. Forward geocoding (address -> latitude/longitude) and reverse geocoding (coordinates -> address) both fundamentally require a geocoding backend: either a live geocoding service/API (Nominatim/OpenStreetMap, Google, etc. -- network access, which gizza blocks do not have; type_hint "pure" is only a starting guess and is wrong here) OR a bundled address database / gazetteer + street-network dataset (multi-GB planet-scale data -- OSM address points, admin boundaries, street geometry -- utterly infeasible to embed in a small self-contained wasm block and its page). There is no deterministic pure-Rust computation that turns an arbitrary street address into coordinates or a coordinate back into a postal address without that data. Existing geo blocks do NOT cover this and it is not a duplicate: gpx-*/nmea/tcx/owntracks blocks parse already-recorded track coordinates; geojson-* convert supplied geometry; geofence-check does point-in-polygon; geometry-calculator/utm-link-builder/cidr-calculator are unrelated -- none map addresses <-> coordinates. Coordinate-format conversion (decimal degrees <-> DMS <-> UTM) would be a DIFFERENT, buildable tool, but it is NOT what this row ("Convert an address to latitude/longitude or coordinates back to an address") asks for. Same external-data/network class the loop skiplists; revisit only if a wasm-safe embeddable geocoder + dataset lands. Scaffold not built.
image-dedupe  # semantic duplicate of blocks/duplicate-image-finder. The pick's entire function -- find near-duplicate images in a set using perceptual hashing and report similarity clusters -- is already fully delivered by blocks/duplicate-image-finder. Verified 2026-08-01 against blocks/duplicate-image-finder/core/src/lib.rs: that block batch-scans a set of images, fingerprints each with a 64-bit perceptual dHash (grayscale -> 9x8 bilinear downscale -> 1 bit per adjacent-pixel comparison, brightness/contrast-invariant and robust to resize/re-compression), and reports near-duplicate PAIRS (similarity% = 100*(1 - hamming/64)) grouped by transitive closure into CLUSTERS (Group{members, keep, delete, kind}) with a keep/delete suggestion (highest-resolution kept). It exposes a similarity `threshold` (0-100) param -- exactly the "perceptual hashing + similarity clusters" the row asks for -- plus exact (byte-identical) vs near classification. A standalone image-dedupe would reimplement the identical dHash + hamming-threshold + cluster pipeline over the same image bytes with strictly the same output shape. Not built. Revisit only if image-dedupe needs a materially different fingerprint (e.g. pHash/DCT or wavelet hash) or feature (e.g. crop/rotation invariance) that duplicate-image-finder's dHash cannot provide.
broken-image-batch-checker  # duplicate of blocks/image-format-validator + batch-only delta. Verified 2026-08-06 against blocks/image-format-validator/core/src/lib.rs: validate() already fully decodes an image to catch corrupt/truncated files, never throws on bad input, and returns exactly this row's pass/fail verdict (`valid: bool` plus a `corruption` diagnostic string for unrecognised signatures and truncated-but-known formats -- see its `truncated_png_is_corrupt_not_a_panic` / `unrecognised_bytes` tests), alongside detected format/MIME and decoded dimensions. The picked row's ONLY new dimension is "many files at once, returned as a list", i.e. a batch invocation pattern over that same engine, not a distinct capability. Established precedent for this exact class: exif-batch-export (single-image engine image-metadata-viewer + "many images into one table"), strip-image-metadata ("batch handling would be an interface improvement, not a distinct tool"), image-resizer ("batch support is a chat/CLI invocation pattern, not a distinct tool surface"). A source_list build would additionally have no page surface (multi-image tools are chat+CLI only, like duplicate-image-finder), so the mandatory query-param + Playwright verification gate could not run. Scaffold not built.
data-profile  # duplicate of blocks/csv-stats (plus re-bundle of blocks/csv-type-inferrer + blocks/descriptive-stats). Verified 2026-08-06 against blocks/csv-stats/core/src/lib.rs: stats(data, has_header, delimiter) already emits, in ONE per-column report over a pasted CSV, 4 of this row's 5 named deliverables — an inferred type per column (`kind`: "numeric" if every non-empty value parses as a finite number, else "text"), the missing-value count (`empty`, counted per cell; missing % is literally empty/rows*100 over two numbers it already returns), CARDINALITY verbatim (`distinct` = number of distinct non-empty values, via HashSet), and a numeric distribution summary (min, max, mean, sum) — plus the total row count; its manifest summary is "Per-column summary statistics for a CSV." Same input (CSV text + header flag + delimiter, with comma/tab/semicolon/pipe accepted), same engine (pure-Rust `csv` crate, flexible reader), same output shape (per-column table + row count), same user function ("what's in each column of this CSV"). The residual deltas each map onto an existing block or onto a field added inside csv-stats' existing per-column loop: finer type labels (int/float/bool/date/datetime/string) are blocks/csv-type-inferrer verbatim (its page description: "per-column types (int, float, bool, date, datetime, string)"); median/quartiles/std-dev are blocks/descriptive-stats verbatim (mean/median/mode/variance/standard deviation/quartiles); and a top-N value-frequency table is one counter added to the loop that already builds the `distinct` set. So the honest change here is "add missing %, quartiles and a top-values column to csv-stats" — an enhancement to an existing block — and shipping it as a second block would fragment per-column CSV reporting across two near-identical tools. Same re-bundle-of-existing-blocks class as the skiplisted csv-clean / bank-csv-summary / password-hash entries, and the same enhancement-not-a-distinct-tool class as statistics-calculator (dup of descriptive-stats). Scaffold not built. Revisit only if profiling needs an engine csv-stats cannot host (e.g. streaming multi-GB files or cross-column correlation/interaction analysis, which this row does not ask for).
eth-wallet  # duplicate of blocks/crypto-keypair-generator. The picked row ("Generates an Ethereum wallet — a secp256k1 private key, public key, and checksummed address") is a strict SUBSET of the existing block's ethereum branch. Verified 2026-08-06 against blocks/crypto-keypair-generator/core/src/lib.rs: `Chain::parse` accepts "ethereum"/"eth" as a first-class enum value on the `chain` param, and `generate(Chain::Ethereum)` calls `ethereum_from_secret(&random_secp256k1_scalar())` — a fresh 32-byte secp256k1 scalar from the OS CSPRNG (`getrandom`, WASI `random_get` on wasm32-wasip1) — returning `private_key_hex` (raw 32-byte scalar, lower-hex) + `private_key_export` (Ethereum `0x`-hex), `public_key_hex` (uncompressed 65-byte `0x04‖X‖Y` SEC1 point), and `address` = EIP-55 mixed-case checksummed hex, computed exactly as the spec requires (Keccak-256 of the 64-byte X‖Y with the 0x04 tag dropped, last 20 bytes, then `eip55()` mixed-case checksum). That is every one of this row's four named deliverables — private key, public key, address, EIP-55 checksum — with nothing left over. Correctness is already locked by known-answer tests in that block: `eip55_matches_spec_vectors` (the four canonical EIP-55 specification vectors) and `ethereum_private_key_one_known_address` (privkey=1 → 0x7E5F4552091A69125d5DfCb7b8C2659029395Bdf, plus the generator-point uncompressed pubkey prefix), i.e. the deterministic derivation test a new eth-wallet block would have to re-add verbatim. The existing block also already OWNS this discovery surface: its manifest summary is "Generate a crypto wallet keypair + address" and its description names "Ethereum EIP-55 checksummed hex" and the "Ethereum 0x-hex" private-key export outright. A standalone eth-wallet would reimplement the identical k256 + Keccak-256 + EIP-55 pipeline over the same CSPRNG and emit a byte-identical result shape with strictly fewer options (one hard-coded chain instead of bitcoin/ethereum/solana), and — being a no-input non-deterministic generator like its parent, which ships chat+CLI only with no page/ directory — it would add no new surface either. Same enhancement-not-a-distinct-tool / strict-subset class as the skiplisted video-faststart (dup of video-duration-fix-remux via a defaulted param) and image-dedupe. Distinct from the related derive-only blocks, which all require a GIVEN key and none of which generate a wallet: blocks/secp256k1-pubkey-derive (pubkey only, explicitly "does not generate random keys or emit addresses"), blocks/bitcoin-address (Bitcoin encodings), blocks/hd-key-derive (BIP32 from a seed/xprv), blocks/ecdsa-secp256k1 (sign/verify). Scaffold not built. Revisit only if an Ethereum wallet needs something crypto-keypair-generator's ethereum branch cannot host (e.g. BIP39 mnemonic phrases or HD account paths, which this row does not ask for).

numeric-column-coercer  # duplicate of blocks/numeric-string-sanitizer. The picked deliverable -- "Forces a text column to numeric, stripping stray whitespace and flagging or quarantining cells that cannot be parsed" -- is exactly that block's stated job: its core doc-comment reads "Turns messy formatted number cells ... into plain machine-readable floats, one output row per input row", it strips stray whitespace/currency/thousands separators/units/percent/accounting parentheses/trailing minus/K-M-B-T suffixes, and it already has BOTH halves of the flag-or-quarantine behaviour -- `on_error` = blank|keep|marker|fail (marker emits `#ERROR` in place of an unparseable cell = flagging; blank/keep keep the column aligned) and `output` = values|table|json where table is a tab-separated `original`/`value`/`status` audit table and json carries per-row status + totals (= quarantining the bad cells into a separate reviewable channel). It also adds decimal_separator auto|dot|comma, decimals rounding, and stats, so there is no in-model capability left for a separate coercer. Nearby but distinct blocks confirm the gap is already closed, not merely adjacent: blocks/csv-column-type-validator (reports type conformance of a column, does not rewrite values), blocks/csv-type-inferrer (guesses column types across a table), blocks/numeric-range-check (bounds checking on already-numeric values), blocks/extract-numbers-from-text (pulls numbers out of free prose, not a column coercion). Scaffold not built. Revisit only if a coercer needs something numeric-string-sanitizer cannot host (e.g. multi-column CSV-in/CSV-out coercion of a named column, which this row does not ask for).

verify-signature  # semantic duplicate of blocks/ed25519-sign-verify + blocks/rsa-verify. The picked row ("Verifies an Ed25519/RSA signature over a message against a public key") names exactly two algorithms, and BOTH already ship a dedicated verify block covering the full input set (message + signature + public key -> valid true/false). Verified 2026-08-07 against source. Ed25519: blocks/ed25519-sign-verify/core/src/lib.rs exposes `pub fn verify(message, key, signature)` via an `operation` param that is `Param::enumv("operation", ["sign","verify"])`, parses public keys in every encoding real tooling emits (SPKI PEM '-----BEGIN PUBLIC KEY-----', or a raw 32-byte point as hex or base64), auto-detects hex vs base64 64-byte signatures, takes the message as utf8/hex/base64 (`message_encoding` enum), and correctly returns `valid=false` as a RESULT rather than an error when the signature merely doesn't match (erroring only on malformed keys/signatures) -- the exact semantics a verifier needs. RSA: blocks/rsa-verify/core/src/lib.rs exposes `pub fn verify(message, signature_b64, public_key_pem, scheme, hash) -> Result<bool,String>` over both signature schemes (`scheme` enum pkcs1v15|pss) and all three digests (`hash` enum sha256|sha384|sha512), accepting SPKI ('BEGIN PUBLIC KEY') and PKCS#1 ('BEGIN RSA PUBLIC KEY') PEM; its manifest summary is literally "Verify an RSA signature against a public key". So the union of the two blocks is a strict SUPERSET of this row: a combined verify-signature block would add no algorithm, no encoding, no scheme, and no output field -- only an `algorithm` selector in front of the same two engines, which is a routing convenience, not a capability. gizza's established shape is one block per signature algorithm (see also blocks/ecdsa-sign, blocks/rsa-sign, blocks/pgp-sign, blocks/jwt-verify, blocks/hmac-verify), and merging two would fragment/duplicate discovery for both existing pages. Same strict-subset / re-bundle-of-existing-blocks class as the skiplisted eth-wallet (dup of crypto-keypair-generator's ethereum branch) and data-profile. Scaffold not built. Revisit only if signature verification needs an algorithm neither block hosts (e.g. Ed448 or ML-DSA), which this row does not ask for.
embedding-export  # out-of-model: the tool's core deliverable is semantic EMBEDDINGS -- a dense vector per row/document that must live in the same learned vector space as future query embeddings, or the exported file is useless to the Qdrant/pgvector/ruvector import it promises. Verified 2026-08-07. (1) The backlog row itself names the implementation as transformers-js running Xenova/all-MiniLM-L6-v2 client-side (22M-param BERT encoder, ~90MB weights) with plain Rust->WASM only ASSEMBLING the export file; the picker's type_hint "pure" is a false negative -- its IN_MODEL regex matched the "plain Rust->WASM assembles the export file in memory" half of the feasibility note and outranked the model half, but the embedding half is the entire value. (2) gizza's runtime is pure-Rust + ffmpeg on wasmi with NO ML model loader and no weight-fetch surface -- same blocker class as the already-skiplisted csv-column-translate (Xenova/nllb-200), video-speech-transcript-extractor (Xenova/whisper-base), entity-anonymizer (NER) and markdown-translate. No wasm-safe transformer inference crate exists in references/wasm-crates.md (no onnx/tract/candle/burn/tokenizers entry); an engine crate must INSTANTIATE under `wafer build`, and none does. (3) A pure-Rust substitute is NOT the picked tool: a hashing/TF-IDF vectorizer would emit vectors in a corpus-local, non-transferable space, so a user could never embed a QUERY the same way at search time -- it would ship a file that silently fails to retrieve anything in the vector DB, which is worse than not shipping it. NOT a duplicate of blocks/vectorize (raster image -> SVG tracing, unrelated) nor of the csv-to-json / csv-to-ndjson family (those carry no vectors). The in-model half -- serializing an already-computed vector column to CSV/JSON/NDJSON -- is already covered by the existing csv-json-convert / csv-to-ndjson blocks. Scaffold not built.
env-file-sort  # duplicate of blocks/dotenv-manager (output="normalized" + sort_keys), and a near-verbatim restatement of the already-skiplisted dotenv-normalizer row. Verified 2026-08-07 against blocks/dotenv-manager/core/src/lib.rs + src/lib.rs. The picked row ("Sorts, deduplicates, and groups keys in a .env file while preserving comments") maps one-for-one onto dotenv-manager's shipped surface: SORT = the `sort_keys` boolean param ("Emit keys in alphabetical order instead of first-seen order"), which sorts the ordered view by key before every value-bearing render (core lib.rs:100); DEDUPLICATE = merge_entries(), whose key->Entry map makes the last occurrence of a repeated key win while `order` preserves first-seen position (last-wins runtime semantics, applied within a single pasted file, not just across the optional merge overlay); OUTPUT = render_normalized(), the `normalized` mode that emits the cleaned, deduped `KEY=value` lines (re-quoting values only where needed) — i.e. exactly "a sorted, deduped .env file"; and duplicate keys are additionally REPORTED by find_duplicates() (key + the line numbers it appeared on) in the default `report` mode. Same input surface (one pasted .env: KEY=VALUE lines, # comments, blank lines, single/double quotes, inline comments and an `export ` prefix all handled), same pure-Rust dotenv parse/dedupe/sort/render engine shared with blocks/dotenv-validator and blocks/env-to-configmap. The row's only two deltas are cosmetic render options on that same normalized output: (a) GROUPING keys by common prefix (a blank line between DB_*/AWS_* runs) and (b) PRESERVING full-line comments through the render (parse() currently skips blank lines and `#` comment lines silently at core lib.rs:128, so normalized output drops them). Both are one-param enhancements to dotenv-manager's existing render_normalized — the same class already ruled on for dotenv-normalizer (whose row asked for the identical prefix-grouping plus '=' alignment), env-template-generator (whose only deltas were typed placeholders + "carrying comments through"), env-file-parser, env-json-converter and note-action-extractor — not a distinct block re-implementing the dotenv parse+dedupe+render engine a fifth time. Both csv rows also come from the same gizza-search-tools-canonical source list, so this is one backlog entry duplicated under two slugs. Scaffold not built.

json-to-query-string  # duplicate of blocks/query-string-codec (its `build` direction, verbatim). Verified 2026-08-07 against blocks/query-string-codec/{core/src/lib.rs,src/lib.rs,page/meta.toml}. The picked row -- "Serializes a JSON object into a properly percent-encoded URL query string" -- is precisely that block's `direction = "build"` branch: `pub fn build(input, array_style, space_as_plus, sort_keys, prefix_question_mark) -> Result<String, String>` parses the pasted text as JSON, rejects a non-object top level with an actionable error ("top-level JSON must be an object ..., got a <kind>"), then walks the object emitting `k=v` pairs joined by `&`, RFC 3986 percent-encoding every key and value (uppercase hex, unreserved set preserved, literal `+` escaped to `%2B`). It already covers every table-stake a dedicated json-to-query-string tool would need, and then some: nested objects and arrays via bracket notation, an `array_style` enum (brackets `tags[]=a&tags[]=b` / indices `tags[0]=a` / repeat `tags=a&tags=b` / comma `tags=a,b`), `space_as_plus` to choose form-urlencoded `+` vs strict `%20`, `sort_keys` for deterministic alphabetical output, and `prefix_question_mark` to emit a ready-to-append `?...`. The existing block also already OWNS this discovery surface: its page tags include the exact phrase "json to query string", its hero copy advertises "build encoded query strings from JSON with repeat, bracket, index, or comma array styles", its `[input.labels]` name the direction "JSON → query string", and its core doc-comment states outright that the build half "is the reason this tool exists" (no other block did that direction). A standalone json-to-query-string block would re-implement the identical percent-encoder + object walker and emit byte-identical output with strictly fewer options (one hard-coded direction, likely one array style). Distinct from -- and not covered by -- the reverse-direction blocks: blocks/parse-query-string and blocks/url-encode/blocks/data-uri-encode encode single strings, not object graphs. Same strict-subset / defaulted-param class as the skiplisted video-faststart (dup of video-duration-fix-remux) and eth-wallet (dup of crypto-keypair-generator's ethereum branch). Scaffold not built. Revisit only if JSON→query-string needs something query-string-codec's build branch cannot host (e.g. a custom key/value delimiter or PHP-style deep `a[b][c]` on non-object roots), which this row does not ask for.
json-to-markdown-table  # duplicate of blocks/json-to-markdown (its default top-level-array path, verbatim). Verified 2026-08-07 against blocks/json-to-markdown/core/src/lib.rs and confirmed by a live run of the installed CLI. The picked row -- "Turns a JSON array of objects into a Markdown table, using union of keys as columns" -- is precisely that block's array branch: `to_markdown()` matches a top-level `Value::Array` straight into `render_array()` (core lib.rs:93), whose default `array_mode = "auto"` predicate `is_flat_object_array()` (lib.rs:262) accepts any array whose elements are all objects with scalar values -- it does NOT require identical key sets -- and dispatches to `render_table()`. `render_table()` opens with the literal comment "Column set = union of keys across all rows, in first-seen (or sorted) order" (lib.rs:168) and builds `cols` by walking every row's keys, then emits a GitHub-flavored pipe table with a `| --- |` separator row and an EMPTY cell wherever a row lacks a column (`map.get(c).map(cell).unwrap_or_default()`, lib.rs:194). Empirical check: `gizza tool json-to-markdown json='[{"name":"Ada","role":"eng"},{"name":"Bo","team":"ops"}]'` returns exactly `| name | role | team |` / `| --- | --- | --- |` / `| Ada | eng |  |` / `| Bo |  | ops |` -- byte-for-byte the deliverable this row asks for, with no non-default params set. The existing block is also a strict SUPERSET on this input class: `array_mode = "table"` (`Param::enumv`) forces a table via `all_objects()` even when cells are nested objects/arrays (rendered as inline JSON) so ragged/nested rows still tabulate; `sort_keys` switches the union of columns from first-seen to alphabetical order; `array_mode = "list"` is the escape hatch for arrays that should not tabulate; and the same union-of-keys table renderer already applies to arrays nested under object keys, which a top-level-only tool could not reach. A standalone json-to-markdown-table block would re-implement the identical column-union walker and pipe-table writer over the same single pasted-JSON input and emit identical output with strictly fewer options. Same strict-subset / already-the-default class as the skiplisted json-to-query-string (dup of query-string-codec's build direction) and video-faststart (dup of video-duration-fix-remux). Distinct from -- and not covered by -- blocks/markdown-table-format (formats an EXISTING Markdown table), blocks/markdown-table-to-csv (reverse direction), blocks/json-to-html-table (HTML output) and blocks/csv-to-table (CSV input). Scaffold not built. Revisit only if JSON->Markdown-table needs something json-to-markdown's table renderer cannot host (e.g. per-column alignment markers or explicit column selection/ordering), which this row does not ask for.
