xlsx-recalculate  # NOT VIABLE (not a duplicate — no existing block recalculates formulas; blocks/spreadsheet-formula-audit explicitly states it "does not recalculate formulas", csv-formula-eval evaluates arithmetic over CSV columns not a workbook, and xlsx-to-csv/csv-to-xlsx/xlsx-sheet-diff only convert/compare). The CSV's authored build path is "via ironcalc crate" and ironcalc 0.8.3 is the only crate offering xlsx recalculation; verified 2026-08-08 against the vendored 0.8.3 sources + real builds, it fails BOTH wasm targets for two independent, non-workaroundable reasons. (1) C toolchain: `ironcalc = "0.8.3"` depends on `zip = "0.6"` with DEFAULT features -> zip 0.6.6 -> zstd 0.11 -> zstd-sys 2.0.16 (`cargo tree -i zstd-sys` confirms the chain), whose build.rs compiles C via cc-rs; `cargo build --target wasm32-wasip1 --release` AND `--target wasm32-unknown-unknown --release` both die with `error occurred in cc-rs: failed to find tool "clang"`, i.e. the block never compiles for either the canonical scripts/build-block-wasm.sh artifact or the wasm-pack browser bundle. ironcalc exposes exactly ONE feature (`mock_time`) and no zip/backend knob, and cargo feature unification is ADDITIVE, so declaring `zip = { default-features = false }` downstream cannot switch zstd/bzip2 off — the only fixes are a clang+wasi-sysroot C toolchain (out of the repo's pure-Rust model, and the C deps would still ship in the block) or an upstream patch. (2) JS-host dependency on every wasm32 target: ironcalc_base 0.8.3's Cargo.toml puts `js-sys`/`wasm-bindgen` under `[target.'cfg(target_arch = "wasm32")'.dependencies]`, so wasm32-WASIP1 (also target_arch = wasm32) takes the browser branch — src/tz/mod.rs cfg-selects browser_tz.rs, whose `#[wasm_bindgen(inline_js = ...)]` extern block imports `ic_tz_validate`/`ic_tz_all`/`ic_tz_parts` (Intl.DateTimeFormat shims) and model.rs:65 uses `js_sys::Date::now()`. `Model::from_workbook` calls `Tz::parse(&workbook.settings.tz)` unconditionally (base model.rs:1686), so those JS imports sit on the mandatory load path — the block.wasm would carry JS-module imports wafer's WASI host cannot supply and would fail to INSTANTIATE even if it compiled; `mock_time` only stubs the clock, not the tz shim. No alternative engine exists: ironcalc_base alone is C-free but has no xlsx import/export (`load_from_xlsx_bytes`/`save_xlsx_to_writer` live only in the `ironcalc` wrapper), tables-core 0.1.0 wraps the same ironcalc and inherits both defects, and calamine (used by xlsx-to-csv/spreadsheet-formula-audit) reads only CACHED values plus formula strings with no evaluator. Writing a spreadsheet formula engine (dependency graph, ~400 Excel functions, arrays/spills, dates/tz) from scratch is out-of-model. Revisit when ironcalc drops the zip default features (or bumps to zip 2.x with a pure-Rust backend) AND gates its js-sys/tz shim on `target_os = "unknown"` rather than `target_arch = "wasm32"`.

markdown-link-extractor  # duplicate of blocks/markdown-query. The picked row ("Lists every link and image in a Markdown document and flags broken reference-style definitions") is already served end-to-end by the shipped markdown-query block, whose descriptor exposes `extract` as Param::enumv["headings","links","images","code_blocks","tables"]: `extract=links` lists every inline/reference link and autolink (text + destination + title) and `extract=images` lists every inline/reference image (alt + src + title), both via the same pulldown-cmark CommonMark+GFM parse, rendered as text/json/markdown with optional 1-based source line numbers (include_line_numbers) -- i.e. the whole "lists every link and image in a Markdown document" deliverable, with strictly MORE output detail (titles, line numbers, three render formats) than the picked row asks for. blocks/link-extractor covers the same Markdown-link half a second time (pulldown-cmark inline+reference links and autolinks, plus heading/anchor jump targets, base_url resolution and dedup, HTML or Markdown source). The only residual sliver is the ref-def lint clause ("flags broken reference-style definitions" -- [text][ref] with no matching [ref]: line, and unused definitions), which is a markdownlint-class rule belonging in the existing blocks/markdown-lint MD0xx rule engine (mode=check/fix), not a justification for a third Markdown-link extractor: a new block would re-emit markdown-query links+images verbatim and bolt one lint check onto it, splitting the same function across three pages and giving chat/CLI two near-identical tools to disambiguate. No new build.

subdomain-extractor  # duplicate of blocks/extract-domains. Verified 2026-08-08 against blocks/extract-domains/{core/src/lib.rs,src/lib.rs,page/meta.toml,page/content.md}. The picked row -- "Extracts unique, deduplicated hostnames and subdomains from pasted logs, source or scan output and groups them by registrable domain" -- is exactly that block's shipped behaviour, clause for clause. (a) "from pasted logs, source or scan output": extract-domains takes ONE free-text param (`Param::string("text")`, described as "plain text, URLs, and email addresses are all supported") and scans it with a broad hostname regex that tolerates an optional scheme (`https://`, `ftp://`) and an optional `user@` / email local-part, so log lines, source files and scanner output are the same input class -- there is no format-specific parsing a dedicated subdomain tool could add. (b) "unique, deduplicated hostnames and subdomains": `extract_opts()` pushes every validated candidate through `push_unique()` (HashSet + first-seen Vec) and returns `hostnames` as the FULL host as written -- `www.blog.example.co.uk`, i.e. subdomains at any depth -- lowercased with the FQDN trailing dot stripped; unit test `dedupes_first_seen` asserts `a.example.com`/`b.example.com`/`a.example.com` collapses to two hosts. (c) "groups them by registrable domain": the same pass computes the eTLD+1 for every host via the Public Suffix List (`psl::domain_str`, with a `suffix.typ()` ICANN/private check that drops IPs, version numbers like `3.14` and bogus TLDs) and the default `mode = "both"` returns BOTH lists with counts (`hostname_count`, `registrable_count`) -- the exact host->registrable-domain relation the row asks for; test `handles_multi_level_suffix` pins `shop.foo.bar.gov.uk` -> `bar.gov.uk`, so multi-level suffixes group correctly rather than naively splitting on dots. The existing block is a strict superset in options too: `mode` is a `Param::enumv["hostname","registrable","both"]` so callers can take just the subdomain list or just the apex list, and `sort` switches first-seen order to alphabetical (useful precisely for the recon/scan-output use case). The only residual sliver is PRESENTATION -- emitting an indented `apex -> [subdomain, subdomain]` tree instead of two flat counted lists -- which is a render variant of data extract-domains already returns in full, not a new capability; a new block would re-run the identical regex + PSL validator over the identical single text input and re-emit the same host/eTLD+1 pairs, splitting one function across two pages and giving chat/CLI two near-identical tools to disambiguate. Same strict-subset / render-variant class as the skiplisted json-to-markdown-table (dup of json-to-markdown's default array path) and markdown-link-extractor (dup of markdown-query). Distinct from -- and not covered by -- blocks/extract-urls (whole URLs, not hosts), blocks/enum-domain-check and blocks/dns-message-parser (network/DNS-record oriented), blocks/url-cleaner and blocks/url-stripper (rewrite a URL, no extraction), and blocks/ioc-extract (multi-IOC triage where domains are one of several types). Scaffold not built. Revisit only if subdomain extraction needs something extract-domains cannot host (e.g. wildcard/`*.` handling or per-apex grouped output as a first-class format), which this row does not ask for.

Existing blocks/php-serialize and blocks/php-unserialize only cover PHP serialized data, and blocks/code-formatter covers HTML/CSS/JavaScript/JSON but not PHP. video-to-hls  # not viable in current gizza ffmpeg model: HLS output is inherently MULTI-OUTPUT (an .m3u8 playlist plus N .ts/.m4s segment files) and the backlog explicitly asks for them "packaged in-browser". gizza's ffmpeg dispatch/page/chat model returns exactly ONE output file (block-utils ExecArgs.output is a single filename and ExecResult.output is one Vec<u8>; page renders one image/video/audio/text artifact), with no zip/multi-file ffmpeg output surface. ffmpeg's HLS muxer writes a playlist that references sibling segment files, so returning only the playlist is broken, and returning only one .ts chunk is not HLS. Same unsupported multi-output class as audio-split-on-silence, video-chapter-splitter, speech-segmenter, and video-split-into-segments. A single-file alternative (DASH/HLS in one fMP4, or a zip wrapper) would need a new multi-file packaging/readback surface in the runtime, not a per-tool block. Scaffold not built.
magic-bytes-fixer  # not viable as a separate honest gizza tool: the row promises to repair corrupted/wrong file headers and structure for PNG, JPEG, ZIP, PDF "and more", but safe structural repair is format-specific validation/reconstruction, not just replacing signatures. A narrow magic-byte patcher would silently produce files that appear typed but remain invalid, while real repair needs per-format parsers and fixers (CRC/chunk tables for PNG, central directory for ZIP, xref/trailer for PDF, segment tables for JPEG). Existing blocks cover detection/inspection/conversion surfaces; this broad repair promise should be split into explicit per-format validators/fixers before building. Scaffold not built.
dummy-csv-generator  # duplicate of blocks/fake-data-generator. Verified 2026-08-09 by RUNNING the existing block, not just reading its descriptor: `gizza tool fake-data-generator count=3 format=csv fields=id,full_name,email,boolean seed=42` returns a header row plus 3 comma-separated data rows (`id,full_name,email,boolean` / `1,Aiden Ahmed,aiden.ahmed764@sample.co,true` / ...), which is the picked row -- "Generates a CSV of N rows with user-specified typed columns for testing and demos" -- clause for clause. (a) "a CSV": `Param::enumv("format", ["csv","json","sql","xml"])` DEFAULTS to csv, with a separate `header` boolean to toggle the column-name row and a `csv_escape()` helper in core/src/lib.rs that quotes/doubles embedded commas and quotes. (b) "of N rows": `Param::integer("count").default(10).min(1).max(MAX_ROWS)` -- 1-1000 rows. (c) "with user-specified typed columns": `Param::string("fields")` takes a comma-separated column list in ANY order over 27 typed generators (id, uuid, first_name, last_name, full_name, username, email, phone, street, city, state, zip, country, latitude, longitude, company, job_title, birthdate, domain, ipv4, ipv6, mac, color, boolean, credit_card, sentence), plus `all`; blank defaults to full_name,email,phone,street,city,state,zip. Each column name IS its type, so choosing the field list is exactly choosing the typed columns. (d) "for testing and demos": that is the block's stated purpose -- its page/meta.toml tags already include "csv generator", "dummy data", "test data", "sample data", and its skill description says the output is synthetic placeholder data for testing/mocking. It also adds `seed` for reproducible datasets, which the picked row does not even ask for. The only sliver a dummy-csv-generator could add is ARBITRARY user-chosen column NAMES (e.g. `order_ref:int`) rather than the fixed 27-name vocabulary -- and that "caller defines the shape: own field names mapped to a type token" grammar is already owned by blocks/mock-data-generator (`Param::string("schema")`, e.g. `id:int(1..1000), user:{name:name, email:email}, tags:string[3]`, count 1-1000, seed, nested objects/arrays), which differs only in emitting JSON instead of CSV. So the row sits entirely inside the union of two shipped blocks, and building it would give chat/CLI a third near-identical data generator to disambiguate. Same strict-subset class as the skiplisted json-to-markdown-table and subdomain-extractor. Distinct from -- and not covered by -- blocks/csv-sample (samples rows from an EXISTING csv), blocks/json-schema-faker and blocks/json-schema-to-sample (drive off a JSON Schema), and blocks/weighted-random-sampler. Scaffold not built. Revisit only if CSV output with arbitrary caller-named typed columns is wanted, which is better added as a `format=csv` option on mock-data-generator than as a new block.

Verified 2026-08-09 by RUNNING the installed CLI, not just reading the descriptor: `gizza tool csv-chart-generator 'data=value\n1\n2\n2\n3\n3\n3\n4\n4\n9\n10' chart_type=histogram y_column=value bins=5 title=Distribution` returns a complete standalone SVG -- titled, with a "Frequency" y-axis label, the column name ("value") as the x-axis label, gridlines with count ticks, bin-edge x labels (1 / 2.8 / 4.6 / 6.4 / 8.2 / 10) and one `<rect>` bar per bucket -- i.e. the picked row ("Plots a histogram of a chosen numeric column to show its distribution") clause for clause. (b) "Plots a histogram": `render_histogram()` (core/src/lib.rs:287) computes the range, splits it into equal-width buckets, clamps the top edge into the last bucket, and draws the bars, with x labels thinned via `label_every` so high bin counts stay readable; unit test `histogram_bins_y_column` pins the behaviour. (c) "to show its distribution": that is the block's advertised purpose -- its page title is "CSV Chart Generator -- Bar, Line, Scatter & Histogram to SVG", its hero copy names the histogram mode, and its page tags already include the exact phrase "histogram generator", so the existing block also OWNS this discovery surface. A standalone histogram-maker block would re-implement the identical CSV parse + column resolver + equal-width binner + SVG bar writer over the identical single pasted-CSV input and emit an equivalent chart, splitting one function across two pages and giving chat/CLI two near-identical histogram tools to disambiguate. Distinct from -- and not covered by -- blocks/data-bin (labels each CSV ROW with its bucket, no chart), blocks/frequency-distribution (character/byte/n-gram symbol tallies, not numeric binning), blocks/descriptive-stats and blocks/csv-stats (numeric summaries, no plot), blocks/hexbin-density-chart (2-D x/y density, not a 1-D distribution), and blocks/histogram-equalizer (image contrast). Scaffold not built. Revisit only if a KDE/density overlay is wanted, which is better added as a `kde` boolean on csv-chart-generator's histogram branch than as a new block.

The picked row asks for a tool that "explains each header in a request/response, including what it does and any security or caching implications." Verified 2026-08-09 against blocks/http-header-analyzer/{src/lib.rs,core/src/lib.rs}: that block already parses a pasted HTTP header block (optional leading status line captured separately via split_status_line, CRLF or bare LF, obsolete folded continuation lines joined, blank line ends the block) and emits, for EVERY header in wire order, a plain-English `explanation` plus a `category` drawn from caching / compression / content / security / cookie / cors / server / redirect / other — i.e. exactly "what it does" tagged by implication. Related existing blocks confirm the space is covered: http-header-parser (case-normalized name -> value map), http-headers-diff (left/right header diff), parse-http-message (full request/response message into structured fields). Scaffold not built.

Same blocker as skiplisted video-pixel-diff-csv and video-crop-difference-detector.

Same blocker as video-pixel-diff-csv, video-crop-difference-detector, and video-changed-region-bbox.

wireguard-config-generator  # duplicate of blocks/wireguard-config-builder (plus a re-bundle of blocks/keypair-generator and blocks/qr-code-generator). Verified 2026-08-13 against source. The picked row has four deliverables and every one already ships. (1) "builds a complete client/server config" is blocks/wireguard-config-builder verbatim: core/src/lib.rs `pub fn build(&WgInput, format)` assembles and validates the full `[Interface]` + `[Peer]` file — PrivateKey, Address, ListenPort, DNS, MTU, PublicKey, PresharedKey, AllowedIPs, Endpoint, PersistentKeepalive — with every key checked as base64 of exactly 32 bytes (`validate_key`), every Address/AllowedIPs/DNS entry parsed as an IP/CIDR with per-family prefix bounds (`validate_ip_entry`), bracketed-IPv6 `host:port` endpoints (`validate_endpoint`), and port/MTU/keepalive range checks, emitted as the ready-to-save wg0.conf text or a parsed JSON object (`format` = conf|json). Both directions are covered by the same block: a client config is the Endpoint+PersistentKeepalive shape, a server config is the ListenPort shape, and its own tests cover full-tunnel, split-tunnel, dual-stack, IPv6-literal endpoint and JSON. (2) "Generates a WireGuard keypair" is blocks/keypair-generator with `algorithm = "x25519"`: it generates a fresh Curve25519 key via x25519-dalek `StaticSecret`/`PublicKey` over the OS CSPRNG and returns `private_base64`/`public_base64` — the raw 32-byte scalar and point in standard base64, which IS the WireGuard key format (the 44-char string `wg genkey`/`wg pubkey` print) — and its manifest description names WireGuard outright as the use case. (3) "derives the public key from a pasted private key" is that same pair, and also blocks/x25519-ecdh, which accepts a private key as hex/base64/base64url/PKCS#8 PEM and returns "the public key derived from that private key" alongside the agreement. (4) "an optional QR code for mobile import" is blocks/qr-code-generator `content = "text"`, which encodes any data string as SVG or PNG with selectable error correction, size and colours — a wg0.conf is exactly the plain-text payload the WireGuard mobile apps scan. So a new block would add no validation, no config field, no key format and no output the union of these four already produces; it would only route between them. It is also the worst discovery collision in the backlog so far: the slug differs from the existing block by one word, both pages render the same wg0.conf artifact, and blocks/wireguard-config-builder/page/meta.toml already OWNS the query — its tags are ["wireguard config", "wg0.conf", "wireguard generator", "allowedips", "vpn config", "wireguard peer"], i.e. the literal phrase "wireguard generator". Same re-bundle-of-verbs-each-already-a-block class as the skiplisted ecc-keygen and image-filter, and same strict-subset class as eth-wallet (dup of crypto-keypair-generator's ethereum branch). Scaffold not built. Revisit only as an ENHANCEMENT to blocks/wireguard-config-builder — an opt-in "generate the interface keypair when private_key is blank" branch and a QR output format — not as a second WireGuard config page.

pdf-extract-attachments  # not viable in current gizza single-result model: extracting embedded PDF file attachments needs binary multi-file output/downloads (often a zip or one file per attachment), while the current CLI/page block contract exposes one text/media result; existing PDF blocks inspect/extract text/forms/metadata but do not provide attachment download surfaces. Scaffold not built.

rss-feed-summarizer  # out-of-model on its differentiator, duplicate of blocks/podcast-feed-parser without it. Verified live 2026-08-13. (1) The row's headline feature -- "optional AI summaries per item" -- is exactly the transformers-js on-device summarization the CSV's own loader_feasibility names ("plain Rust->WASM for XML parsing plus transformers-js for on-device summarization"), i.e. the ML-model class SKILL.md step 1 defers by default (gizza is pure-Rust + ffmpeg); same class as the ocr-to-hocr / screenshot-link-extractor skips. (2) The deterministic residue -- "parses pasted RSS/Atom XML and produces a clean digest" -- is already blocks/podcast-feed-parser, which despite its podcast name is a GENERIC RSS 2.0 / Atom 1.0 item parser: build_episode() never requires an audio enclosure and every podcast-only field (audio_url/audio_type/audio_length_bytes, duration, season, episode, explicit) is an Option omitted when absent. Confirmed by running the shipped block on a plain news RSS feed with no enclosures and no iTunes namespace (`gizza tool podcast-feed-parser --json '{"feed":"<rss>...","include_descriptions":true}'`): it returned the channel block (title/description/link/language) plus an item list carrying title, `published` normalised to RFC 3339 ("2026-08-11T09:00:00+00:00") with `published_raw` kept, guid, link and the plain-text description -- i.e. the whole "clean digest of pasted RSS/Atom XML" deliverable, plus `limit` and `order` = feed|newest|oldest to trim and sort it. (3) The only remaining sliver is deduplication, which is both a one-flag addition to that existing parse and already shipped generically: blocks/fuzzy-dedupe (near-duplicate titles), blocks/list-dedupe-merge and blocks/jsonl-deduplicator (exact dedupe by key over the parsed item list). Deterministic per-item summaries -- the non-AI version of the row's other half -- are blocks/textrank-summarize (extractive TextRank, no model), and HTML-tag stripping of item bodies is blocks/html-to-text. So a new block would re-emit podcast-feed-parser's item list verbatim and bolt one dedup pass onto it, splitting one function across two RSS/Atom pages and giving chat/CLI two near-identical feed parsers to disambiguate -- the same re-emit-plus-one-check shape as the skiplisted markdown-link-extractor, and the same param-shortcut shape as crop-image. Scaffold not built. Revisit only as an ENHANCEMENT to blocks/podcast-feed-parser -- a `dedupe` flag (guid, else link, else normalised title), item-level author/categories (currently only channel-level author is read), and an option to strip HTML tags from descriptions rather than only collapsing whitespace -- not as a second feed-parser page.
progressive-jpeg-converter  # not viable as specified in the current gizza model: the row's defining promise is a LOSSLESS scan-order transcode ("Re-encodes a baseline JPEG as progressive (or vice-versa) without changing pixels' quality" = jpegtran -progressive), i.e. a DCT-COEFFICIENT-domain operation -- Huffman-decode the entropy stream to quantized coefficients and re-emit them under a progressive (SOF2) scan script, never touching pixels. Verified 2026-08-13. (1) ffmpeg -- the row's type_hint, and the ONLY engine with a browser/page surface for image transcode -- CANNOT ENCODE PROGRESSIVE JPEG AT ALL: `ffmpeg -h encoder=mjpeg` (7.1.4) exposes no progressive/scan/interlace option anywhere in its full option set (mpv_flags, luma/chroma_elim_threshold, quantizer_noise_shaping, error_rate, qsquish, rc_*, border_mask, lmin/lmax, skip_*, sc_threshold, noise_reduction, ps, huffman, force_duplicated_matrix -- `huffman` only picks default-vs-optimal TABLES, not scan structure), the only other JPEG-family encoders in the build are jpeg2000/libopenjpeg/jpegls/ljpeg/libjxl (different codecs entirely, none of which write a baseline-or-progressive JFIF), and a real run confirmed the output marker is SOF0 (FF C0 = baseline). The page/chat surface runs @ffmpeg/core@0.12.10, the same libavcodec mjpegenc, so NEITHER ffmpeg surface can emit SOF2. (2) The row's own build note ("mozjpeg/jpeg-encoder in WASM re-scans coefficients locally -- via jpeg-encoder crate"; found crate:jpeg-encoder) is factually wrong about that crate: jpeg-encoder 0.7.1 is a PIXEL-domain encoder -- its only entry points are `Encoder::encode(pixels, width, height, ColorType)` / `encode_image(ImageBuffer)`, with no coefficient input. `set_progressive(true)` DOES work (spiked here: encoding a 64x64 RGB buffer emitted marker FF C2 = SOF2 progressive), and the crate is already proven wasm-safe in this repo (blocks/svg-to-png/core depends on it) -- but feeding it requires DECODING the JPEG to pixels first, so the tool would re-compress and lose quality on every run, precisely what "without changing pixels' quality" forbids. Shipping that is the same lie-about-losslessness failure the skiplisted jpeg-lossless-rotate rejected ("Building this via `image` would ship a tool that LIES about being lossless"). (3) No wasm-safe pure-Rust coefficient path exists -- the jpeg-lossless-rotate (2026-07-22) scan re-confirmed and extended here: mozjpeg / mozjpeg-sys are C FFI bindings to libjpeg-turbo (won't instantiate under wafer's wasmi, same class as pdfium-render); lepton_jpeg 0.5 exposes only whole-file encode_lepton/decode_lepton and is built on a mandatory thread pool (no std::thread under the minimal WASI host, no wasm-threads in the browser build); jpeg-rusturbo 0.9.2 and rusty_jpeg 0.3.2 both ADVERTISE progressive SOF2 encoding but expose pixel-only APIs (`decode(src, PixelFormat) -> Vec<u8>`; `encode_rgb`/`encode_rgba`/`encode_grayscale`/`encode_cmyk` -- no coefficient read/write surface to hang a scan-order transcode on) and default to SIMD; dct-io 0.1.1 is the one crate with a real `read_coefficients`/`write_coefficients` API, but it is BASELINE-ONLY in both directions -- it can neither read a progressive input nor write a progressive output, so it cannot express either direction of this conversion. (4) Even a deliberately-lossy pixel-domain build could not be verified on the required surfaces: binary image INPUT on a page exists ONLY for `runtime = "ffmpeg"` (110 blocks) or `runtime = "model"` (1); there is no generic pure-wasm file-input path (tools/generator/src/meta.rs documents `"file" = a file picker, for ffmpeg/model tools`, and the default tool.js wires file inputs only for those runtimes), which is why EVERY pure-Rust image-bytes block here ships chat+CLI with NO page (gif-from-images, image-to-ico, image-to-pixel-art, image-trim). A pure jpeg-encoder block would therefore be CLI+chat only and fail the loop's page/query-param + Playwright verification gate -- same architecture-fit class as video-embed-cover-art. (5) Stripped of the losslessness that makes it distinct, the residue (decode a JPEG, re-encode it at a chosen quality) is already covered by blocks/image-compress (JPEG `-q:v` at a chosen quality, same format out) and blocks/image-convert (format=jpeg + quality); the ONLY delta would be the scan-order flag, which neither can express and neither engine behind them supports. Revisit only if a wasm-safe, single-threaded pure-Rust crate exposes JPEG DCT coefficients WITH a progressive (SOF2) scan writer (dct-io growing progressive read+write would be sufficient), or if the page runtime gains a generic pure-wasm binary-file input AND the backlog row is retitled to an explicitly LOSSY re-encode. The sibling backlog row progressive-jpeg-checker (report baseline-vs-progressive by reading the SOF marker) is unaffected by this skip and remains buildable as a pure text-output tool. Scaffold not built.

mono-to-stereo-duplicator  # duplicate of blocks/audio-channel (its `stereo` and `left` operations, with format=wav, ARE this tool). Verified 2026-08-13 against source + real ffmpeg runs. The row's deliverable is "duplicates a mono WAV into a two-channel stereo file with identical left and right" (dual mono). blocks/audio-channel is the shipped channel router for exactly this family: core/src/lib.rs `plan_channels(in, operation, format)` emits `-i in -vn <op args> <codec args> out.<ext>` for operation = swap|mono|stereo|left|right and format = mp3|wav|ogg|flac|m4a, and `Format::Wav => -c:a pcm_s16le` is the WAV-in/WAV-out path the row asks for. Two of its five operations already produce the requested artifact, and its own unit test `mono_uses_ac_1_and_stereo_uses_ac_2` pins the mapping. (1) `operation=stereo` is `-ac 2`, ffmpeg's mono->stereo up-mix. Ran it on a locally generated 1 s 44.1 kHz mono pcm_s16le fixture: ffprobe reports channels=2, and splitting the result with `channelsplit=channel_layout=stereo` then byte-comparing the two raw s16le channel dumps gives a bit-for-bit `cmp` match (88200 bytes each, identical) -- i.e. left and right ARE identical, the row's stated requirement. Note this path carries ffmpeg's default rematrix normalisation, so each channel sits at 0.707x the mono sample values (measured: 181 vs 256 on the first non-zero sample, exactly -3 dB) -- total power preserved, per-channel level down 3 dB. (2) `operation=left` is `pan=stereo|c0=c0|c1=c0`, which on a MONO source (c0 is the only channel) copies that single channel onto both sides at UNITY gain: same run gives channels=2, left == right bit-exact, AND both channels bit-exact against the original mono samples -- literally the row's "interleaves each sample twice and updates the channel-count header", produced by the shipped block with no new code. So the exact behaviour the row specifies is one existing dropdown selection away (audio-channel operation=left format=wav), and the -3 dB-normalised variant is another (operation=stereo). (3) All three surfaces are already live for it: the descriptor takes `Input::Audio` with `Param::enumv("operation", [swap, mono, stereo, left, right])` and `Param::enumv("format", [mp3, wav, ogg, flac, m4a])`, and blocks/audio-channel/page/meta.toml ships `runtime = "ffmpeg"`, `format = "audio"`, `accept = "audio/*"` plus preset examples including a wav one -- so the page/query-param + Playwright gate is satisfied by the existing block. A new block would re-emit one of audio-channel's existing argv branches under a second page, giving chat/CLI two overlapping channel tools to disambiguate for a strict subset of one -- the same strict-subset class as the skiplisted audio-gain-adjust (dup of audio-volume-adjust) and jpg-to-png (dup of image-convert). (4) The sibling backlog rows video-audio-mono-to-stereo and video-audio-to-mono are NOT covered by this skip: those take a VIDEO input and must remux the video stream, which audio-channel's `-vn` explicitly drops. Scaffold not built. Revisit only as an ENHANCEMENT to blocks/audio-channel -- e.g. document the -3 dB rematrix on `stereo` and/or add an explicit unity-gain "dual-mono / duplicate" operation label so users searching "mono to stereo" land on the right choice -- not as a second channel-routing page.
query-string-builder  # duplicate of blocks/query-string-codec (direction=build serializes a JSON object into an application/x-www-form-urlencoded query string and direction=parse reverses it; already handles nested objects/arrays via array_style=brackets/indices/repeat/comma, space_as_plus for %20-vs-+, sort_keys, and prefix_question_mark. The parse half is also covered by blocks/parse-query-string and component encoding by blocks/url-encode. Same scope, no new capability.)

markdown-code-extractor  # duplicate of blocks/markdown-query (extract=code_blocks already collects every fenced/indented code block from pasted Markdown via pulldown-cmark, exposing each block's language and code as text, JSON `{count, code_blocks:[{language, code}]}`, or reconstructed ```lang fences, with optional 1-based source line numbers). xml-numeric-value-extractor  # duplicate of blocks/xpath-query + blocks/xml-to-csv (numeric filtering already blocks/extract-numbers-from-text). Verified live 2026-08-13 by RUNNING the installed CLI on a product feed (<catalog> of <product sku qty><name/><price currency>...), not by reading descriptors. The picked row -- "Extracts numeric element and attribute values from XML by tag/attribute path into a numeric column", use case "Pull all <price> values out of this XML product feed as a list" -- is covered clause for clause by shipped blocks. (a) "by tag ... path": `gizza tool xpath-query expression=//price xml=<feed>` returns {"count":3,"outputs":["19.99","4.50","N/A"]} -- literally the row's own use-case sentence, as a list, in document order. (b) "and attribute ... path": `expression=//product/@qty` returns {"count":3,"outputs":["3","10","0"]}; xpath-query's descriptor already advertises attribute paths (//a/@href) as a first-class form. (c) "numeric": stock XPath 1.0 predicate does it in the same call -- `expression=//price[number(.)=number(.)]` returns {"count":2,"outputs":["19.99","4.50"]}, dropping the non-numeric N/A cell; and for messy values XPath's number() cannot coerce, blocks/extract-numbers-from-text already takes the extracted blob and returns {"numbers":["19.99","4.50"],"output":"19.99\n4.50","count":2,"sum":24.49,"min":4.5,"max":19.99,"average":12.245} -- i.e. the cleaned numeric series AND its stats. (d) "into a numeric column": blocks/xml-to-csv IS the column form -- `gizza tool xml-to-csv xml=<feed> record=product` emits `@sku,@qty,name,price@currency,price` / `A1,3,Widget,USD,19.99` / ..., one named column per element AND attribute path (attributes as '@attr' / 'path@attr', nested elements as dot-notation), so every numeric field is already a column addressable by path; blocks/csv-filter and blocks/csv-stats take it from there. So the row adds no engine (same sxd-xpath / quick-xml walk over the same single pasted-XML input), no input surface, and no output the toolkit lacks -- only a numeric predicate that is one XPath token, plus a column/list output framing. That output-framing delta is exactly why xpath-extractor is already skiplisted above ("CSV is a one-param format=csv enhancement that belongs on xpath-query"), and this row is that same class with a numeric filter bolted on -- same strict-subset shape as the skiplisted histogram-svg and markdown-link-extractor. Building it would give chat/CLI a third XML-value-selection page to disambiguate against xpath-query and xml-to-csv. Distinct from -- and not covered by -- blocks/xml-to-json (whole-document transcode, no path selection) and blocks/numeric-string-sanitizer (cleans an already-extracted numeric column, no XML). Scaffold not built. Revisit only as an ENHANCEMENT to xpath-query -- a `format=csv|list` output knob and an optional `numeric_only` filter -- not as a second XML path-extraction page.

thousands-separator-normalizer  # duplicate of blocks/numeric-string-sanitizer (decimal_separator=auto, its default). Verified live 2026-08-13 by RUNNING the installed CLI, not by reading the descriptor. The picked row -- "Detects US vs European grouping/decimal styles (1,234.56 vs 1.234,56) and normalizes to canonical floats" -- is that block's shipped default path, clause for clause. DETECTS: DecimalSep::Auto is the default and runs infer_decimal() (core/src/lib.rs:234), documented as "Column-level inference: let the values that are unambiguous decide the convention for the ambiguous ones, so a single `1.234` in a European column is not read as 1.234" -- it votes per value on dot-vs-comma using group-run length (a 3-digit tail after a mark = grouping, not decimal), multi-mark counts, and, when BOTH marks appear, whichever mark comes last; the block's own test is literally named european_decimal_is_inferred_per_column, with explicit_decimal_separator_overrides_inference as the manual escape hatch (decimal_separator = auto|dot|comma, Param::enumv). NORMALIZES TO CANONICAL FLOATS: `gizza tool numeric-string-sanitizer input='1,234.56\n12,345.67\n1 234.5' output=table` returns 1234.56 / 12345.67 / 1234.5 with "Decimal separator: ." reported, and the European column '1.234,56\n9.876.543,21\n0,5' returns 1234.56 / 9876543.21 / 0.5 with "Decimal separator: ," -- i.e. it both REPORTS the detected US-vs-European style (a first-class `decimal_separator` field in the json output and in the stats summary) and emits the canonical floats. It also already handles the row's whole input class beyond the two named styles: space/NBSP/apostrophe grouping (1 234,56), currency symbols and unit suffixes, percent (strip|divide), accounting parentheses, trailing minus, and K/M/B/T magnitude suffixes, over the same single pasted one-value-per-line column, with on_error = blank|keep|marker|fail and output = values|table|json. Same input surface, same pure-Rust string-rewriting engine (no locale library either side), same output. The ONE behavioural delta is detection SCOPE, and it is a scope knob on that same function, not a new tool: inference is deliberately per-COLUMN, so a list that MIXES conventions resolves to a single convention and one value loses -- the row's own example prompt ('1.234,56', '12,345.67', '1 234,5') returns 1234.56 / 12.34567 / 1234.5, the middle value misread because the column vote went to comma. Fixing that means skipping the vote and using infer_decimal's already-existing PER-VALUE branch directly (for '12,345.67' that branch alone answers "last mark is the dot" correctly), i.e. a `detect_scope = column|value` parameter on numeric-string-sanitizer -- and per-value detection is only ever a heuristic, since a bare '1,234' is unresolvable without column context, which is why column scope is the documented default. That is the same one-param-enhancement class already ruled on for numeric-column-coercer (the dup of this same block, from this same gizza-search-tools-canonical source list), dotenv-normalizer, env-file-sort and note-action-extractor -- not a second block re-implementing the strip/split/disambiguate/render engine and adding a third numeric-cleanup page for chat/CLI to disambiguate against. Distinct from -- and not covered by -- blocks/extract-numbers-from-text (pulls numbers out of free prose), blocks/round-decimals (rounds already-numeric values), blocks/number-to-currency-formatter (reverse direction: float -> formatted string), blocks/numeric-range-check (bounds checking) and blocks/csv-type-inferrer (column type guessing, no rewrite). Scaffold not built. Revisit only as a `detect_scope = column|value` (per-value disambiguation, with an ambiguous-cell status) enhancement on numeric-string-sanitizer, not as a separate page.

wif-key-decode  # duplicate of blocks/bitcoin-address (its WIF input path IS the decode direction and its `network` + `compressed` params ARE the encode direction). Verified live 2026-08-13 by RUNNING the installed CLI on the picked row's own example key, not by reading the descriptor. The row asks for five things and blocks/bitcoin-address ships every one from a single `key` field that auto-detects hex-vs-WIF (core/src/lib.rs `parse_key`). (1) "Base58Check WIF decode to 32-byte private key hex": `gizza tool bitcoin-address key=L1aW4aubDFB7yfras2S1mN3bqg9nwySY8nkoLmJebSLD5BWv3ENZ` -- literally the CSV row's own prompt string -- returns `key_source: wif` / `private_key_hex: 820b967776cab711e0e05a8d04385e71d4333b2be73f39edfd73d2d3ea9e1312`, i.e. the raw 32 bytes, decoded via `bs58::decode(..).with_check(None)`. (2) "detect mainnet/testnet version byte": `Network::from_wif_version` maps 0x80 -> mainnet and 0xef -> testnet and is reported as a first-class `network` field; runs confirm `KwDiBf89QgGbjEhKnhXJuH7LrciVrZi3qYjgd9M7rFU73sVHnoWn` -> mainnet and `cMahea7zqjxrtgAbB7LSGbcQUr1uX1ojuat9jZodMN87JcbXMTcA` -> testnet, and an unrecognised byte errors with "unknown WIF version byte 0x.. (expected 0x80 mainnet or 0xef testnet)". (3) "detect compressed flag": reported as a first-class `compressed` field off the payload length -- 33 bytes -> false, 34 bytes with a trailing 0x01 -> true; confirmed `5HpHagT65TZzG1PH3CSu63k8DbpvD8s5ip4nEB3kEsreAnchuDf` -> compressed: false and the K.../c... keys -> compressed: true, and its own unit tests `wif_input_roundtrips_network_and_compression` / `wif_uncompressed_input` pin both. (4) "validate checksum/version/length/range": all four are already enforced with distinct messages -- checksum via with_check (`gizza tool bitcoin-address key=KwDiBf89...VHnoWX` -> "key is neither valid hex nor a valid WIF (base58check failed)"), version per (2), length ("WIF payload has unexpected length {n} (expected 33 or 34 bytes)" plus "invalid WIF compression flag 0x{..} (expected 0x01)"), and RANGE via `k256::SecretKey::from_slice` -> "private key is not a valid secp256k1 scalar (must be non-zero and below the curve order)", confirmed by real runs on the all-zero key and on n = FFFF...364141. Hex input is length-checked too ("hex private key must be 32 bytes (64 hex chars), got N chars"). (5) "encode raw 32-byte key hex to WIF with network/compressed options": that is the block's hex branch verbatim -- `Param::enumv("network", [mainnet, testnet])` + `Param::boolean("compressed")` feeding `fn wif(network, secret, compressed)` (version byte + 32-byte key + optional 0x01, base58check). Confirmed against the three canonical vectors for privkey=1: default -> KwDiBf89QgGbjEhKnhXJuH7LrciVrZi3qYjgd9M7rFU73sVHnoWn, `compressed=false` -> 5HpHagT65TZzG1PH3CSu63k8DbpvD8s5ip4nEB3kEsreAnchuDf, `network=testnet` -> cMahea7zqjxrtgAbB7LSGbcQUr1uX1ojuat9jZodMN87JcbXMTcA. So a new block would add no engine (same bs58 with_check over the same single pasted-key input), no format, no validation and no field -- only a narrower output framing that drops the addresses/pubkey lines, the same strict-subset class as the skiplisted jpg-to-png (dup of image-convert), audio-gain-adjust and eth-wallet, and the same output-framing-only class as xpath-extractor. It is also a discovery collision: blocks/bitcoin-address/page/meta.toml already OWNS the query with tags ["bitcoin address", "bitcoin wif", ..., "base58check", "secp256k1"], its hero copy is "Paste a secp256k1 private key as hex or WIF", and it ships an "Uncompressed WIF" example chip. Two further shipped blocks overlap the same ground: blocks/secp256k1-pubkey-derive also accepts "64-char hex ... or a WIF" for the same key input, and blocks/base58-codec is the generic Base58 (bitcoin/ripple/flickr alphabets) encode/decode engine underneath. The row's stated privacy constraint ("never sends the private key anywhere") is already satisfied -- every one of these blocks runs the key handling entirely locally in wasm. Scaffold not built. Revisit only as an ENHANCEMENT to blocks/bitcoin-address -- e.g. a `format`/`fields` knob to return just the decoded private_key_hex or just the WIF, mirroring secp256k1-pubkey-derive's `format` param -- not as a second WIF/private-key page.
tar-create  # duplicate of blocks/tar-archive (same: bundle multiple named files into one .tar; tar-archive default compression=none is exactly an uncompressed .tar preserving entry names, with .tar.gz/.tar.bz2/.tar.xz as extra options)
file-finder  # not viable in current gizza surfaces: the row requires searching a dragged-in folder's file names/paths with glob/regex, but descriptors support single binary inputs (image/video/audio/document/file), text params, maps, and source lists—not a browser folder/directory tree with filenames/paths. A text-list matcher would not satisfy the dragged-folder tool and would be unverifiable against the intended input model.
text-token-extractor  # duplicate of blocks/ioc-extract (plus blocks/bulk-artifact-extractor). Verified 2026-08-14 against blocks/ioc-extract/{src/lib.rs,core/src/lib.rs}: the picked row ("Scans a blob of text and pulls out all URLs, emails, IP addresses, file paths, and hashes") is ioc-extract's exact job — its extract(text, types, defang) tokenizes an arbitrary blob and pulls URLs (scan_url, http/https/ftp), email addresses (scan_email/is_email), IPv4 (scan_ipv4/is_ipv4) and IPv6 (scan_ipv6/is_ipv6), plus MD5/SHA-1/SHA-256/SHA-512 hashes recognised by hex length (scan_hash(text, 32|40|64|128)) — i.e. every requested category except file paths — then de-duplicates (BTreeSet), sorts, and groups the hits by type, with a 'types' filter ('ipv4,url,sha256', 'hash' for all hash types, 'all' by default) covering the requested filter control and a 'defang' toggle on top. blocks/bulk-artifact-extractor covers the same ground a second way (email/url/ipv4/domain/phone/bitcoin/credit_card with byte offsets + context snippets, kinds filter, table|json output, overlap resolution by specificity), and blocks/extract-urls, blocks/extract-email-addresses, blocks/extract-ip-addresses and blocks/extract-hashes ship the single-category versions. The only genuine delta is file-path matching (absolute/relative Unix paths, Windows paths), which is one more category in ioc-extract's CATEGORIES table and scan() dispatch — an enhancement to that block, not a distinct tool; shipping a fifth text-indicator extractor would fragment the discovery surface with no new capability.
word-diff  # duplicate of blocks/diff-code. Verified live 2026-08-14 by RUNNING the installed CLI, not by reading the descriptor. The picked row ("Computes an inline word- and character-level diff between two texts, highlighting exactly which words changed inside each line rather than marking whole lines") is diff-code's stated job verbatim — its own summary is "Compare two code snippets side by side with word-level highlighting" and its descriptor description says "two code or text snippets", so the code/text framing is not a distinction. (1) Inline word-level stream: `gizza tool diff-code left='the quick brown fox\nsecond line' right='the quick red fox\nsecond line' view=word-diff` returns ` 1  the quick [-brown-]{+red+} fox` — the git word-diff convention, one merged stream, only the changed WORD marked while the unchanged line 2 passes through untouched. (2) Character-level: `granularity=char` on left='colour scheme' right='color scheme' returns ` 1  colo[-u-]r scheme`, i.e. the single changed CHARACTER, and the block also exposes `granularity=none` to fall back to whole-line marking — so the row's whole "word AND character level, not whole lines" axis is already a first-class enum (`Param::enumv("granularity", ["word", "char", "none"])`). (3) The intra-line refinement is real, not just a renderer: core/src/lib.rs has `word_tokens` (alphanumeric/underscore runs vs single punctuation), `char_tokens`, and a refine step that LCS-diffs the token vectors of each CHANGED line pair, gated by `Granularity`, with unit tests `side_by_side_marks_word_level_change`, `word_diff_merges_both_sides_into_one_stream`, `char_granularity_is_finer_than_word`, `granularity_none_marks_whole_lines`, and `long_lines_skip_intra_line_refinement`. The marks surface in every view, not only `view=word-diff` — `view=side-by-side` on the same input renders `[-colour-]` / `{+color+}` inside the aligned columns. (4) Everything a word-diff tool would add on top is already there: `ignore_case`, `ignore_whitespace`, `context`, `line_numbers`, `width`, and `view=unified|stats|json` (the json view emits per-row intra-line spans). blocks/text-diff is the deliberate LINE-level counterpart the row contrasts itself against (no granularity param at all), so the pair is already split the way the row wants — a third block would sit exactly on top of diff-code. It is also a discovery collision: blocks/diff-code/page/meta.toml already OWNS the query with tags ["code diff", "compare code", "side by side diff", "word diff", "unified diff", "text compare", "developer"], a hero subtitle promising "only the changed words marked", a `[input.labels]` entry "Word diff (one merged stream)", and an example chip that presets `view = "word-diff"`. Scaffold not built. Revisit only as an ENHANCEMENT to blocks/diff-code — e.g. HTML/ANSI-colourised output or a prose-oriented sentence/token mode — not as a second word-level diff page.
diff-stat  # duplicate of blocks/diff-viewer (view=stats). Verified live 2026-08-14 by RUNNING the installed CLI, not by reading the descriptor. The picked row ("Parses a unified diff or git patch and produces a diffstat summary: per-file lines added and removed, total churn, and the most-changed files") is diff-viewer's stats view verbatim -- its own core doc comment says it renders "a `git --stat`-style `stats` summary" and its descriptor already ships `Param::enumv("view", ["inline", "side-by-side", "stats", "json"])` over the same single pasted `diff` field. (1) Same input: diff-viewer accepts "the output of `git diff`, `diff -u`, or a .patch file" and parses multi-file patches with rename and binary markers; running it on a two-file git patch returns, byte for byte, ` src/main.rs |    3 ++-` / ` README.md   |    2 +-` / ` 2 files changed, 3 insertions(+), 2 deletions(-)` -- i.e. the per-file added/removed bar graph AND the total-churn summary line the row asks for, in real `git diff --stat` format (core/src/lib.rs `render_stats` + `summary_line`, scaled to a 40-column graph with `Bin` for binary files and `old -> new` naming for renames, pinned by the unit test `stats_view_bar_and_totals`). (2) Machine-readable per-file counts are already first class: `view=json` on the same patch returns `{files_changed: 2, additions: 3, deletions: 2}` plus one object per file carrying `path`/`old_path`/`new_path`/`change_type`/`additions`/`deletions`, so "per-file lines added and removed" and "total churn" are both exposed as numbers, not just as rendered text. (3) The only genuine delta in the row is ORDERING -- "the most-changed files" is diff-viewer's existing per-file additions+deletions figure sorted descending, which is a sort key on an already-computed field, not a new engine, new input format, new validation or new output kind. That is the same strict-subset/output-framing-only class as the already-skiplisted jpg-to-png (dup of image-convert), xpath-extractor and wif-key-decode. (4) It is also a discovery collision: blocks/diff-viewer/page/meta.toml already OWNS the query with tags ["diff viewer", "unified diff viewer", "patch viewer", "git diff viewer", "side by side diff", "diff stats", "patch to json"], a hero subtitle promising "a git-style stat summary", and an example chip labelled "Stats ignoring whitespace" that presets `view = "stats"`. Two further shipped blocks cover adjacent ground: blocks/diff-code also offers a `stats` view (for two pasted snippets rather than a pasted patch) and blocks/diff-hunk-selector already inventories a unified diff's hunks with per-hunk counts. Scaffold not built. Revisit only as an ENHANCEMENT to blocks/diff-viewer -- e.g. a `sort` knob (`path` | `churn`) and/or a `top_n` cap on the stats view -- not as a second diffstat page.
extract-matches  # duplicate of blocks/regex-extract (plus the four preset-category blocks). Verified live 2026-08-14 by RUNNING the installed CLI, not by reading the descriptor. The picked row ("Pull every regex match (or all emails/URLs/IPs/numbers via presets) out of text into a clean deduplicated list") is regex-extract's job verbatim -- its own summary is "Return every match of a regular expression in text", its page title is literally "Regex Extract - Pull Every Match from Text", and every requested knob is already a shipped param: (1) regex over pasted text -> `pattern` + `text`; (2) "clean deduplicated list" -> `unique` (first-seen order), confirmed by `gizza tool regex-extract text='Mail bob@x.com and ann@y.org or bob@x.com; see https://a.io and 10.0.0.5' pattern='[\w.+-]+@[\w-]+\.[\w.]+' unique=true` -> {"count":2,"matches":["bob@x.com","ann@y.org"]}; (3) capture-group selection -> `capture_group` (0 = whole match, out-of-range errors with the pattern's real group count), confirmed `pattern='key=(\w+)' capture_group=1` -> ["alpha","beta"]; (4) case sensitivity -> `ignore_case`, confirmed 'Cat cat CAT' -> 3 matches; (5) multiline -> `multiline` (^/$ per line), confirmed `pattern='^\w' multiline=true` on a 3-line input -> ["a","b","c"], and it ships `dotall` on top, which the row does not even ask for. The preset half is covered twice over. Presets are one-line patterns against the SAME engine -- live runs returned the email list above, `pattern='https?://[^\s]+'` -> ["https://a.io/x?q=1","http://b.net"], `pattern='\b\d{1,3}(\.\d{1,3}){3}\b' unique=true` -> ["10.0.0.5","192.168.1.1"], and `pattern='[-+]?\d+(\.\d+)?(e\d+)?' unique=true` -> ["3","4.5","-7","6.022e23"] -- and each category also has a dedicated, better-validated block already: blocks/extract-email-addresses, blocks/extract-urls (validated + prose-punctuation trimmed), blocks/extract-ip-addresses (octet-checked IPv4 + compressed/bracketed IPv6), blocks/extract-numbers-from-text (whose summary is, word for word, the row's own "Pull every number out of text into a clean list"), with blocks/ioc-extract and blocks/bulk-artifact-extractor covering the combined multi-category scan behind a `types` filter. The only genuine deltas are a preset ENUM naming those four patterns and `sort`/output-format knobs -- and blocks/extract-numbers-from-text already ships exactly that shape (`sort` original|ascending|descending + `delimiter` newline|comma|space|tab|semicolon + `unique`), so both are param additions to regex-extract, not a new engine, input, validation or output kind. Same strict-subset/format-option class as the already-skiplisted regex-extractor (dup of this same block), xlsx-sheet-extractor and audio-format-converter. It is also a discovery collision: blocks/regex-extract/page/meta.toml already OWNS the query with tags ["regex extract", "regex tester", "regex match", "extract matches", "regular expression", "capture group", "find all matches"] -- the row's own slug is a verbatim tag -- and its hero copy is "pull out every match ... or deduplicate the results". Scaffold not built. Revisit only as an ENHANCEMENT to blocks/regex-extract -- a `preset` enum (email|url|ipv4|number) that prefills `pattern`, plus `sort` and a `delimiter`/output-format knob mirroring extract-numbers-from-text -- not as a second regex-match page.
